## R15.4 — The instrument-lie class, now four members and a stated predicate

QA verified R14.1.1 and found it **worse than reported**: `git rev-parse <tree>:<absent-path>` not only
echoes its argument to stdout, **it exits 0** — so an `|| echo MISSING` guard **never fires**. The
guard silently does nothing. QA had used that exact form earlier in the session, safe only because all
three paths happened to exist.

**Four members in this chain, and the predicate is worth more than the list:**

| # | Instrument | Lie |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | piped `grep -c` over filtered output | undercounts (46 against a true 51) |
| 2 | `ls -a \| grep -x <name>` | false absence, non-deterministic across identical runs |
| 3 | `git diff --name-only \| grep -c .` | 8 against a true 7 — **observed today** |
| 4 | `git rev-parse <tree>:<path>` on an absent path | echoes the argument, **exits 0**; existence guards never fire |

> **Predicate**: *an existence or count predicate that cannot fail loudly.* Each member returns a
> plausible value where it should have refused. **Remedy in every case: an instrument that computes
> before the filter and fails loudly** — `grep -c`/`grep -rc`, `git diff --numstat`, `ls -d <path>`,
> `git cat-file -e <tree>:<path>`.

**Stated on its own terms so it survives these four instances**, per the document-lifecycle rule that
a rule and the sighting that motivated it separate. Carried to the retrospective, where it belongs with
the rev-13 note that this chain's most reliable defence was re-derivation rather than review.

---

- R13.5 states the delta as **`abcf585..4a7126b`** throughout.

**Why it never entered.** At rev 13 the delta was **derived from the tree** — `git diff --numstat`
plus per-path blob identity at both tips — rather than restated from QA's `Verified-At-Note`, which is
where F-9 lived. **This is a concrete instance of the predicate carried to the retrospective**, not a
restatement of it: the false form was in a document this stage read, and re-deriving is the reason it
did not propagate. Recorded because evidence for that claim is worth more than the claim.

## R17.2 — BC-19 split: ADOPTED as BC-19a / BC-19b

The split R15.2 recommended is **performed by QA** (`aef3c2e`), whose row it is:

- **BC-19a · `appears-at-all`** — a **real code import that drops components**, not a look at a page.
  Carries the unproven producer and mount. **One successful import refutes *"never appears"* by
  construction.**
- **BC-19b · `legible-and-placed`** — the eye check. **Earliest-decidable depends on 19a**: it cannot
  be observed until 19a produces something to look at.

**The ordering dependency is the part worth having**, and this document did not state it: the unsplit
row read as one observation when it is two, the second conditional on the first. Both remain **`PRE`,
owner, executable now**, and **neither is discharged by the Phase 3 verdict** (release record §5;
`agent-output/qa/release-4a7126b-tip-verification.md`). **BC-18 unchanged.**

## R17.3 — Failure mode (b), *"the page throws"*: RETIRED. Verified, not adopted

R15.2 added **(b) the page throws** to BC-19's falsity signature, on the strength of QA's instrument
comment (*"a null context would throw in production and pass here"*). **QA declines it, and QA is
right: that comment is true about the test and does not establish a live failure mode.**

Re-derived here git-side rather than accepted:

| Claim | Instrument | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `CodeMessageNotice` has one mount site | `git grep -c` at `4a7126b`, `src` | **2 non-test files**: its own definition and `layout.tsx`. **One mount site**, inside `<MessageProvider>` |
| A sibling proves the boundary in production | `git cat-file -e` + `git rev-parse` at three tips | `src/app/message/m/[id]/_layout/Navigation.tsx` = **`339ed840` at `a151fb4`, `abcf585` and `4a7126b`** — matching QA's cited blob |
| …and this release does not touch it | `git diff --name-only a151fb4 4a7126b -- <path>` | **empty — untouched** |
| …and it is literally the sibling slot | `git ls-tree` of the `_layout` directory | that directory holds **exactly two files**: `CodeMessageNotice.tsx` and `Navigation.tsx` |

**So the boundary (b) posited as unproven — async server layout → client component consuming
`useMessage()` inside `<MessageProvider>` — has been running in production continuously**, via a
byte-identical component this release does not touch. The null-context state **cannot arise at the
shipped mount**. **(b) is retired**, and R15.2's statement of it is corrected here rather than left
standing.

> **The reasoning is worth keeping past this row.** An **unreachable** failure is not a reason. Carrying
> (b) would have made a row *already blocking a release* harder to settle while adding nothing an
> observation could discharge — it would have undone the very benefit the split was for. **A widened
> falsity signature is not automatically a better one.** My rev-15 widening was right on (a) and (c)
> and wrong on (b), and the difference is reachability, which I did not test before adding it.

**BC-19's falsity signature therefore stands as: (a) no notice appears at all → BC-19a; (c) the notice
appears but is hidden, overlapped or illegible → BC-19b.**

**Noted, not adopted as a release row**: QA found `useMessage`'s null guard names the **wrong
component** — it throws *"UnsavedWarning components must be wrapped in `<UnsavedWarning />`"*. **Pre-existing,
outside the `a151fb4..4a7126b` delta, so it does not gate `4a7126b`**; routed to the implementer as
diagnosability. Recorded here only because had (b) ever been reachable, the guard would have sent the
investigator to the wrong component.

## R17.4 — The instrument class gains two sightings, from this pass's own verification

Both hit while checking R17.3, both the same predicate, **both caught by a contradiction rather than by
re-reading**:

| # | Instrument | Lie |
|---|---|---|
| 5 | `git cat-file -e <tip>:<guessed-path>` | Returned **ABSENT** for `src/components/Navigation.tsx` — a path that never existed. **Absent-because-wrong-path is indistinguishable from absent-because-not-there.** Had it been reported, it would have contradicted a true claim of QA's |
| 6 | `git rev-parse "$t:$P"` with **`$P` empty** | Returned **three different plausible 8-char hashes** — the *root tree* SHA at each tip. An empty path variable yields a confident, differing, entirely wrong answer |

**Sighting 6 is the sharpest member yet** and is why the predicate is worth stating on its own terms:
it did not fail, it did not return a null, and it did not repeat itself — **it returned three
*differing* values, which is exactly what a real difference looks like.** Nothing in the output
announced that the question asked was not the question intended. **Remedy in both cases: name the path
from the tree (`git ls-tree`), then assert existence before comparing.**

**Six members now, predicate unchanged**: *an existence or count predicate that cannot fail loudly.*

## R19.0 — Why a revision was judged required, stated before it was written

Three reasons, each independent:

1. **Rev 18 §R18.4 is now false and it is load-bearing.** It states BC-19a/BC-19b *"stand exactly as
   written"* on the ground that their three files are *"byte-identical at `e343927`"*. One of those three
   files no longer exists. A reader landing on R18.4 would execute a row against a subject that is gone.
2. **Four rows have reached terminal states** and a register whose rows reach terminal states silently is
   not a register.
3. **The standing release decision names an anchor whose every covered blob has moved.** Its own expiry
   clause has fired and nothing else in this document says so.

Correction form follows the surface: **§R18.4 is a record and is corrected by a dated reconcile note
that preserves its text (R19.9); `Status-Detail` and `Verified-At` are declarative and were replaced.**

## R19.1 — What `2d90e8a` did, established from the tree — and two claims it falsifies

`origin/master` = **`2d90e8a60270740b59b4768a68cf3f67641e4345`**, verified at the remote with
`git ls-remote origin master`, not from a tracking ref. One commit ahead of `e343927`, subject
*"remove useless message"*. `git diff --name-status e343927 2d90e8a` — read from a file, not a filtered
count:

| Path | Change | Blob `4a7126b` → `e343927` → `2d90e8a` |
|---|---|---|
| `src/app/message/m/[id]/_layout/CodeMessageNotice.tsx` | **`D` — deleted** | `75dafce9` → `75dafce9` → **absent** |
| `src/app/message/m/[id]/layout.tsx` | `M` — mount removed | `d50a1992` → `d50a1992` → **`aef786f7`** |
| `src/providers/MessageProvider/index.tsx` | `M` — `codeMessageDroppedNotice` state, effect and context field removed | `955fc0ea` → `955fc0ea` → **`1bab189c`** |
| `src/app/message/_components/NewMessageJsonInput.tsx` | `M` — reworded | `f63b95df` → `664d938e` → **`a30edd0`** |
| `src/app/message/_components/NewMessageModal.tsx` | `M` — reworded | — |
| `src/app/message/m/[id]/_components/JsonModalBody.tsx` | `M` — reworded | — |
| `src/utils/__tests__/013-owner-toast-vessel-2026-08-25.test.ts` | **`M` — trimmed, NOT deleted** | 75 → **48** lines |
| `src/utils/__tests__/013-qa-toast-vessel-render.test.ts` | **`M` — trimmed, NOT deleted** | 132 → **81** lines |

**The mechanism, so the rows below are ruled on the code path and not on a resemblance.** At `e343927`,
`MessageProvider` read `searchParams.get('code')` → `api.message.getFromCode` → set
`codeMessageDroppedNotice` from `codeMessage.droppedComponentTypes` → the sole consumer
`<CodeMessageNotice />`, mounted once in `layout.tsx` inside `<MessageProvider>`. **At `2d90e8a` that
entire chain is gone.** `getFromCode` and `droppedComponentTypes` survive — the drop still *happens*;
only the *telling* was removed. The three reworded surfaces are the **paste/dialog** surfaces
(`Not supported on Hooks.gg: <types>`); none of them is the code ingress.

### R19.1.1 — Correction owed: *"plus two toast-vessel test files"* is false at the tree

**D40 registers, and the brief repeats, that the owner deleted *"two toast-vessel test files"*. He did
not.** Both are `M`, and both survive with content. The error is a misreading of `git diff --numstat`,
which reported `0 51` and `0 27` for them: **`0 <n>` means no lines added, not a deleted file.** Verified
by `git cat-file -e` at both tips, by `git diff --name-status` status letters, and by line counts of the
blobs themselves — 132−51 = 81 and 75−27 = 48, which reconcile exactly.

**The instrument did not lie here; the inference did.** Recorded as such rather than as a seventh member
of the instrument class, because the class's predicate is *an existence or count predicate that cannot
fail loudly* and `--numstat` failed loudly the moment a second instrument was asked. **What caught it was
a contradiction** — `git grep` finding the notice string inside a file believed deleted — which is the
same detector every prior member was caught by.

**What survives, and what it does and does not cover.** The trimmed `013-qa-toast-vessel-render.test.ts`
retains the `NullableComponent` primitive tests and the paste-surface `!error && settledNotice` conjunct
isolation. Its `NOTICE` constant still reads `'Not carried: Thumbnail. …'` — the pre-`2d90e8a` wording,
now present **nowhere in `src` outside that file**. It is used as an opaque sentinel, so **nothing is
red**; but it means **no surviving instrument pins the shipped wording**, and a wording regression on the
three paste surfaces would not be caught. Recorded as an observation, **not raised as a row**.

**This correction is owed to the plan's D40, whose write is steering's.** It is not performed here.

## R19.2 — BC-19a → `EXECUTED` 2026-08-25. TERMINAL.

**The brief asks whether the owner's pre-deletion observation survives the deletion of its subject. It
does, and the evidence is stronger than the brief's framing of it.** This is not an inference from BC-3.
It is the owner naming the row, in D40's own evidence column:

> *"**BC-19a - Done.** And I've also removed the notice and all test code for it. It was unnecessary and
> I don't recall approving it anyway."*

**Corroborated independently, and by construction rather than by resemblance.** D41 records him reaching
the same message through the bot's **Get Message** code path. Per R19.1 that path *is* BC-19a's subject —
`?code=` → `getFromCode` → `droppedComponentTypes` → the notice. **This is not the substitution C.3
refused.** C.3 refused BC-6 standing in for BC-2 because those are genuinely different code paths
(lenient paste filter vs `discordResponseToMessage`). Here there is no substitution to refuse: **BC-3 and
BC-19a are one observation on one code path with one input**, and the identity was established from the
tree, not from the brief's prose.

**Why the deletion does not un-execute it.** An observation is a dated fact about a tree; a verdict is a
forward-looking judgement that a tree is fit. **This document's expiry clause expires verdicts, not
observations.** `2d90e8a` therefore changes what the observation is *evidence for* — it no longer
describes live code — and not whether it happened. **BC-19a is terminal at `EXECUTED` and stays there.**

**The whole row is terminalised, not one limb.** BC-19a as QA wrote it carries an *appears* limb and a
*clears* limb; he attested against the row by number. **This stage's rule is explicit — record his
statement with its timestamp and terminalise on it; never record a capture as missing against a condition
he executed.** Asking after the *clears* limb would be exactly that, and it is not asked.

**One reconcile, because rev 15 said the opposite.** Rev 15 recorded that this surface *"has never
executed in production"*, true when written (`CodeMessageNotice.tsx` was absent at `a151fb4`). **It is
superseded by events**: the component shipped at `abcf585`/`e343927` and the owner observed it render.
**Not overclaimed** — QA's row names *"the owner, local dev server"* as its subject, so what is
established is that it executed **in the environment the row designates**, which is all the row asks.

## R19.3 — BC-19b → `TERMINAL: SUBJECT WITHDRAWN`. A fifth state, named precisely rather than fitted to an existing one.

BC-19b is *legible-and-placed*: **given a notice that appeared under BC-19a**, it is legible and not
obscured. Its precondition is now satisfied (R19.2). Its own predicate is **not** attested — he named
19a and did not name 19b — and its subject was deleted before it could be. **No future observation can
decide it.**

**The brief offers three readings and asks for a fourth if none fits. None fits.**

- **Not *condition met*.** Nothing was observed against its predicate and nothing can be.
- **Not *condition failed*.** Failure means the delivered behaviour is wrong against the requirement. The
  requirement was **removed by its owner**. A row cannot fail against a requirement that no longer exists,
  and reading it as a failure would smuggle in a judgement on his product decision that is not mine.
- **Not *subject deleted* as a bare fact either** — that describes the cause, not the state.

**The state:**

> **`TERMINAL: SUBJECT WITHDRAWN`** — the behaviour this row observes was removed from the product by an
> owner ruling (**D40**, `2d90e8a`). No observation can decide the row and **none is owed**.

**Distinguished from every state that nearly fits, because being nearly right here is the whole danger:**

| Not this | Why not |
|---|---|
| **`WITHDRAWN`** | Withdrawal is the owner's ruling **on the row**, and he made none. He withdrew the **requirement the row gated**; the row died as a consequence. **D40 says so in its own reversal column** — *"nothing here discharges, closes or withdraws either row"* — and routes the disposition here. **This pass withdraws nothing.** |
| **`SUPERSEDED`** | `SUPERSEDED` names **what replaced it**. Nothing did. The three surviving paste/dialog notices are different surfaces on different inputs; offering them here is the substitution **C.3** already refused in writing. |
| **`EXECUTED`** | No observation exists against its predicate. |
| **BC-11b's `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED`** | **The sharpest distinction and the one that matters.** BC-11b is unrunnable **while its requirement still stands**, so it leaves a real, unverified requirement live in production — which is exactly why this document forbids reading it as *closed*. **BC-19b leaves no residual risk at all**: there is no notice left that could be illegible. **The two must not be collapsed.** |

**And it is genuinely closed out, which BC-11b is not.** Recorded plainly so no later reader inherits a
phantom obligation — and equally so no later reader treats BC-11b as having been settled the same way.

**Why this is a state and not a `RECOMMEND WITHDRAWAL`.** `RECOMMEND WITHDRAWAL` routes a **live choice**
to the owner. There is no live choice here: he already ruled, and D40 forbids restoring the element to
satisfy M3-b. Marking it `RECOMMEND WITHDRAWAL` would ask him to rule twice on one thing.

**No new row is created by naming a new state.** The register's row set is unchanged at 21 `BC` rows plus
the `U-C` series.

## R19.4 — BC-2 → `EXECUTED`. BC-3 → `EXECUTED`. 2026-08-25. TERMINAL. Run, never withdrawn.

Their standing **RECOMMEND WITHDRAWAL** (Appendix C.2) is **moot, and moot is not withdrawn**: the rows
were executed, by the only party who could execute them, each through its own method. **This is the
third of the three options C.2 put to him resolving itself by the second** — he supplied the message.

- **BC-2** — a real Discord message carrying an unsupported component type, imported **by URL**.
- **BC-3** — the same message reached through the bot's **`Get Message`** code path.

**Basis**: the owner's attestation as registered at **D41**, which is the terminal form of owner-supplied
evidence under this stage's own rule. **BC-3 is additionally corroborated by D40's `"BC-19a - Done"`**,
which is the same path (R19.2).

**Steering's TTL reasoning: ACCEPTED, and the reason is given rather than the conclusion adopted.**
BC-3's one-hour store-TTL clause is a **precondition of the code resolving at all** — an expired code
does not load a message, so a code that loaded was within its TTL. The clause is not an independent
oracle and no separate timing attestation is owed. **Accepted on that ground, not on steering's say-so.**

### R19.4.1 — One delta between the verbatim and the registration, recorded because it is load-bearing

The verbatim available to me is *"When I said both, I meant both. **I have seen the message** in both
places, through both of their respective methods."* D41's registered sentence is *"observing **the
notice** on both surfaces."* **Those are different claims, and the notice is the rows' entire oracle.**

**I terminalise on the registration**, for three reasons: it was written after a second, deliberately
corrected prompt that established the **input**; BC-3's limb is independently corroborated by D40; and
this stage may not ask the owner to produce further evidence for a condition he executed.

**But the assumption is named so it is falsifiable by the one party holding the prompt text.** If
steering's second prompt did **not** in fact ask about the notice, then D41's sentence overstates it, D41
is owed a reconcile note, and **BC-2 reverts to open**. That is steering's to check against its own
transcript. **Nothing is asked of the owner.**

## R19.5 — The standing release decision: `EXPIRED IN FULL` by its own clause

**The decision**: *"APPROVED FOR RELEASE, conditional on the human click-through of BC-18, BC-19a and
BC-19b"*, anchored at `website` **`4a7126b`** with a four-path production population (R13.5).

**Its expiry clause** — *"Any change to a production-code blob expires the predecessor verdicts and this
decision with them"* — **has now fired on all four paths** (R19.1): one deleted, three moved. Rev 18
recorded the first firing (`NewMessageJsonInput.tsx` at `e343927`) and scoped it to the paste surface.
**It is now total.**

**What `EXPIRED` is and is not.** It is **not** *discharged*, **not** *met*, **not** *withdrawn*, and
**not** a finding that the decision was wrong — it was correct at the tree it named. **It is the expiry
clause working as designed**, and this stage records it rather than waiting for another agent to notice,
exactly as the clause requires.

**The register does not collapse with it.** Per R13.7, conditions reach their own terminal states
individually — which is the entire reason they are written down one by one:

| Condition of the standing decision | State at rev 19 |
|---|---|
| **BC-18 · `paste-notice-renders`** | **`NOT EXECUTED` — non-terminal, OPEN.** Its prediction was marked **STALE** at rev 18 (`NewMessageJsonInput` moved; D31/D32), and `2d90e8a` moved the same surface again. **The sole surviving condition of the three.** |

| **BC-19b · `legible-and-placed`** | **`TERMINAL: SUBJECT WITHDRAWN`** (R19.3) |

**What BC-18 now gates.** Not a release act — three have already run past this document (`a151fb4`,

and its prediction still needs QA's re-derivation before it can be executed without producing a **false
failure** — the exposure rev 18 was written to prevent. **That routing is unchanged and no new item is
raised for it.**

## R19.6 — No release decision is issued at `2d90e8a`, and the reason is stated rather than left as a gap

**This is a recovery state, not a sanctioned ordering.** `2d90e8a` is the owner's own commit, pushed and
live, and there is no UAT document covering it. This stage's rule is *no UAT doc, no release*; the deploy
nonetheless ran, so the live question is the narrower one: **given what production has shown, should this
state stand, and what must still be established?**

**Both available evasions are declined.** It is not treated as a normal ordering, and it is not treated as
a proposal — the system is live and working.

**Production evidence, classified before it is weighed.** `2d90e8a` **removes** a notice and **rewords**
three others. The deleted notice's failure modes are moot — there is nothing left to render invisibly.
The rewording is a string swap inside an unchanged `NullableComponent show={droppedComponentTypes.length > 0}`
wrapper on three surfaces that were already rendering. **The one silent mode that survives is not a
regression but a design consequence, and it is R19.7's subject.**

**Why no approval is issued anyway.** A release decision requires predecessor gates **at the tree it
names**. At `2d90e8a` there are none:

- **No code review and no QA at that tree.** Both are owed under **D37** and, for the D30–D34 round,
  **D39** — which states in its own words that *"rounds after D37 carry their debt under D37 itself"*.
  `2d90e8a` is such a round. **This is a debt that already exists; this pass neither creates nor schedules
  it, and D39 is explicit that its timing is the owner's.**
- **D35's waiver does not reach it.** D35 covers **D30–D34** and, in its own terms, **reaches no fifth
  item**. `2d90e8a` is not within it. **That is not a criticism of D35** — D35 was given for a round that
  shipped lawfully under it.

**Approving `2d90e8a` because it is live is precisely the laundering this stage's rules forbid**, and a
working system is the most persuasive available argument for doing it. So: **the tree at `2d90e8a` is
UNGATED — it carries no release decision, neither granted nor refused.** That is a statement about the
record, not a verdict on the code, and **no rollback is recommended: nothing in the delivered behaviour is
known to be wrong.**

## R19.7 — Value delivery: what D40 costs, stated as an assessment and not raised as a row

The Value Statement's core is that a user is **told** when the editor cannot carry something. At
`2d90e8a`:

- **Paste / dialog surfaces** (`NewMessageJsonInput`, `NewMessageModal`, `JsonModalBody`) — **still tell
  the user**, reworded to `Not supported on Hooks.gg: <types>`. **D9 and D29 are expressly not
  withdrawn.**
- **Bot-code ingress** (`?code=` → `getFromCode`) — **no longer tells the user anything.**
  `droppedComponentTypes` is still computed and still dropped; only the display is gone. On that one path
  the component vanishes without trace.

**That is a silent mode, and it is now the product's intended behaviour rather than a defect** — the
owner's decision, registered at D40, which also forbids any stage restoring the element. **It is recorded
as a value delta for the retrospective's benefit, is not a finding against the implementation, and is not
a condition.** M3-b's text is knowingly divergent and D40 is what makes that deliberate.

**Objective alignment: PARTIAL by owner ruling, not by drift.** The narrowing is his, made with the
mechanism in front of him. Drift is when delivery diverges from the objective **unnoticed**; this is the
opposite.

## R19.8 — The brief's framing, checked as asked

Steering asked whether its framing elsewhere shares the defect of its own near-miss. Three answers:

1. **Yes, once, and it is the brief's central premise.** *"…now that two of its three conditions have no
   subject"* packages BC-19a and BC-19b as one stranded pair. **They are in materially different states,
   and steering's own D40 evidence column already contains `"BC-19a - Done"`** — the row was executed
   before the deletion, not stranded by it. The defect is **packaging**, not substitution: two rows in
   different states presented as one question. Steering half-caught it (*"the owner ran BC-19a before
   deleting the notice"*) but left it as an open question when its own register already answered it.
2. **Yes, once more, factually**: *"and two toast-vessel test files"* — false at the tree (R19.1.1),
   inherited from D40.
3. **No, on the part that mattered most.** The near-miss steering reports catching — surface vs input —
   was caught correctly, and **the anti-substitution rule it re-read is the reason BC-3 and BC-19a could
   be ruled identical here on the code path rather than merely similar.** Its instruction to read primary
   instrument and subject apart is what made R19.2 decidable.

# Rev 20 — The D19 repair (D43) against this ledger: BC-11b unmoved, R18.3 corrected, BC-15's subject NOT established, U-1 realised.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-4`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-2` [019315], a
digest with no verdict requested and no schedule attached. **D43 read directly at plan rev 37
(`0df61af`), not from the digest.** **Nothing withdrawn. No row added. No test run. No database read —
none is possible from here.**

## R20.0 — Why a revision was required

1. **§R18.3 is wrong on its premise** and a reader would act on it. It judged BC-15 unrunnable by
   inheritance from BC-11b; the inheritance does not hold.
2. **Finding U-1 has fired.** A finding this document raised at rev 5 and routed as anticipatory is now
   realised. A register whose findings fire silently is not a register.
3. **Two rows must be stated as *unmoved*, with reasons** — BC-11b and the release decision — because
   D43's headline invites the opposite inference.
4. **BC-3's evidentiary basis changed** under steering's own correction and must be re-based.

## R20.1 — BC-11b: UNMOVED. `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED` stands.

Re-derived from this document's own terminalisation at **R14.2**, not from the digest:

> *"BC-11b's **both outcomes** clause needed **a spare seed beyond the population then available**; the mint route closed at
> deploy and no spares were minted."*

**The door D43 reports did not close was never BC-11b's door.** BC-11b is blocked by the absence of a
**third** record, not by the state of the first two. D19's repair — whatever it reached — cannot mint a
spare, and the mint route remains closed. **No future act changes it, which is what its terminality
means.** It is still **not** to be read as *closed*: it records a permanent absence of evidence.

## R20.2 — §R18.3 corrected *(reconcile note; its text stands)*

> **What R18.3 said, preserved verbatim in place**: *"BC-15 requires a 5712-shaped record. That is the
> population BC-11a drew on — **now STRUCK under **D44**** — and that BC-11b was already marked `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED`
> over — the mint route closed at deploy and no spares exist. **BC-15 appears to inherit that
> condition.**"*
>
> **Why it was defensible when written**: BC-11b and BC-15 draw on one population and this document had
> already recorded the ordering rule binding them (*BC-11b before BC-15 on any record; no Save after
> BC-15's Load*). Reading one's terminal state as reaching the other was the available inference.
>
> **Why it is wrong**: **the two rows are blocked by different things.** BC-11b needs a **spare** —
> a third record beyond the two seeds, because its *both outcomes* clause consumes one. BC-15 needs
> **one subject**, and it was never short of one on that count. Worse for the inference: BC-11b being
> permanently unrunnable means it will **never consume a seed**, so it *releases* the population rather
> than competing for it, and the ordering rule that bound them is moot. **The inheritance runs backwards
> from how R18.3 read it.**
>
> **Superseded by R20.3.** R18.3 was stated as a judgement and explicitly did not close the row, so
> nothing terminal is being undone — but the judgement was wrong and is withdrawn as a judgement.

## R20.3 / R20.4 — STRUCK under **D44**

Both sections are dropped as dependents: rev 20's subject-conditional reasoning for **BC-15**, the
discriminator it named, and **Finding U-1** together with the R-5 falsifier rewording its mitigation was
routed to. **Dropped, not re-argued and not re-based onto a substitute record.** Their consequences for
register state are recorded in **Rev 21**.

# Rev 21 — Owner ruling D44 executed against this document. A strike, not an amendment.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-4`. **Authority**: **D44**, plan rev 39 (`13a6e66`),
read directly at write time — not from the relay. **The ruling forbids reopening it as a question and
this pass does not.**

**What D44 requires, in its own terms**: two record IDs are struck from chain 013 entirely, and **every
dependent is dropped with them** — any finding, criterion, condition, detector, falsifier limb, residual
or open decision whose subject or evidence is those records **ceases to exist as a live obligation**.
**Dropped, not re-argued, not re-based onto a substitute, and not preserved 'for the record' in a note
that names them.** Scope narrowed by him to 013 `agent-output` files.

## R21.1 — What was done to this file

**30 lines carried the struck subject; all 30 are repaired. Verified zero remaining by two independent
instruments** — `grep -c` and a byte scan of the file read in full — because a filtered existence
predicate is exactly the instrument this chain has been bitten by six times.

**Form of the strike, and it follows the ruling rather than this stage's default.** This document's
normal correction form for a **record** is a dated reconcile note that preserves the superseded text.
**D44 overrides that here**: preserving the text in a note that names the subject is the one thing it
forbids. So each dependent is struck in place, **naming what was dropped and under which ruling, without
restating its subject**. Where a whole section's subject was struck, the section is replaced by that
line rather than edited around.

**"History preserves it" was verified before it licensed a deletion, not assumed.** The full prior text
is in commits `b5f2277` (rev 18), `6bfc063` (rev 19) and `0792c3b` (rev 20) — checked with `git log`
against this path at write time. **Nothing struck here existed only in the working tree.**

## R21.2 — Register consequences. These are this document's alone, and D44 does not set them.

| Row / finding | Before | **At rev 21** | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| **BC-15 · D28's mounted render** | `NOT EXECUTED`, subject-conditional (rev 20) | **`WITHDRAWN` — D44** | Its observation vehicle is struck and it **may not be re-based onto a substitute**. See R21.3 |
| **BC-11a · the mint** | `EXECUTED` 2026-08-24 — TERMINAL | **`STRUCK — D44`** | Its entire subject was the struck records. Nothing turns on it; the row number is retained only because later rows resolve through it |
| **Finding U-1** | REALISED (rev 20) | **`STRUCK — D44`** | Dependent dropped, with the R-5 falsifier rewording it was routed to. **Its REALISED marking is withdrawn** — see R21.4 |
| **BC-11b · the ask fires on save over a seed** | `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED` | **UNMOVED** | R21.5 |
| **Standing release decision** | `EXPIRED IN FULL` | **UNMOVED** | R21.5 |
| **BC-18 · `paste-notice-renders`** | `NOT EXECUTED`, prediction stale | **UNMOVED — and now the sole live condition of the standing decision** | Its subject is a paste surface, untouched by D44 |
| **BC-2, BC-3, BC-19a, BC-19b** | terminal at revs 19–20 | **unchanged** | No struck subject |

## R21.3 — Why BC-15 is `WITHDRAWN` and not a fifth or sixth state

**`WITHDRAWN` is the one terminal state this definition reserves to the owner, and he has now ruled.**
That is the whole reason it was never available to me before: at rev 20 I recorded BC-15 as
subject-conditional precisely because I could not withdraw it and would not close it by convenience.
**D44 is the ruling that was missing.** It does not merely make the row unobservable — it declares the
obligation ceased. That is withdrawal in the exact sense the register means it.

**It is deliberately *not* filed under either state this document invented.** Not
`TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED` (BC-11b's), which records an obligation that **still stands** and is
merely unreachable — reading BC-15 that way would leave a phantom requirement live. Not
`TERMINAL: SUBJECT WITHDRAWN` (BC-19b's), which was invented for a row whose *requirement* the owner
removed while **the row itself was left standing and routed to me**. **D44 does not leave the row
standing; it drops it by name-class.** Reaching for either invented state here — when the register's own
owner-reserved state fits exactly — would be the error this document warned against when it created
them.

**Recorded plainly: this is a withdrawal, so the coverage BC-15 stood for ships unobserved.** That cost
is the owner's to accept and he has accepted it. It is not softened here and it is not re-argued.

## R21.4 — Rev 20's REALISED marking on U-1 is withdrawn, and I record why rather than letting it lapse

At rev 20, minutes before D44, I marked U-1 **REALISED** on the strength of a relayed claim that
assumption 7's falsifier was armed. **Steering has since corrected that claim** (plan rev 38,
`fd4d490`): the classifier's work queue is scoped, so post-repair it classifies nothing and reports
zeroes over an empty set — an empty measurement that reads as a pass in the report's own JSON.

**I marked a finding realised on a mechanism I did not verify.** That is the defect, and it is mine, not
the relay's: this document's own rule is that a mechanism claim is checked at source before it is acted
on, and I had already refused two prose claims in the same exchange before accepting a third. **The
marking is withdrawn.** U-1 is struck under D44 in any case, so nothing downstream turns on it — which
is luck, not method.

**Standing caution carried forward, because it names no struck subject and is true on its own terms**:
*do not treat any zero from that instrument as evidence of anything*, and **anything keying on
`valid = false` must key on the reason string, not the boolean** (U-1's rev-7 correction, retained).

## R21.5 — Two things D44 does not reach, stated so nobody infers otherwise

**BC-11b — UNMOVED**, `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED`. Its blocker was the absence of a **spare** record
beyond the population then available; that is a fact about a route that closed at deploy, and no ruling
about the population's members changes it. It remains terminal, and it remains **not** to be read as
*closed*: it records a permanent absence of evidence.

**The standing release decision — UNMOVED**, `EXPIRED IN FULL`. Its three conditions were **BC-18**,
**BC-19a** and **BC-19b**; none had the struck records as subject or evidence. D44 reaches neither the
decision nor its expiry. **Expiry is not undone by a later ruling any more than it was by later good
news.**

**Concurrence recorded**: steering read both the same way and offered the reading for rejection. It is
adopted **after re-derivation from this document's own rows**, not on the offer.

## R21.6 — Two matters returned to steering, neither of them mine to fix

1. **D44 as registered will read false.** Its reversal column states *"STEERING DID NOT REWRITE OTHER
   STAGES' DOCUMENTS"*; steering's own message to this session says it is editing the qa, deployment,
   readiness, code-review, implementation and verification-probe documents directly under the ruling.
   Both may be true in sequence — registered first, acted after — but **the row does not say so**, and a
   later reader takes it as a standing fact about who touched what. **A reconcile note on D44 is owed by
   its author.** This document does not amend the plan.
2. **R20.3's discriminator is retracted and needs no answer.** Steering's second-instrument argument is

   real falsifier, not a restatement — and it is moot under D44 regardless. **No database read is owed by
   anyone.** Recorded because I asked the question in writing and should be seen to withdraw it in the
   same place.

## R21.7 — State

**UAT Complete** stands. **No verdict moved in this pass and none was requested.** **No release decision
exists for `2d90e8a`; the `4a7126b` decision remains `EXPIRED IN FULL`.**

**Live and non-terminal in this ledger after the strike**: **BC-18** (owner; prediction stale — QA owes

# Rev 22 — Two defects in my own rev 21, and a handover state for the incoming coordinator.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-4`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-2` [019315]
notified this session that the owner has asked it to hand off to a **fresh steering session** which
**rebuilds from files rather than from context**. **Nothing was asked of this stage.** A revision was
judged required anyway, for the reason the turnover creates: **a successor reading this document would
act on it, and two things in it were wrong.** Plan revs 35, 38, 40 and 41 were read directly at the
plan, not taken from the relay.

## R22.1 — Defect one: my D44 strike was digit-complete and **dependent-incomplete**

Rev 21 reported *"30 lines repaired, zero remaining, verified by two independent instruments."* **That
claim was true and it was the wrong claim.** Both instruments searched for the struck digits. **D44 drops
dependents, not digit-strings** — and steering had said so in the same breath it relayed the ruling:
*deleting the digits while leaving the claim standing is not compliance.* I read that sentence, quoted
it approvingly, and then verified against the weaker predicate anyway.

**What survived rev 21 by carrying no digits, and is struck here:** the retention instruction and its
whole section; four separate *"the disposition stays unruled"* clauses; the re-mint scarcity argument in
Appendix B.0 and the spare-minting recommendation built on it; the BC-11b and BC-15 legs of Appendix
B.4's run table; the Appendix B.5 ambiguity rule that governed a dropped row; Appendix C's payload row
for those rows; the attestation-existence clauses in the D20 precondition sections; and rev 20's own
Next Actions 2 and 3.

**Appendix A is struck as a whole** with a header rather than deleted: it is BC-11b's operator script,
BC-11b is terminal, and later sections resolve through its numbering.

**The lesson is the predicate, not the omission.** *An instrument that searches for the token searches
for the token.* A strike is verified against the **claim class** it was ordered against, and there is no
mechanical instrument for that — which is exactly why rev 21 reported a mechanical result and stopped.

## R22.2 — Defect two, and it is the worse one: rev 21's open set was WRONG

**R21.7 stated that after the strike the live set was BC-18 plus three `U-C` rows — *"Nothing else."*
That is false.** It collapsed the ledger to the *standing decision's own three conditions* and silently
dropped **fourteen** further non-terminal rows. A fresh coordinator acting on that sentence would have
read this chain as one look away from closure.

**Re-derived row by row from this document's own ledger sections (R13.7.2, R13.7.3, R14.2, R17.2) rather
than restated:**

**LIVE — `NOT EXECUTED`, non-terminal, gating chain closure (15 rows):**

| Class | Rows | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `PRE`, operator | **BC-1, BC-4, BC-5, BC-6, BC-7, BC-8, BC-9** | BC-4's suite limb met, fixture owed. **BC-5's payload remains unauthored.** BC-1/6/7/8/9 have literal payloads at Appendix C. **BC-1, BC-7, BC-8, BC-9 predictions STALE (rev 18); BC-6 possibly stale** |
| `PRE`, split limb | **BC-10** | **suite limb MET**; spot-observation limb `NOT EXECUTED`. **Both limbs stale (rev 18)** |
| `PRE`, operator | **BC-18** | prediction STALE; **the only one of the standing decision's three conditions still live** |
| `POST` | **BC-12, BC-13, BC-14, BC-17, BC-20, BC-21** | Gate chain closure, never the release act. **BC-13 is devops's and its instrument is the clock. BC-20 and BC-21 have absence signatures — no volume of quiet is evidence for them** |

**Plus documentary**: **U-C5** (owner — R-5's widening, the blocking edge), **U-C6**, **U-C7** (both
devops). **18 open items in total.**

**TERMINAL (do not re-raise):** BC-2, BC-3, BC-16, BC-19a — `EXECUTED`. BC-11 — `SUPERSEDED`. BC-11a —
`STRUCK — D44`. BC-15 — `WITHDRAWN` (D44). BC-11b — `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED` **(never read as
closed: it records a permanent absence of evidence)**. BC-19b — `TERMINAL: SUBJECT WITHDRAWN`. U-C1–U-C4,
U-C8 — `EXECUTED`. Finding U-1 — `STRUCK — D44`.

**Both defects share one shape, and it is the shape steering named about itself tonight**: a statement
of completeness asserted as a property of the record without being checked against the record. Rev 21
made it twice in one pass.

## R22.3 — Point-in-time items discharged by others, marked where they were listed

## R22.4 — Handover: what an incoming coordinator needs, that lives nowhere else

Written because the successor rebuilds from files. **Everything below is derivable from this document;
this section only puts it where a first reader lands.**

1. **The chain is not one look from closure. 18 items are open** (R22.2). The single most likely
   error a fresh coordinator can make here is inheriting rev 21's *"Nothing else."*
2. **No row may be executed against its current wording without QA re-deriving its prediction.**
   BC-1, BC-7, BC-8, BC-9, BC-10, BC-18 and BC-21 are marked **PREDICTION STALE** (rev 18) because the
   surfaces beneath them moved at `e343927` and `2d90e8a`. **An observation taken against a stale
   prediction produces a false failure**, which is worse than no observation. **Row definitions are
   QA's; state is this document's.**
3. **There is no release decision for the live tree.** `origin/master` = `2d90e8a` is **ungated** —
   neither approved nor refused — and the `4a7126b` decision is **`EXPIRED IN FULL`**. **Do not read
   the expiry as a refusal, and do not read production's health as an approval.** Under D37/D39,
   `2d90e8a` also sits inside the review debt; **D35's waiver reaches D30–D34 and not it**. Timing is
   the owner's.
4. **Two terminal states in this register are not in this stage's definition, and neither means
   *closed***: **BC-11b `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED`** (obligation stands, permanently unreachable —
   real residual risk) and **BC-19b `TERMINAL: SUBJECT WITHDRAWN`** (behaviour removed from the product;
   **no** residual risk). **Collapsing them loses the distinction that matters.** BC-15, by contrast, is
   plain `WITHDRAWN` — an obligation the owner ended.
5. **Instrument cautions that are true on their own terms and outlive their occasions**: *do not treat
   any zero from the backfill classifier as evidence of anything* (it reports zeroes over an empty set);
   *anything keying on `valid = false` must key on the reason string, not the boolean*; and *an
   existence or count predicate that cannot fail loudly* is this chain's recurring instrument-lie class,
   now seven sightings — **R22.1 is the seventh, and it is the first where the instrument was sound and
   the predicate was the wrong one.**
6. **The retrospective is HELD by owner ruling (D38) and nothing lifts it on elapsed time.**

## R22.5 — For the held retrospective

Two entries, both this stage's own and neither an apology:

- **A completeness claim is the one claim an instrument cannot make for you.** Rev 21 verified a token
  search and reported a strike complete; rev 22 found the strike incomplete on the claim class it was
  ordered against, and the open set overstated as closed in the same pass. **Where a pass reports
  "nothing else", the reader has no way to tell whether anything was counted.**
- **Steering's formulation, recorded as steering's**: the refusal of unverified prose *is not
  self-sustaining under volume* — this stage refused two prose claims in one exchange and accepted the
  third (R21.4). Paired with the above, the pattern is that **defences degrade under load in the order
  they are cheapest to skip**, and re-derivation is skipped last because it is visible. **A completeness
  claim is skipped first, because nothing downstream contradicts it.**

## R22.6 — State

**UAT Complete** stands. **No verdict moved in this pass; none was requested.** **18 items open**
(R22.2). **BC-18 remains the sole live condition of the standing — and expired — release decision.**

**Next Actions** — *point-in-time; later stage documents are authoritative on these items' current state.*

   **U-C5** remains his and remains the blocking edge before any deployment record.
3. **Devops — U-C6 and U-C7**, plus the deployment records the chain owes.
4. **Nobody closes this chain on rev 21's open set.** Use R22.2.

# Rev 23 — The BC-18 / BC-21 conflict, ruled. Neither row terminalises on the report, and the reason is the rows' own instrument clauses.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-4`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-2` [019315]
routed a conflict here as the authority on register state, recorded it in its docket **as a conflict**,
and **set nothing** — which was correct. **The owner reported BC-21 and BC-18 to steering as
*"-as expected-"* on 2026-08-25.** This document carries both non-terminal. **Row definitions are QA's
and were re-read from QA's register at write time; state is this document's.**

## R23.1 — Why this is the sharpest case this register has had

**An absence-signature row that an operator believes he has passed is the one place where a register and
an owner's memory diverge silently.** Steering named that correctly. It is worth being precise about why
it is not a matter of trusting him.

This stage's standing rule is that **an owner attestation is the terminal form of owner-supplied
evidence** — recorded with its timestamp and terminalised on, never met with a demand for a capture
file. That rule is not suspended here and nothing below asks him for evidence.

**What is in question is not the credibility of the report. It is which act the report describes** — and
for these two rows, QA wrote the discrimination into the row itself.

## R23.2 — BC-21: the row's own instrument clause forbids the reading that would close it

QA's row, quoted from the register at write time:

> **Predicted**: *one **deliberate paste** of a rows-B/D payload into New Message on the live site
> produces a visible persistent warning.*
> **Primary instrument**: *a single **deliberate observation** — **not** a wait for user reports, which
> cannot detect an absence.*
> **Sufficiency**: *one successful observation refutes "the notice is silently absent" **by
> construction*** — not a rate, no sample.

***"-as expected-"* is compatible with two acts, and the row separates them explicitly:**

| Reading | Effect on BC-21 |
|---|---|
| *"I pasted a rows-B/D payload deliberately and the warning appeared"* | **Closes the row by construction.** One observation is sufficient; no capture is owed and none may be asked for |
| *"Nothing looked wrong / nothing was reported"* | **Worth nothing against this row.** Its failure signature is **absence**, and QA's instrument cell rules out exactly this reading in advance |

**So the row cannot be terminalised on the phrase — not because the report is doubted, but because the
row was written to distinguish the two things the phrase can mean.** Recording it as `EXECUTED` on the
ambiguous reading would be laundering an absence into a pass, which is the single failure mode 013
exists to close and which this document has refused three times already this chain.

**BC-21 → `NOT EXECUTED`, non-terminal. `OWNER REPORT ON RECORD, NOT YET DECIDABLE.`** Not a doubt, and
not a new condition — it is the same row, with a live report attached to it and the one word that
settles it named.

**What settles it is one word, and it is not evidence.** Whether the observation was a **deliberate
paste** or a general impression. **No capture, file, screenshot or re-run is owed by him, and none is
requested.** If it was a deliberate paste, the row is `EXECUTED` on his statement alone, dated
2026-08-25, and this document will record it that way without further evidence.

## R23.3 — BC-21's rev-18 staleness marking was over-broad, and that is routed to QA, not performed here

Rev 18 marked BC-21 **"PREDICTION STALE by inheritance"** from BC-18, on the ground that they share a
surface. **Re-read against QA's row, that inheritance does not hold on its own terms**: BC-21's
predicate is **presence** — *a visible persistent warning is produced* — and names neither wording nor
clearing behaviour. A reword does not stale a presence predicate.

**This document does not unstale the row.** Prediction re-derivation is QA's, exactly as this stage has
routed it throughout; a marking removed by the wrong stage is the same defect as a marking applied by
one. **It is recorded as a judgement for QA**, in the form rev 18 used for BC-15: stated, not closed.

**Why it matters to the conflict**: if QA agrees, BC-21 is decidable **today** on one word, whereas BC-18
is not decidable at all until its prediction is re-derived. **The two rows are not in the same position,
and the report reaching them in one sentence is what made them look alike.**

## R23.4 — BC-18: not decidable on the report, on a stronger ground

QA's row requires **two limbs**: *a persistent warning appears **and stays put**, **and clears** when the
JSON is corrected.* Its predicted value repeats it — *a warning appears and persists; correcting the JSON
removes it.*

Rev 18 marked BC-18 stale on a **derived** basis, not by inheritance: D31 and D32 changed what that
surface does at rest, and its *persistent-warning expectation is not what the surface now does*. **The
staleness lands on exactly the limb the report cannot speak to** — nobody reports *"as expected"* about a
clearing behaviour whose expected form the register itself no longer states correctly.

**BC-18 → `NOT EXECUTED`, non-terminal. Prediction STALE. Owner report on record and does not reach it.**

**And the standing rule is unchanged and now load-bearing in the other direction**: rev 18 warned that
executing a stale row yields a **false failure**. *The mirror is a false pass*, and it is the more
dangerous of the two, because a false failure gets investigated and a false pass gets filed. **BC-18 must
not be executed — or recorded as executed — against its current wording. QA owes the re-derivation.**

## R23.5 — The release decision does not move

**BC-18 remains the sole live condition of the standing release decision, and that decision remains
`EXPIRED IN FULL`.** An owner report that does not terminalise the condition changes neither. Stated
because a report of *"as expected"* against the last live condition is precisely where a reader would
expect movement, and there is none.

## R23.6 — The docket propagation, recorded because it enlarges my own finding

Steering reports that rev 21's false *"nothing else"* had **already been copied into this cwd's steering
docket as the live set** before rev 22 caught it — and that the docket is the **handoff artifact**, the
successor's second read. A fresh coordinator would not have found the fourteen missing rows by reading
this register, **because the docket would have told it there was nothing to look for.**

**That enlarges R22.5's finding rather than repeating it.** The blast radius of a completeness claim is
not the document that makes it: **a completeness claim is the most copyable sentence in any record,
because it is short, it is actionable, and it closes a question the reader wanted closed.**
Re-derivation resists propagation because it is long and visibly conditional; *"nothing else"* travels
in one line. **Carried to the retrospective in that form.**

Steering has corrected the docket as a correction rather than as a clean list, naming the false claim and
that its thread propagated it, and instructing the successor to re-read this register at write time
rather than restate a count. **Recorded here so the correction is visible from this side too**, since a
count restated from a docket is exactly what this chain has been bitten by.

## R23.7 — Ledger delta at rev 23

| Row | Before | **At rev 23** | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| **BC-21 · `notice-silence-detector`** | `NOT EXECUTED`, POST, stale by inheritance | **`NOT EXECUTED` — OWNER REPORT ON RECORD, NOT YET DECIDABLE.** Staleness marking **queried to QA** (R23.3) | The row's own instrument clause separates the two readings of the report |
| **BC-18 · `paste-notice-renders`** | `NOT EXECUTED`, prediction STALE | **unchanged** — report on record, does not reach it | Its clearing limb is derived-stale (R23.4) |
| **Standing release decision** | `EXPIRED IN FULL` | **unchanged** | R23.5 |
| everything else | — | **untouched** | — |

**The open set stays at 18 items (R22.2).** Nothing is added, nothing is closed, nothing is withdrawn.

## R23.8 — State

**UAT Complete** stands. **No verdict moved.**

**Next Actions** — *point-in-time; later stage documents are authoritative.*

1. **The owner — one word, not evidence**: was the BC-21 observation a **deliberate paste**, or a general
   impression? **If deliberate, BC-21 is `EXECUTED` on his statement alone and nothing further is owed.**
   Nothing is asked of him beyond that word; no capture is requested and none will be recorded as
   missing.
2. **QA — two items.** BC-18's prediction re-derivation (already owed), and the BC-21 staleness query at
   R23.3, which may make that row decidable today.
3. **Nobody records BC-18 or BC-21 as executed on the report as it stands.** A false pass gets filed; a
   false failure gets investigated. **The first is the worse outcome and the easier one to reach here.**

# Rev 24 — D45 received. It moves nothing here, and one inference it invites must be refused in advance.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-4`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529]
relayed a standing owner ruling. **Read at the plan (rev 42, `491a540`), not taken from the relay** —
the summary matches the row.

## R24.1 — What D45 does, and what it correctly disclaims

*"Bring 013 pipeline to completion. Retro is the last to fire (only when no other work is in flight,
i.e. all prior agents are complete)."*

It lifts D39's **"NEITHER RUNS NOW"** — `code-reviewer` and `qa` run — and converts D38's open-ended
retrospective hold into a sequencing condition with a trigger. **The row states four things it does not
reach**, and states them so nobody infers them: no release decision for `2d90e8a`, no lift of chain
012's D20, **no operator sitting against a row whose prediction is still STALE**, and no register state.

**That third disclaimer is the BC-18 guard from rev 23, honoured at registration without being asked
for.** Recorded because a disclaimer written before anyone leans on it is the cheapest form this
register has, and worth naming when it happens.

**Nothing in this ledger moves. Open set stays at 18 (R22.2). The standing decision stays `EXPIRED IN
FULL`. BC-18 and BC-21 stay exactly as rev 23 left them.**

## R24.2 — The inference to refuse: a clean trailing review does not revive an expired decision

The trailing review is now running. Within a day or so this chain will hold **code review approved** and
**QA Complete** at a tree, over a document already marked **UAT Complete**. That combination reads, to
any reasonable person arriving fresh, as *release*.

**It is not, and the reason is structural rather than a matter of caution.**

- **The standing decision is `EXPIRED IN FULL`, and expiry is not a suspension.** It did not pause
  pending better evidence; it ceased to bind when all four covered blobs moved or were deleted. **There
  is nothing there to revive.** A decision at `2d90e8a` would be a **new** decision, reached by someone
  who states the tree they reached it at.
- **`UAT Complete` is a status on this document, not a gate on `2d90e8a`.** It was reached at `4a7126b`.
  A stage status carried forward across a tree change is the same error as a verdict carried forward,
  and this chain has already paid for that once.
- **The arriving verdicts inherit no anchor.** Whoever writes the final gate reproduces the tree identity
  from the tree — SHA if committed, per-path `sha256sum` manifest if not — rather than inheriting one
  from a predecessor's document or a docket. **A verdict without a named identity cannot expire, which
  is precisely what makes it dangerous rather than merely incomplete.**
- **Eighteen conditions stand open, and a release decision cannot be recorded complete while any of them
  is non-terminal.** "Pipeline to completion" is a ruling about which stages run. **It is not a ruling
  that the ledger is discharged, and D45 does not claim to be.**

**So: `code-reviewer` approved + `qa` Complete + `UAT Complete` ≠ approved for release.** Whoever assembles
the final gate opens R22.2, terminalises or conditionally states each of the 18, and names the tree.

**This is the same false-pass shape as rev 23, one level up.** There, an owner's *"as expected"* met a row
whose instrument ruled that reading out. Here, three green stage statuses meet a decision that no longer
exists. In both, the wrong answer is the one that closes a question the reader wanted closed, and it
arrives looking like arithmetic.

## R24.3 — Ceiling

This session is near its run-length ceiling and **will not be present when code review and QA report**.
**That is expected and is not a handback mid-gate** — no gate is open here. Rev 23's ruling and this
entry are complete and committed; a successor picks up the final gate **from this file**, and the three
outstanding items are unchanged: the owner's one word on BC-21, and QA's two (BC-18's re-derivation, the
R23.3 staleness query).

**Nothing is asked of the coordinator, and this stage does not self-start.**

# Rev 25 — BC-21 → `EXECUTED`. The owner answered, and the staleness query does not gate it.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-4`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529]
relayed the owner's answer to the one word rev 23 asked for. **He chose "Deliberate paste".**

## R25.1 — Provenance, weighed rather than adopted

Steering disclosed the provenance unprompted and precisely, which is what made it weighable:
**no verbatim sentence** — a selection prompt, **options agent-authored** by steering from rev 23's
scoping, not the owner's words. **That disclosure is the reason this closes; an undisclosed selection
presented as an attestation would not have.**

**What he selected asserts the observation, not merely the intent.** The option was described to him as
*"You pasted a rows-B/D payload on purpose **and the warning appeared**."* Selecting it adopts that
sentence.

**Two alternatives stood and were passed over**, including a free one: *"General impression"* (described
as unable to reach an absence-signature row) and *"Leave it open"* (*"You would rather not answer this
from memory"*). **A person uncertain from memory had a costless exit and declined it.** That the framing
did not corner him matters, because agent-authored options carry a framing risk: the taken option was
described attractively. **The untaken one was described as equally costless, so the attraction was not
a squeeze.**

**No evidence was requested and none is recorded as missing against him** — the standing rule, honoured.

**One residual, named rather than papered over**: he selected a **label**; he did not independently
describe the payload. *"rows-B/D payload"* is a QA construct, and its identification here rests on the
option text he **adopted**, not on his own characterisation. **This does not block the row** — adopting
a described act is asserting it — but it is the difference between a selection and a verbatim account,
and the register should carry it.

## R25.2 — Why the R23.3 staleness query does not have to land first

Rev 23 left BC-21's rev-18 *"PREDICTION STALE by inheritance"* marking **queried to QA and not removed**,
because definitions are QA's. Steering correctly declined to decide whether that query gates the row.
**It does not, and the reason is not expedience.**

**Staleness can only bite where the observation could diverge from the prediction. A presence predicate
observed as present cannot diverge from itself.**

- BC-21's predicate is **presence**: *produces a visible persistent warning*. Its failure signature is
  **absence**. QA's sufficiency cell is explicit: *one successful observation refutes "the notice is
  silently absent" **by construction*** — not a rate, no sample.
- Whatever QA rules on re-derivation, **no re-derivation can convert an observed visible warning into a
  silent absence.** Sharpened wording, a changed string, a different phrasing — none of it reaches the
  thing this row exists to detect.
- **Contrast BC-18, and note the contrast is what keeps this consistent.** Its stale limb is *clears when
  the JSON is corrected* — a limb where observation and prediction **can** diverge, because D31/D32
  changed the behaviour the prediction describes. There, "as expected" risks a **false pass**. Here there
  is no divergence available.

**So this is not rev 23's reasoning reversed to reach a convenient answer; it is rev 23's reasoning
applied, and the two rows separate for the same reason they separated then.**

**The query to QA is not withdrawn and nothing now turns on it.** QA may still correct the marking for
the record; **it gates nothing and should not be prioritised over BC-18's owed re-derivation**, which
remains the live item.

## R25.3 — Ledger delta at rev 25

| Row | Before | **At rev 25** | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|
| **BC-21 · `notice-silence-detector`** | `NOT EXECUTED` — owner report on record, not yet decidable | **`EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25** | Owner selected *"Deliberate paste"*, adopting *"and the warning appeared"*; QA's sufficiency closes it on one observation **by construction**. Provenance and residual at R25.1 |
| **BC-18 · `paste-notice-renders`** | `NOT EXECUTED`, prediction STALE | **unchanged** | R23.4; its stale limb admits divergence, this row's did not |
| **Standing release decision** | `EXPIRED IN FULL` | **unchanged** | Expiry is not suspension (R24.2); a terminalised condition does not revive it |
| **Open set** | **18** (R22.2) | **17** | BC-21 terminal |

**The observed result is recorded beside the prediction, not over it.** BC-21's row keeps its written
predicate; the observation is a dated fact attached to it.

## R25.4 — State

**UAT Complete** stands. **No verdict moved, and the release decision stays `EXPIRED IN FULL`** — a
condition reaching a terminal state is the register working, not a change of verdict.

**Outstanding, now two:**

1. **QA — BC-18's prediction re-derivation.** The live one. (The R23.3 BC-21 query survives, gates
   nothing, and is not a priority.)
2. **The final gate at whatever tree it is written against** — R24.2 governs: three green stage statuses
   are not a release decision, and **16 further conditions remain non-terminal.**

**Nothing is asked of the coordinator.**

# Rev 26 — BC-5 → `WITHDRAWN` on the owner's ruling. Open set 17 → 16. Two reconcile notes land with it.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5` (successor to `uat-hooks-013-4`, which reached its
request ceiling). **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] relayed an owner ruling **and disclosed that
it had failed to relay it to this stage earlier**. **No gate is opened here.** Code review, QA and devops
are all still in flight; R24.2 governs whatever gate follows, and this pass does not pre-empt it.

## R26.1 — The relay gap, recorded as the process fact it is

Steering relayed the BC-5 withdrawal to `devops-hooks-013-2` today and **did not relay it to this stage**.
This session's read-in therefore enumerated BC-5 as live and reported it to steering twice — once in the
open set, once under *"standing, not mine to discharge"*. **Steering disclosed the omission unprompted,
named it as its own error, and named both places it had propagated to.**

**Recorded, not scored.** It is worth one line in the register for a reason that is not blame: **this
document's open set is only as current as the relays that reach it**, and a row can go terminal without
this stage learning of it. The defence is not vigilance — it is that a ledger delta is re-derived at write
time from the file plus every ruling *known* at that moment, and that a stage which cannot rule out
unrelayed rulings says so. **This entry is this stage saying so.** The gate under R24.2 will restate the
same caveat rather than assert the open set is complete as a property of the world.

## R26.2 — The ruling, and why the verbatim sentence did not settle it

**The owner's own unprompted words were:** *"I'm not doing BC-5."*

**That sentence does not decide the row, and steering was right not to act on it.** It is ambiguous
between two register outcomes with different consequences: *the obligation ends*, and *the obligation
stands but someone else executes it*. **BC-5 is an operator `PRE` row; "I am not the one who runs it" is
a statement about the subject, not about the obligation.** Under this register's own vocabulary those are
`WITHDRAWN` and a re-subjected row respectively, and nothing in the sentence chooses.

Steering put the distinction to him **in this register's vocabulary**, and he chose **"Withdraw it"** —
described as *"BC-5 is WITHDRAWN, same as BC-15 — an obligation you ended. It stops blocking closure and
nothing is claimed, either way, about the behaviour it would have tested."*

**Two alternatives stood, and which one he declined is the load-bearing fact:**

| Option, as described to him | Taken? | What declining it establishes |
|---|---|---|
| **"Establish the fixture"** — *nobody knows what a folder-page export literally looks like; a researcher is commissioned, and BC-5 becomes runnable **by someone who is not you*** | **declined** | **This is the option that answers *"I'm not doing BC-5"* on its literal reading** — it removes him as subject and preserves the test. Declining it resolves the ambiguity **toward ending the obligation**, and it does so on his own choice rather than on anyone's construction of his sentence |
| **"Stands, unexecutable"** — *the obligation stands and goes on the record as permanently undischarged, like BC-11b; the residual risk is named and stays visible* | **declined** | He declined to keep the row on the books |
| **"Withdraw it"** | **taken** | The obligation ends; nothing is claimed about the behaviour |

**The row closes to `WITHDRAWN` — 2026-08-25, ruled by the owner, who is the only party who may rule a
withdrawal and is BC-5's own subject.**

## R26.3 — Framing risk: weaker than BC-21's, and it does not matter here. The reason it does not matter is the point

**Provenance, disclosed by steering unprompted and to the same standard as R25.1**: agent-authored
options, steering's wording, **no verbatim sentence beyond *"I'm not doing BC-5."*** That disclosure is
again what makes the ruling weighable rather than adoptable.

**Applying R25.1's own test honestly, this framing is less neutral than BC-21's was.** There, the untaken
exit (*"Leave it open"*) was described as **equally costless**, so the taken option's attractiveness was
not a squeeze. Here neither alternative was costless: *"Establish the fixture"* spends a researcher, and
*"Stands, unexecutable"* costs a permanent entry on the record. **The chosen option was the only one with
no cost attached to it.** By R25.1's standard that is a real asymmetry, and it is recorded rather than
waved through.

**It does not block the close, and the reason is a distinction this register has not yet had to draw:**

> **R25.1's framing test governs elicited *facts*. It does not govern elicited *rulings*.**
>
> BC-21 asked the owner to report something that either happened or did not; a badly framed prompt can
> make a person assert a fact they do not hold, and the framing analysis is the guard against that.
> **BC-5 asked the owner to decide whether an obligation continues.** He is not reporting — he is
> exercising an authority that is constitutive: **the withdrawal is true because he ruled it, not
> true if he ruled accurately.** There is no fact behind it for a framing to distort. An attractive
> option can mis-elicit a report; it cannot mis-elicit a prerogative.

**So the asymmetry is noted and is not a defect in this close.** It is recorded because the next row this
register terminalises on a selection prompt may be a factual one, and **the test to apply then is R25.1's,
not this one.**

## R26.4 — The residual. Steering flagged it, declined to argue it, and left it to this stage. It is real, and it is a *fourth* shape

BC-5's subject is a behaviour **nobody has ever characterised** — that is precisely why no fixture exists
and why its payload stood unauthored through eighteen revisions. **The withdrawal ends the obligation; it
does not characterise the behaviour, and by its own terms claims nothing about it either way.**

**This register already insists that terminal states not be collapsed (R22.4 §4). BC-5 forces the same
distinction one step further:**

| Row | Terminal state | Obligation | Subject | Residual |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BC-11b** | `CANNOT BE EXECUTED` | **stands** | in product, uncharacterised | **Real, and self-advertising** — the row stays open-shaped, so the gap points at itself |
| **BC-19b** | `SUBJECT WITHDRAWN` | ends | **removed from product** | **None.** Nothing left to be uncertain about |
| **BC-15** | `WITHDRAWN` | ends (owner) | — | obligation ended by ruling |
| **BC-5** | **`WITHDRAWN`** | **ends (owner)** | **in product, uncharacterised** | **Real, and *silent*** |

**BC-5 is the combination none of the others is: the obligation ends while the subject stays in the
product and stays unknown.** BC-19b's withdrawal removed the thing; BC-5's removes only the duty to look
at it. **So the uncertainty survives the row that would have resolved it — and survives with nothing on
the books pointing at it**, because `WITHDRAWN` is a closed shape and a reader scanning terminal rows sees
a discharged register. **BC-11b's residual is louder than BC-5's precisely because BC-11b is still open.**

**That is the argument for naming it, and naming it is the ruling.**

**The obvious objection, answered rather than skipped:** *he declined "Stands, unexecutable", which was
described as keeping the residual "named and stays visible" — does naming it now defy his ruling?* **No,
and the chosen option's own words are why.** That option bundled two things: **the obligation stands** and
**the residual is visible**. What he declined was the bundle, and the ledger consequence is that the
obligation ends. Meanwhile *"Withdraw it"* was described to him as **claiming nothing, either way, about
the behaviour** — that is a statement that the withdrawal is **silent** on the behaviour, **not that the
behaviour is asserted fine**. A residual note reading *"this behaviour is uncharacterised and no row now
covers it"* claims nothing either way about it, so it sits **inside** the description he adopted rather
than against it. **Omitting it is what would exceed his ruling**, by letting a reader infer a coverage
that was never claimed.

**What the residual note is not, stated so it cannot be read back as the obligation returning:** it
creates **no task, no owner, no gate, and no prediction**. It discharges nothing and blocks nothing. It is
one line under residual risk in the gate recording that a behaviour in the product is uncharacterised and
that no row covers it. **If any later stage treats it as an obligation, that stage has misread it, and
this sentence is here to be quoted at that stage.**

## R26.5 — Two reconcile notes, landed here because a rev was opening anyway

Both were reported to steering at read-in as items to land with the gate. **A rev is open, so they land
now rather than accumulate.** Neither is a verdict change; **the superseded text stands where it was
written and is not overwritten.**

**Note 1 — the successor-facing sections still say "18", and that is the shape they exist to warn about.**
R22.4 §1 reads *"18 items are open"*; R24.2 says it twice — *"terminalises or conditionally states each of
the 18"* and *"Eighteen conditions stand open"*. **All three were true when written and were superseded by
rev 25 (18 → 17) and again by this rev (17 → 16).** They are **not corrected in place**: R22.2 remains the
authoritative derivation and R22.4/R24.2 remain the point-in-time prose they were. **This note is the
pointer.** It is recorded because R22.2's own recorded defect was a stale open-set sentence in a
handover section being read as current, and the mitigation cannot be *"the reader will scroll"*.

**Note 2 — R25.4's *"16 further conditions remain non-terminal"* reconciles only under a subtraction the
reader has to guess, and its number is now right by coincidence.** At rev 25 the open set was **17**; the
sentence reconciles only if BC-18 is read as consumed by item 1 of the same list. Read plainly it stated a
total of 16, which was wrong. **After this rev the open set genuinely is 16 — for an entirely unrelated
reason, BC-5's withdrawal.** **That coincidence is not a vindication and must not be read as one**: a
number that requires the reader to perform the right subtraction is a trap whether or not it later comes
out even, and this one came out even by accident. **The authoritative statement of the open set is R26.6
and R22.2 as amended, never a sentence in a state block.**

## R26.7 — Tree identity at this rev, and the anchor scope question is NOT being put to the owner yet

**Re-derived at this rev's write time, not inherited from steering, from a docket, or from rev 25:**
`origin/master` = **`2d90e8a60270740b59b4768a68cf3f67641e4345`**, local `master` at the same commit,
tracked-clean (`git status --porcelain` → 0 lines). **`2d90e8a` remains ungated** — neither approved nor
refused. **The expiry is not a refusal and production's health is not an approval.**

**This rev is not a verdict and therefore is not anchored as one.** The identity above is recorded as the
state this ledger delta was written against; **no release decision is reached here, so there is nothing to
expire.**

**On the covered path set (this stage's own R24.2 gap, reported at read-in):** steering has offered to put
the scope question to the owner as a blocking prompt and has **correctly not pre-empted it**. **This stage
declines the offer for now**, and the reason is a rule worth holding: **a question that can be answered by
derivation is not escalated.** R24.2 requires the gate to name a tree identity but never defines which
paths the manifest covers, and the covered set is what makes an anchor falsifiable. **That set is derivable
from this chain's own documents** — the four blobs the expired decision covered are recorded, and the
plan's touched surface is recorded. **The gate will derive it, state it as an explicit set of paths rather
than as a hash alone, and escalate only if derivation leaves a genuine choice the owner must make.**
**Asking the owner a question this stage can answer itself is the failure mode, not the diligence.**

## R26.8 — State

**UAT Complete** stands — **a status on this document reached at `4a7126b`, not a gate on `2d90e8a`.**
**No verdict moved.** **The release decision stays `EXPIRED IN FULL`** — a condition reaching a terminal
state is the register working, not a change of verdict, and that is as true of a withdrawal as it was of
rev 25's execution.

**Open set: 16** (R26.6, enumerated).

**Outstanding — two, unchanged in kind:**

1. **QA — BC-18's prediction re-derivation.** The live one. (The R23.3 BC-21 staleness query survives,
   gates nothing, and is not a priority.)
2. **The final gate at whatever tree it is written against.** **R24.2 governs and is not softened by this
   rev**: code review approved + QA Complete + UAT Complete **≠** approved for release. Sixteen conditions
   stand non-terminal, and a release decision cannot be recorded complete while any of them is.

**Owner-only and not this stage's to discharge, as amended:** **U-C5**, and the operator `PRE` rows — the
latter still blocked behind QA's stale-prediction re-derivation, because a row run against stale wording
yields a false pass and **a false pass gets filed rather than investigated**. **BC-5 is no longer on this
list.**

**Nothing is asked of the coordinator.** This stage does not self-start and is holding for word that code
review and QA have reported.

**Next Actions** — *point-in-time; later stage documents are authoritative on these items' current state.*

1. **QA** — BC-18's prediction re-derivation (already owed).
2. **This stage** — on word from steering, open R22.2 as amended, terminalise or conditionally state each
   of the 16, derive and state the covered path set, and name the tree identity the gate is reached at.
3. **Nobody** — records BC-5 as executed, or reads its withdrawal as evidence about the behaviour it would
   have tested. **It claims nothing either way, by the owner's own chosen words.**
