# Rev 27 — U-C5 → `EXECUTED`, BC-13 and U-C7 confirmed terminal at their own documents. Open set 16 → 13. **And a conflict on BC-11b that must be settled before any gate.**

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529]
relayed an owner ruling on U-C5 plus three row corrections from `devops-hooks-013-2`, **explicitly as
citations to check rather than as facts it had established, and set nothing itself.** **Every citation
below was read at the cited document at this rev's write time.** **No gate is opened here** — code
review and QA have still not reported.

## R27.1 — U-C5 → `EXECUTED`. What he ruled on was narrower than the row as written, and that delta is the record

**The row as written is obsolete in two of its three limbs, and neither this stage nor the owner made it
so.** Verified at the documents, not taken from the relay:

| Limb of U-C5 as written | Status | Verified at |
|---|---|---|
| *R-5's widening gates an unwritten deployment record* | **Obsolete.** D25 offered two discharge routes; readiness **L531** records the owner choosing the planner pass with *"the record stays unwritten"* — **and the record was then written anyway and took route two.** Deployment **L158–160**: *"This record therefore takes D25's second route"*; **L12** discharges **D25 item (iii)** that way, since `e34df07` | readiness L531; deployment L12, L152–162 |
| *U-1 folds into this same decision point* | **Folds in nothing.** **U-1 was struck under D44 at rev 21** by this document | R22.2, this file |
| *the plan's own description of the shipped change* | **Live, and the only thing left** | — |

**So the question actually put to him was the narrow one**: is the **plan document** corrected to describe
the whole of the shipped behaviour change, or left describing half of it. The plan states that
mode-follows-marking changed how **flagged-but-empty** messages open. It does not state that **ordinary
component-less messages changed too** — previously skipped, leaving whatever mode was on screen; now
actively set to classic.

**HIS RULING: "Record is enough."** Described to him as *"Nothing more happens. The plan keeps a sentence
describing half the change; anyone reading the plan later rather than the deployment record gets the
narrow version and could act on it."* **Not taken:** *"Fix the plan"* (one planner round, after which the
plan matches what shipped) and *"Leave it open"* (*"the chain does not close while it sits there"*).

**He declined the option that was described to him as blocking closure.** He therefore unblocked closure
**knowing** the plan stays incomplete, with that consequence stated in the option he took.

**Terminal state — `EXECUTED`, 2026-08-25, and the reason it is not `SUPERSEDED`:** two limbs were
discharged elsewhere and one was ruled. This stage's standing rule is that **a required action discharged
in a different document than the one it named is closed on the substance, with the delta recorded — not
returned for a documentation shuffle.** U-C5's substance is discharged: partly by the deployment record
at `e34df07` taking D25's second route, partly by rev 21's strike, and the remainder by the owner's
ruling today. **The delta is the table above. The row closes on the substance.**

**Provenance** — agent-authored options, enumerated by `devops` from routes already existing in the
record, **no verbatim sentence**; disclosed unprompted, the same discipline as BC-21 and BC-5.

**Framing, tested against R26.3 and passing more cleanly than BC-5 did:** this is again an elicited
**ruling**, not an elicited fact, so R25.1's framing test is not the governing one. But it is worth
recording that **the taken option carried its own cost in its own description** — *"anyone reading the
plan later … could act on it."* **An option that states the price of taking it is the form these prompts
should take**, and this one did.

## R27.2 — The narrower point devops got right, and it is worth naming

Devops declined to ask the owner U-C5's question **as written**, on the ground that the question no longer
matched the facts — two limbs having been discharged or struck. **That judgement is correct and is the
harder call.** Putting a stale question to the owner would have produced an answer to a question nobody
had, and **an answer to a stale question gets filed as though it answered the live one.** That is this
chain's recurring false-pass shape, caught here **before** it reached the owner rather than after.

## R27.3 — **BC-11b: this register and both devops documents state incompatible terminal states, and the gate cannot be written until it is settled**

**This is the finding of this pass and it is not a documentation tidy-up.**

| Document | BC-11b's state | Consequence for the gate |
|---|---|---|
| **This register** (R22.2, R22.4 §4) | **`TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED`** — *"never read as closed: it records a permanent absence of evidence"*; **R22.4 §4 instructs the successor to carry it into the gate as real residual risk** | **A residual is named** |
| **Readiness** L958, **deployment** L141–143, L254, L535–537, L567 — **four places, mutually consistent** | **`DROPPED under D44`** (plan rev 39, `13a6e66`) — *"Its subject is struck from chain 013"*; *"not a live obligation on this chain… blocked nothing before the strike and blocks nothing after it"* | **There is nothing to name.** Carrying a residual would be carrying a **phantom** |

**Both cannot be current.** `CANNOT BE EXECUTED` says *we are obliged to know and permanently cannot* —
R22.4 §4's whole point is that this must not be collapsed into "closed". `DROPPED under D44` says the
obligation is **gone with its subject**. **One of these two documents will mislead a reader on residual
risk, and it is the gate that publishes the answer.**

**What this stage can and cannot do here, stated exactly:**

- **It cannot resolve it.** Deciding which row D44 dropped means identifying the struck subject. **D44 is
  absolute: no stage may cite, rely on, reason from or re-derive the struck IDs.** This stage has not
  attempted to and will not. **This section names a conflict between two documents' recorded states; it
  does not investigate D44 and asks nobody why.**
- **It is bounded, and the bound matters.** **Both documents agree BC-11b is terminal and blocks
  nothing.** So the conflict **does not move the open set** — 13 either way — and does not touch the
  release decision. **It is confined entirely to the gate's residual section**, which is also the one
  place a reader looks to learn what is *not* known.

**And this stage must record which way the evidence leans, against its own document.** **It leans against
this register.** Three grounds:

1. **Four consistent statements across two devops documents, each naming the same ruling and commit
   (`13a6e66`, plan rev 39), against one statement here.**
2. **BC-11a and BC-11b are the mint and the ask of the same subject.** This register already carries
   **BC-11a as `STRUCK — D44`**. **If the subject is struck, both legs fall** — and a register that struck
   one leg while leaving the other on a pre-strike terminal state is describing an outcome that has no
   coherent reading.
3. **It is exactly the defect rev 22 diagnosed and evidently did not fully clear.** R22.1's own words:
   the rev-21 strike was *"digit-complete and dependent-incomplete"* — the instruments searched for the
   token, **and D44 drops dependents.** Rev 22 struck nine dependent passages. **BC-11b's own row state
   appears to be a tenth it did not reach.** *An instrument that searches for the token searches for the
   token* — and BC-11b's row carries no token.

**So the likely fact is that this register is the stale document and R22.4 §4's instruction to the
successor is itself the propagation.** **That is not asserted, because this stage cannot verify it without
doing the one thing D44 forbids.** **It is routed.**

**Routed to steering, for the owner or for whoever holds D44's execution** — one question, requiring no
explanation and no reopening: **did D44 drop BC-11b?** **Yes** → this register corrects to `DROPPED —
D44` by dated reconcile note, the superseded text preserved, and **no BC-11b residual is named in the
gate.** **No** → the devops documents carry the correction, and the residual stands as R22.4 §4 has it.

**Nobody writes the gate's residual section until this is answered**, because both available answers are
currently supported by a committed document, and **a residual section that is confidently wrong is worse
than one that is late.**

## R27.4 — A restatement lag, recorded so it is reconciled rather than argued

The deployment record's second-act table (**L552**) carries **BC-21 as `NOT EXECUTED`**. **This register
has BC-21 `EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25 (rev 25).** **This is not a conflict**: the record predates rev 25, and
the standing division is that **row definitions are QA's and row state is this document's**. Devops's
table is a restatement that has fallen behind its authority, and the fix is devops's at its next pass.

**It is recorded because the same shape, one level up, is R27.3.** A restated status is only as current as
the moment it was restated, and **neither document above names the other as its authority for these rows**
— which is why the BC-11b divergence could sit in four committed places without anything contradicting it.

## R27.5 — The residual inventory the gate will publish, assembled here so it is not assembled under time pressure

**Four candidates, three certain and one pending R27.3.** **All four are *silent* — none announces itself
to a reader, and that is the property they share:**

| Residual | Status | Why it is silent |
|---|---|---|
| **BC-5's subject** (R26.4) — a behaviour in the product, **never characterised**, obligation ended by owner ruling | **Certain** | `WITHDRAWN` is a closed shape; a reader scanning terminal rows sees a discharged register |
| **The plan's half-sentence** (R27.1) — by owner ruling, the plan describes half the shipped change and **no stage may "correct" it** | **Certain** | The sentence **reads complete**. Nothing marks it as partial. Steering reports this is the **same shape as D40** and is owed a Decisions Register row, **not yet written** and parked in its docket |
| **BC-21's provenance** (R25.1) — the owner adopted a described act; he did not independently characterise the payload | **Certain** | The row reads `EXECUTED`, which is what it is; the distinction between a selection and a verbatim account survives it |
| **BC-11b** — obliged and permanently unreachable | **PENDING R27.3.** Named only if D44 did not drop it | Either a real silent residual or a phantom. **Currently unknown which** |

**BC-11b apart, this chain's residuals are not failures — they are rulings and limits, correctly taken.
What they have in common is that none of them can be found by reading the register's terminal column.**
**That is the argument for the residual section existing, and it is why it is assembled before the gate is
under time pressure rather than during it.**

# Rev 28 — BC-11b corrected against this register: `DROPPED — D44`. **And three state changes that no relay carried, caught by re-deriving at write time.**

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529]
returned the R27.3 answer. **No gate is opened here**, and R28.5 explains why the code review's arrival
does not change that.

## R28.1 — Reconcile note, dated: **D44 dropped BC-11b. This register was the stale document.**

**The answer to R27.3's question is YES.** Put as the bare yes/no this stage scoped it to; nothing was
reopened and nobody was asked why. **Devops's four places were right and this register was wrong.**

**BC-11b · `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED` → `DROPPED — D44`** (plan rev 39, `13a6e66`), **2026-08-25.**

**The superseded text is preserved where it was written and is not overwritten.** It read, at R22.2:

> *"BC-11b — `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED` **(never read as closed: it records a permanent absence of
> evidence)**"*

and at R22.4 §4:

> *"**BC-11b `TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED`** (obligation stands, permanently unreachable — real residual
> risk)"*

**Both are superseded as of this note. R22.4 §4's instruction to the successor — to carry BC-11b into the
gate as residual risk — is WITHDRAWN.** **No BC-11b residual is named in the gate**, because there is no
obligation left to be unreachable: its subject was struck and the row fell with it.

**The three grounds this stage recorded against its own document at R27.3 all held**, and the third is the
one worth keeping: **R22.4 §4's instruction was itself the propagation** — a handover section carrying a
superseded state into a successor's hands, which is **the exact shape this session arrived warning about
at read-in and flagged again at R26.5.** **This register caught its own handover doing the thing it was
warning about.** That is not a comfortable sentence and it is the accurate one.

**What made the question answerable at all was not touching what was forbidden.** This stage did not
investigate D44, did not identify the struck subject, and asked nobody why. **A conflict between two
documents' recorded states can be named without reopening the ruling that produced them**, and that
distinction is what let the question be put.

## R28.2 — What this correction does *not* cost, stated because the doctrine is easy to over-correct

**R22.4 §4's underlying doctrine survives intact and is strengthened, not weakened.** The instruction was
wrong about **which rows** it applied to; it was right that **terminal states must not be collapsed**.
BC-11b is the proof rather than the counterexample: **filing a row under the wrong terminal state
propagated a residual that did not exist**, into a handover, into a successor's read-in, and to the edge
of a gate's published residual section. **A collapse would have hidden it; the distinction is what
eventually surfaced it.**

**The live taxonomy after this correction**, which the gate will use:

| State | Obligation | Subject | Residual |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`DROPPED — D44`** — BC-11a, BC-11b, Finding U-1 | gone with its subject | struck from the chain | **None** |
| **`TERMINAL: SUBJECT WITHDRAWN`** — BC-19b | ends | removed from the product | **None** |
| **`WITHDRAWN`**, subject uncharacterised — **BC-5** | ends (owner) | **in the product, never characterised** | **Real, and silent** (R26.4) |
| **`WITHDRAWN`**, plain — BC-15 | ends (owner) | — | — |
| **`EXECUTED`** / **`SUPERSEDED`** | discharged | — | per row |

**`TERMINAL: CANNOT BE EXECUTED` now has no members in this register.** It is not deleted from the
vocabulary — it remains the correct filing for an obligation that stands and is permanently unreachable —
but **no row currently sits in it, and none may be moved there without a ruling.**

## R28.3 — **Three state changes landed that no message carried. Re-derivation at write time caught all three.**

**Rev 26 wrote that this register's open set is only as current as the relays that reach it, and that the
defence is re-derivation at write time from the file plus every ruling known at that moment. One rev
later that caveat paid.** Verified at the documents, not relayed:

| Landed | Commit | Effect here |
|---|---|---|
| **QA Phase 3 — the STALE predictions re-derived by execution, not by file list** | **`f3cbb66`** | **This stage's #1 outstanding item is discharged.** See R28.3.1 |
| **Devops §12 — four deploy records, U-C6 discharged, F-1 raised** | **`14bc31c`** | **U-C6 → `EXECUTED`**; **F-1 routed to qa then uat** |
| **Two owner items from code review round 23** | **`0134e63`** | Noted; steering's queue, not this register's |

**No blame attaches to steering** — it was holding and these are direct commits by their own stages. **The
point is structural and it is the one rev 26 made**: a stage that waits to be told its ledger has moved
will be wrong, and the only defence that works is re-deriving at the moment of writing. **This section is
that defence producing three results in one pass.**

### R28.3.1 — QA Phase 3 discharges the blocker on seven rows. It terminalises none of them.

QA re-derived rev 18's eight `PREDICTION STALE` markings **by executing both trees over the surface**
rather than by reading the commits' file lists — which is what produced the marking in the first place.
Instrument: `verification/013/probe-qa-p3-stale-prediction-differential/`, run unchanged at `2d90e8a`,
`d7c4836` and `4a7126b`, then diffed.

- **BC-1, BC-7, BC-8, BC-21 — staleness LIFTED**; the predictions did not move.
- **BC-6, BC-9, BC-10, BC-18 — RE-DERIVED** to the literal value the surface now produces.
- **BC-9's staleness was real but attributed to the wrong commit** — its cause is D31 at `d7c4836`, not
  the two rev 18 named.
- **BC-18 is decidable today.**
- **The R23.3 query is answered**: BC-21's *"stale by inheritance"* marking was **over-broad and is
  LIFTED**, on two independent grounds, one measured. **It gates nothing** — rev 25 terminalised that row
  on other grounds, exactly as R25.2 said it would.

**What this changes here, precisely:** since rev 18 this register has recorded the operator `PRE` rows as
**blocked behind QA's re-derivation**, because *a row run against stale wording yields a false pass, and a
false pass gets filed rather than investigated.* **That block is lifted.** **BC-1, BC-6, BC-7, BC-8, BC-9,
BC-10 and BC-18 are now executable against sound predictions.**

**What this does not change: not one of them terminalises.** They still require the human walkthrough,
and **row definitions being QA's does not make row state QA's.** The rows move from *"not runnable — the
wording is wrong"* to *"runnable, and awaiting their subject."* **That is a real advance and it is not
progress toward approval; it is the removal of this stage's own stated excuse for their standing open.**

## R28.4 — F-1 is folded into this register rather than left travelling

**This stage's standing rule: UAT consumes the chain's *one* blocking-condition register, and discovering
a second travelling list is a finding — fold it in, record the discovery, never carry a parallel ledger.**

**F-1** (deployment §12.6, routed **qa → uat**): **the serving tree carries 4 of the 8 test blocks of QA's
formally adopted render instrument** (`013-qa-toast-vessel-render.test.ts`, adopted at `664e0c0` as
F-QA23). The routing asks whether the removals are correct **and whether a verdict is owed at `2d90e8a`.**

**Folded in as an open item**, because it is a coverage question that reaches a release decision and it is
routed to this stage. **It is not made a `BC` row**: it has no prediction, no primary instrument and no
subject, and **an item missing those is not executable — writing one for it here would be improvising a
row definition, which is QA's to author, not this stage's.** It stands as an open finding awaiting QA's
ruling and then this stage's.

**Recorded with it, and deliberately not folded in:** **F-2** (`d7c4836` was never a local master
position; pushed from elsewhere), **F-3** (operator/approval for three acts are second-hand, stated as
such), **F-4** (the readiness sibling still fails `check-repo.js` CHECK 4; a name declined rather than
coined, disclosed rather than worked around). **These are devops's own disclosures on devops's own
document, not a parallel blocking register, and this stage does not adopt them.** They are named here so
that the boundary is a recorded judgement rather than an omission.

**Devops's in-round disclosure is noted with approval**: it caught a false *"instrument deleted"* reading
via `git cat-file` **before it reached the record**. That is the instrument-lie class caught by its own
author at the right moment, which is the second time in two passes (BC-13's proven-present `bot/` path was
the first).

## R28.5 — Code review has landed, its anchoring is exemplary, and **it is born expiring. Recorded in advance so nobody assembles a gate from it.**

**Verified at the document, not taken from the relay** (`code-review/013-…-code-review.md`, `5f6fda8`):

- **`APPROVED_WITH_COMMENTS`**, **`Reviewed-At: 2d90e8a` on master**, delta reviewed **`4a7126b..2d90e8a`** — 4 commits, 17 files, 10 non-test.
- **`HEAD == origin/master == 2d90e8a` verified by `git ls-remote` — computed AT THE REMOTE — at pass start `08:24:00Z` AND pass end `08:33:42Z`**, with the working tree **proven blob-identical to the SHA path-by-path.**
- **D35 re-derived as bounded to D30–D34**, so `2d90e8a` is **inside** the review debt and **not waived** — reached independently and agreeing with this register.

**That anchoring is the standard R24.2 asked for, and it is worth naming because it demonstrates R24.2's
converse.** R24.2's warning was that *a verdict without a named identity cannot expire, which is what makes
it dangerous rather than merely incomplete.* **Here is the other half: a verdict that names its identity
this rigorously lets its expiry be seen coming — before it happens, rather than discovered afterwards.**

**And it is coming.** The owner accepted the review's **MEDIUM-1** (*D32 readable errors defeated on a
reachable path*) as a **rider on 013**. **That is production code.** So:

> **The code review verdict at `2d90e8a` will cease to bind the moment the rider lands, and it is recorded
> here as expiring before anyone can act on it.** QA's verify pass is stopped for the same reason.
> **The gate is written against the post-rider tip, and its predecessor checks are re-run there — not
> inherited from this round.**

**This is R24.2 with a concrete instance, and the instance is sharper than the rule.** R24.2 warned that
`code-reviewer` approved + `qa` Complete + `UAT Complete` would arrive looking like release. **It is now
arriving, and the first of the three is already known to be expiring.** A reader who finds
`APPROVED_WITH_COMMENTS` in that document and stops reading will have found a verdict about a tree that no
longer exists. **Recording it in advance is the only defence that works, because after the rider lands
nothing in that document will announce that it went stale.**

# Rev 29 — A blocking release condition that travelled here by name is lifted. **Recorded with its result, because that is this stage's first responsibility.** And BC-6 is not executable yet, for a reason QA disclosed and framed as retrospective.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529]
relayed QA's `f5f3264`. **Read at QA's document, not taken from the relay.** **No gate is opened here.**

## R29.1 — The blocking-condition register: both P3.11 items, with results

**This stage's first responsibility is that every item QA marked `BLOCKING RELEASE CONDITION` is
recorded here with its result, and that none is silently omitted.** P3.11 carried two, **addressed to
this document by name**. Both are recorded below whether or not they still bind.

**QA framed them correctly and it is worth naming:** they were written *"as additions to chain 013's
single register's standing obligations rather than as new IDs."* **That is the one-register rule
honoured by the stage that had the most convenient reason to break it** — a new ID would have been
easier to write and would have created the parallel ledger this stage is required to refuse.

| Blocking condition, as QA wrote it | State | Date |
|---|---|---|
| **BC-1 must not be executed until `load-confirmation-names-wrong-mode` (F-QA31) is fixed** — *the dialog will tell him the message type changes when it does not, or stay silent when it does, immediately before an irreversible overwrite* | **STANDS — unaffected.** In the rider round | — |
| **BC-6 cannot pass while `emptied-composite-drop-unnamed` (F-QA32) stands** — *a Container emptied by the filter is deleted from the user's message with nothing shown anywhere, beside a green state* | **`LIFTED — QA RULING (NEVER APPLIED)`** | **2026-08-25** |

**Verified at QA's document at this rev's write time**: the original stands at **P3.11 (L2885)** and the
withdrawal at **L3231–3244**, **side by side, neither edited out of the other.** QA's stated ground for
that arrangement is one this register endorses without reservation: **a blocking condition that quietly
disappears is worse than one that was never raised.**

## R29.2 — `LIFTED` is a deliberately different word from `WITHDRAWN`, and two collapses are refused here

**This register's `WITHDRAWN` is owner-only — an obligation the owner ended.** QA's act is not that, and
filing it under the same word would corrupt the one terminal state whose whole content is *who ruled it*.
**So the constraint is filed `LIFTED — QA RULING (NEVER APPLIED)`, and the vocabulary now separates them.**
This is R28.2's doctrine applied **prospectively** rather than after a propagation: the cheapest error
available here was to write "withdrawn" twice and mean two different things.

**Two further collapses, refused:**

- **BC-6 is not withdrawn. BC-6 stands as written, open and `NOT EXECUTED`.** What lifted is a
  **constraint on its executability**, not the row. A reader who takes "the BC-6 blocking condition was
  withdrawn" as "BC-6 is closed" has lost the entire content of the sentence.
- **It was not *fixed*. It *never applied*.** QA's first two grounds are **definitional** — they establish
  the finding was never within BC-6's scope, not that a defect was repaired. **The distinction is load-
  bearing for this register**: a fixed blocker implies a code change happened and would put a commit in
  the chain; a never-applied blocker implies the row's scope was misread and puts nothing there. **Only
  the second is true, and the gate must not imply the first.**

## R29.3 — Weighing QA's three grounds, and which one this stage rests on

**This stage does not read source and does not re-run tests.** It can therefore weigh a ground only
insofar as the ground is mechanical — which is exactly the property QA identified in the one it said it
would defend hardest.

1. **BC-6's condition and predicted-value cells are both about *types*; an emptied Container is an
   instance removal, and Container is a supported type.** **Mechanical, and this stage accepts it.** No
   type was dropped, so the row's own predicate is not engaged.
2. **The other reading would require the notice to lie.** The surface renders `Not supported on
   Hooks.gg: ${types}`; naming an emptied Container asserts *"Container is not supported on Hooks.gg"*,
   **which is false**. **This is the ground this stage rests on**, and QA was right about why: **it is
   mechanical, and it is independent of what the owner ruled.** **A register row cannot require a
   surface to make a false statement** — a row that did would be unsatisfiable by any correct
   implementation, which makes it a defective row rather than a failing product.
3. **The filter already satisfies BC-6 across its class, by QA's own control.** **Noted and not relied
   on.** It is a test result from the ruling party's own instrument; it corroborates and does not
   establish. **Grounds 1 and 2 carry this alone, and would carry it if ground 3 were withdrawn tomorrow.**

**Accepted. The constraint is lifted, and nothing about it returns to the owner** — he ruled on the
filter's behaviour; QA then ruled on its own row's scope, which is QA's to rule because **row definitions
are QA's and this register has said so since R22.4 §2.**

## R29.4 — **BC-6 is still not executable, and the reason is the defect QA disclosed against itself**

**QA disclosed, unprompted, that BC-6's failure-mode cell over-reaches its condition cell:** condition
*"the dropped types"*; failure mode *"a component vanishes with no trace"* — **strictly wider** — and it
named that over-reach as what made the withdrawn finding feel supported.

**QA framed this as retrospective material. This stage's ruling is that it is live, and it blocks BC-6's
execution until it is reconciled.** Stated as a disagreement rather than an amendment, because QA owns
the row:

> **The over-reach is not a historical explanation of a past error. It is still in the row the operator
> will read at the walkthrough.** QA ruled on the **condition** cell. **The failure-mode cell was not
> reconciled to it and still says something wider.** An operator sat in front of BC-6 who executes
> against the failure-mode cell can observe an emptied Container vanishing with no trace, and record a
> **FAIL** — **on a row QA has just ruled the code satisfies.**

**That is a false failure, manufactured by the row's own internal disagreement**, and this register has
spent four revisions on the principle that produced it: *a row run against wording that does not match
its predicate yields a result that gets filed rather than investigated.* **Rev 18 marked eight rows stale
to prevent exactly this; QA Phase 3 cleared all eight by execution. This is the same hazard arriving by a
different door — not staleness, but two cells of one row specifying two different predicates.**

**And it is worse than a missing cell, not better.** This stage's standing rule is that **an item missing
its prediction or subject is not yet executable and is routed back to QA rather than improvised.** A row
with **two predicates** is executable — **to either answer** — which is the failure mode that does not
announce itself.

**Routed to QA. Not improvised here**, because reconciling the cells is authoring a row definition and
that is QA's, not this stage's. **The ask is one line: make BC-6's failure-mode cell say what its
condition cell says.** **BC-6 stays open and is marked NOT EXECUTABLE pending that reconciliation** — a
constraint of this register's own raising, and the first one in this chain that did not come from QA.

## R29.5 — For the gate, and for whoever reads it first: **an absence that is the correct state**

QA states — and this register carries it forward, because **the gate is what a later reader opens
first** — that **`emptied-composite-drop-unnamed`'s absence from the fixed tree is the EXPECTED state.**
**A reader must not read that absence as a missed fix.** It is in QA's carry-forward and the planner is
being told to put it in the plan; it is recorded here for the same reason.

**This is worth more than a pass-through note, because it inverts this chain's own doctrine and the
inversion is easy to miss.** This register's standing rule on silent failure modes is that **absence is
what an attestation is made of, so quiet is no evidence** — the guard against reading nothing-happened as
nothing-wrong. **Here the absence is correct, and the risk runs the other way**: a later reader, primed by
that very doctrine, finds nothing where a defect was described and reads the nothing as an **omission**.

**Both readings of an absence are available, and neither is safe by default.** What settles it is never
the absence — it is the record saying which one was expected. **That is what this section is, and it is
why it belongs in the gate rather than only in QA's carry-forward.**

## R29.6 — The renumbering: this register's own citations checked rather than assumed

QA disclosed, unprompted, that it numbered its Phase 3 findings from the last ID it could see rather than
from the true high-water mark, and **collided with two of its own closed findings** — the defect its
register carries a note warning against. Resolved on that register's precedent: **older keeps the number,
newer renumbers, and every row now carries a stable name.**

| Superseded ID | **Correct ID** | **Stable name — cite this** | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| F-QA29 | **F-QA31** | **`load-confirmation-names-wrong-mode`** | OPEN, in the rider round; **blocks BC-1** |
| F-QA30 | **F-QA32** | **`emptied-composite-drop-unnamed`** | **WITHDRAWN, pulled from the round** |
| F-QA31 | **F-QA33** | **`disclosure-pointer-dangles`** | OPEN, in the rider round |

**This register's own exposure, measured rather than assumed:** every `F-QA` identifier appearing anywhere
in this document is **F-QA11, F-QA14, F-QA15, F-QA21, F-QA22, F-QA23, F-QA26**. **None falls in the
collided range. No citation in this document needs correcting.** *Method: enumeration written to a file
and counted on the file, not as a predicate over filtered stream output.*

**Recorded even though the answer was "nothing to do", because "I checked and none applied" and "I did not
check" are indistinguishable in a record that omits the check** — which is this chain's most-repeated
lesson and the one rev 21 was corrected for.

**Carry the names, not the numbers, from here on.**

## R29.7 — The tree moved to a chain branch. Re-derived, and it changes nothing anchored

**Verified at this rev's write time, not inherited:** the working tree is on **`chain/013-rider-2` at
`2d90e8a`**; **`master` = `2d90e8a`**; **`origin/master` = `2d90e8a`** (computed at the remote);

**Its reason is sound and this stage records agreement:** the release had collapsed `master` and the chain
branch, **which silently converts the implementer's standing commit grant into permission to commit onto
the branch serving production.** A grant whose scope changes because two refs happen to coincide is a
grant nobody authored. **Checking out the chain branch is the steering thread's own step**, so this is
ownership exercised, not a tree switched from under anyone.

**Nothing anchored moves.** Every verdict in this chain is anchored to a **commit**, and `2d90e8a` is the
same object under either ref. **The code review verdict is unaffected by the switch — it remains affected
by the rider, which is a different thing and is recorded at R28.5.**

**One consequence carried forward to the gate:** the gate will be written at the **post-rider tip on the
chain branch**, and it names **that commit**. **The release act — the merge to `master` — is a separate
step with a separate owner**, and an approval at a chain-branch commit is not an approval of a merge that
has not happened. **The gate will say so in terms.**

# Rev 30 — The ruling QA asked for. **Neither column is the answer: the checklist is decided by the row's subject.** Four rows to reconcile, one cleared, one that is not a row.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529]
relayed QA's `0b648e5` and its request for a ruling. **Read at QA's document (L3296–3345), not taken
from the relay.** **No gate is opened here.**

## R30.1 — BC-6 discharged, and BC-1 is the mirror QA found by sweeping the class rather than the instance

**BC-6's cells are reconciled.** Verified at QA's document: the failure-mode cell now speaks only of an
unsupported **type**, **with the emptied-composite case named as not a member so a later reader cannot
re-widen it by inference.** No condition, predicted value, subject, instrument or state changed — only
what the operator is told to watch for, which is the whole and correct scope of the fix.

**This register's rev-29 constraint is discharged: BC-6 → EXECUTABLE, 2026-08-25.**

**And QA did the thing that was worth more than the fix.** It swept on **the class predicate** — *two
cells of one row specifying two different predicates* — **rather than on the instance it had been handed**,
and found a second member. **A sweep on the instance would have found nothing and reported the row fixed.**

**BC-1 diverges in the opposite direction, and the opposite direction is the worse one:**

| Row | Divergence | Direction | Manufactures |
|---|---|---|---|
| **BC-6** | cell described an **instance** removal; condition is about a dropped **type** — a scenario the condition does not cover at all | **wider** | a false **FAILURE** |
| **BC-1** | condition has **two clauses** — which types change, **and whether the message type changes**; the cell spoke only to component loss | **narrower** | a false **PASS** |

**A false failure gets investigated. A false pass gets filed.** This register has said so since rev 23,
and **BC-1 is where that principle stops being a maxim.** F-QA31 `load-confirmation-names-wrong-mode` is
*entirely* about the mode clause. **BC-1 was declared blocked on that defect while its own failure-mode
cell described a symptom the defect does not produce.** An operator handed BC-1 and reading that cell for
what to watch **would have watched for lost components, seen none, and recorded PASS over the exact defect
the row was blocked on** — on the row carrying this chain's one live blocking condition.

**Its cell now carries both clauses, each independently sufficient to fail the row, and names the finding.
BC-1's blocking condition is unchanged and still stands** — what changed is that the row can now express
the thing it is blocked on.

**QA named its own reasoning failure and it generalises**: *"having found the cause of my own withdrawn
finding, I stopped looking at the row."* **A resolved question closes the file on the object it was
about.** That is not an oversight and it is not carelessness — it is what resolution *feels* like, which
is why it needs a rule rather than more care. **Recorded for the retrospective as this stage's own
material too**, because this register has done it: rev 21 verified a strike against the predicate it was
handed and stopped.

## R30.2 — **The ruling. QA asked which of two columns is the checklist. Neither answer is correct, because the question has a hidden variable.**

QA's question, stated fairly: *"I am assuming the operator's checklist is the Predicted-value column, not
the failure-mode column. If that is wrong — if rows are executed from the failure-mode cell — those six
become the same defect and I will reconcile all six on request."*

**The answer is not one column. It is: whichever column the row's *subject* can act on — and that is
settled row by row by the subject, not once for the register.**

**This stage's standing rule already contains it:** *every blocking item carries a prediction, a primary
instrument, and a subject — read them apart; the owner comes from the primary, never the fallback, and a
row whose primary needs no human belongs to a stage from the moment it is written.* **Applied here:**

- **Where the primary instrument requires a human subject, the failure-mode cell IS the checklist.** It is
  the human-legible cell and that is its function. **The subject of this chain's `PRE` rows is the owner
  at a walkthrough — not a QA engineer working a ten-cell table methodically.**
- **Where the primary needs no human, the failure-mode cell is documentation, not a checklist.** Nothing
  an operator reads governs a row a probe settles.

**The decisive argument is internal to QA's own two rulings in this same pass**, and it is why this is a
denial rather than a preference:

> **QA corrected BC-1 *because* an operator reading its failure-mode cell for what to watch would have
> recorded PASS.** That correction is only necessary if the failure-mode cell is what the operator reads.
> **If the Predicted-value column were the checklist, BC-1's narrow cell would have been harmless — by
> exactly the argument QA is making for the six.** The two rulings cannot both stand as stated. **The
> subject test is what reconciles them**, and it upholds QA's assumption precisely where the subject is
> not human.

**So the assumption is DENIED for human-subject rows and UPHELD for machine-subject rows.** QA's
*under-illustrate* / *misdirect* distinction is real but is not the operative one: **an under-illustrating
cell and a misdirecting cell both let an operator watch the wrong thing, see nothing, and pass.** They
differ in probability, not in kind — and **which omitted limb turns out to matter is knowable only after
the failure.** QA knew BC-1's omitted clause mattered because a finding named it. **For the rest, "no
finding currently points at the omitted limb" is not evidence the limb is safe** — this register's own
silent-mode doctrine, applied to itself.

**The first thing this ruling implied was worse than the ruling**: BC-16 stands **terminal `EXECUTED`** in

stage's rule is exact about that: *"satisfied incidentally" is not a terminal state — record it as
`EXECUTED` with the evidence, or it was never a gate.*

by a probe with 25 assertions and two killed mutants at `164d720`. **Its evidence is instrumental, not
observational, so no cell an operator reads could have narrowed it.** **The terminal state stands
undisturbed.**

it cleared"* and *"I did not check"* are indistinguishable in a record that omits the check.**

BC-14 pending its subject test — are marked `NOT EXECUTABLE` pending reconciliation**, on the same ground
and with the same one-line remedy.

**Marking BC-18 is the uncomfortable one and is therefore the test of the rule.** It is the sole live
condition of the standing decision and QA has just made it decidable today; blocking it is the opposite
of momentum. **It is marked anyway** — a row that can least afford a false pass is the last row to
exempt, and **a standard applied to every row but the urgent one is not a standard.**

## R30.5 — QA's counting defect, and the remedy it shares with this register's own

QA disclosed, against itself, that its sweep section first read *"21 ten-cell rows, 19 live"* — **both
wrong; 26 and 23** — because it **counted rows by eye off a printed listing instead of computing the
partition.** Caught before commit by re-running it as a computation.

**Its framing is exactly right and is recorded in its words**: *"the listing was correct and complete —
the instrument did not lie — and I still got the number wrong, because I read it instead of computing
it."* **The cheaper cousin of this chain's recurring class: a reader that cannot count a member, rather
than a predicate that cannot see one.** **Eighth sighting.**

**Paired with this register's own defect two revisions ago, because the pair names the remedy.** Rev 28
dropped rev 27's Status-Detail marker; the diffstat read `267 insertions, 1 deletion`, which is exactly
what a legitimate line-split looks like. **It was caught by counting the rev headers and getting 3 where
4 were expected.** **Both defects were invisible to reading and both fell to computing** — one a count
read off a listing, one a diff read off a plausible summary. **The rule is not "look harder": it is that a
claim about a quantity is checked by computing the quantity, and a diffstat that looks right is not a
check.**

# Rev 31 — Six reconciled, not four. **Executability lifted on five rows; eleven of twelve are now executable.** And a defect in this register's own rev-30 ruling, found by QA's re-derivation.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529]
relayed QA's `bef6968`. **Read at QA's document (L643–647, L3449–3532), not taken from the relay** — and
R31.3 is why that mattered in this pass specifically. **No gate is opened here.**

## R31.1 — **A defect in rev 30, and it is this register's, not QA's**

**QA reconciled six rows. This register had ruled on four. The two extra — BC-4 and BC-7 — were rows QA
had classified *aligned* under the weaker test, and they exist only because QA re-derived the enumeration
from scratch instead of reusing the list it was handed.** Its stated reason is exactly right and is
recorded here as its own: reusing that list *"would have been a sweep on the instances rather than on the
predicate — the exact error whose correction produced BC-1 two sittings ago."*

**Rev 30's ruling changed the standard. This register then tested the old classification in one direction
only.**

It asked whether the set of six was too **large** — and removed two, correctly (BC-11b is not a row;
BC-16's primary needs no human). **It never asked whether the set was too small.** But a set produced
under a superseded test is unreliable **in both directions**, and the direction this register failed to
check is the dangerous one: **a row wrongly dropped from the set stays in the ledger marked *executable*
while its cell names one limb of three.**

**Which is precisely what happened. BC-4 and BC-7 stood in this register as EXECUTABLE through revs 29
and 30, each with a failure-mode cell naming 1 limb of 3.** Had the walkthrough run in that window, **an
operator would have been handed two rows this register had certified executable and QA had certified
aligned, and both were neither.**

**The general form, recorded because it outlives this instance:** **when a ruling changes the standard,
every classification made under the old standard is void — not narrowed.** Auditing the set you were
handed tests only the errors that produce false members. **The errors that produce missing members are
invisible to that audit, and they are the ones that leave rows certified safe.** Re-derivation is the
only instrument that finds both, and QA reached for it while this register audited a list.

**This is the same lesson QA recorded against itself one sitting ago** — *"having found the cause of my
own withdrawn finding, I stopped looking at the row"* — **arriving at this stage by a different route.
Rev 30 found the cause and then audited rather than re-derived.**

## R31.2 — The six, and what QA did that this register did not ask for

| Row | Limbs in `Predicted value` | Named in the old cell | |
|---|---|---|---|
| **BC-4** | dialog stays · error shown · nothing created | **1 of 3** | **not in this register's four** |
| **BC-7** | V2 · bit set · warning | **1 of 3** | **not in this register's four** |
| **BC-8** | Classic · content intact · bit cleared · warning · no V2 error | 1 of 5 | |
| **BC-9** | two options · each names its drop · nothing created first · dismissal non-destructive · both outcomes | 1 of 5 | |
| **BC-14** | four observations | 2 of 4 | **subject test done: primary *operator observation in production*, subject *the owner, in normal use*** — human |
| **BC-18** | appears · persists · clears | 1 of 3 | **done first, per rev 30** |

**Not reconciled, each with its reason recorded rather than left as an absence:** BC-11b is not a row;
**BC-16 and BC-13 have machine primaries** — BC-16 independently confirming this register's R30.3.1
clearing; **BC-5 and BC-15 are owner-`WITHDRAWN`**, with QA recording that **BC-15 would otherwise have
qualified**, so that omission reads as a decision. **That is the correct treatment of a row excluded by a
ruling rather than by its properties**, and it is the same discipline this register applied to BC-11b.

### R31.2.1 — The exclusion clauses, which QA added unprompted and which prevent the defect the widening would have caused

**BC-18's and BC-9's reconciled cells now state what is *not* a failure** — naming the D31 row-E resting
state, where a correction traversing both V2 components and classic content replaces the warning with the
conflict notice and its **Resolve** link.

**QA's reason is the one this register would have had to raise if QA had not**: without that clause, **the
wider cells it had just written would manufacture the exact false failure the re-derivation exists to
prevent.** An operator newly told to watch for *"it appears and never clears"* would see the notice
**change** rather than disappear, and record a **FAIL**.

**The principle, worth stating once because it will recur:** **widening a failure-mode cell creates
false-failure risk in proportion to the widening.** A cell corrected from one limb to three does not
become safer by naming more — it becomes safer only if it also names what the new limbs do **not** cover.
**A widening without its exclusions trades a false pass for a false failure and calls it a fix.**

## R31.3 — **BC-7: the relay and the document said different things, and the document is why this row is not blocked**

**Steering's summary — accurately conveying QA's flag — was that BC-7's bit-set limb is *invisible on
screen*, that an operator sees the warning and the container, and that *"the flag can be wrong with
nothing to show it."*** **On that framing this register would have marked BC-7 `NOT EXECUTABLE`**: a limb
whose signature is absence cannot be settled by a human observation, and a PASS recorded over it is a
false pass of the exact kind this chain's silent-mode doctrine exists to refuse.

**The cell as written says something materially different.** Verified at QA's document, **L647**:

> *"Three things, and one of them is **invisible without opening the JSON**… **The flag bit is not set** —
> everything looks right on screen and the message is wrong on the wire, and **only the Message JSON shows
> it**."*

**The limb is not unobservable. It is observable by a named act that is not looking at the screen** — and
the reconciled cell names that act. **So the cell reconciliation already solved it, and BC-7 is
executable.**

**This is recorded at length because it is the clearest instance this chain has produced of why this
stage verifies at the document rather than ruling from a relay.** The relay was **directionally right** —
BC-7 does carry a limb invisible on screen, and flagging it was correct — **and the remedy was already in
the artifact.** A ruling made on the summary would have blocked a row that QA had already fixed, and
would have been defensible on every word it was given.

**BC-7 is EXECUTABLE, with one recording constraint that is this register's to impose:**

> **BC-7's bit-set limb is settled by opening the Message JSON. It is never settled by the screen looking
> right.** A result recorded for BC-7 states that the JSON was opened; **"everything looked correct" is
> not an observation of this limb and must not be filed as one.** The doctrine applies with full force —
> not to whether the row can run, but to **what may be recorded as having been seen.**

## R31.4 — QA's backward sweep: accepted, with one question it did not answer and one residual it enlarges

**Accepted.** QA extended this register's R30.3.1 check to every **terminal** row with a **human** primary
— the only place a false pass could already be filed — and reports **no backward risk**:

- **BC-2, BC-3** — all limbs named in the cell. **Clear on the cell.**
- **BC-19a** — *appears* named, *clears* not; **clears on this register's own rev-19.2 reasoning**: *"the
  whole row is terminalised, not one limb — he attested against the row by number."* **The attestation
  route never passed through a cell, so no cell could have narrowed it.**

**That reasoning is sound for the risk under examination, and the two clearings must not be collapsed:**
BC-2 and BC-3 clear because their **cells were complete**; BC-19a clears because **no cell was in the
path at all.** Different grounds, different durability.

**One question QA's sweep does not reach, and this register names rather than assumes:** the sweep tested
**cell completeness**. It did not test **whether a terminal row's recorded observation actually covered a
limb that is invisible on screen.** BC-7 has just demonstrated that this chain contains such limbs, and
**BC-3's own cell describes one** — *"the code path has no dialog, so a silent drop here is invisible
until the message is sent wrong."*

> **Routed to QA, bounded, and disturbing nothing unless answered adversely: for BC-2, BC-3 and BC-19a,
> did the recorded observation cover every limb that is invisible on screen, or was any limb taken from
> appearance?** **A complete cell guarantees the operator was *told* what to watch; it does not establish
> that a limb requiring a separate act was actually observed.** These terminal states stand; this is a
> question about their evidence, not a challenge to their status.

**And BC-19a's clearing enlarges a residual this register already carries.** R25.1 recorded that BC-21
rests on the owner **adopting a described act** rather than characterising it himself. **BC-19a's
by-number attestation is the same class**: it immunises the row against cell-narrowing precisely because
**it does not tell us which limbs he observed.** The property that clears it is the property that makes
it opaque.

**So the gate's third residual is promoted from an instance to a class:** *attestation by reference rather
than by characterisation* — **members: BC-21, BC-19a.** **Neither row's state moves**; both are correctly
terminal. **What changes is that the gate names the class**, because a residual carried as a one-off
invites the reader to treat it as a quirk of one row.

## R31.5 — QA's third instrument note, which strengthens this register's own rule

QA disclosed, against itself, that its partition's fourth bucket **was first written 9 against a list of
ten names** — 21 against 22 in scope — **and that the discrepancy is what located it, not re-reading the
names.** Its framing: *"Last sitting I read a listing instead of computing it; this sitting I counted a
list I had written myself. Same defect, one step further in."* **Ninth sighting.**

**The rule it has adopted generalises rev 30's, and this register adopts it too:**

> **A partition is published only after its buckets are checked against the enumeration by a program.
> The sum matching is not the check, because a sum can match with two errors in it.**

**That is strictly stronger than what rev 30 said.** Rev 30's rule was *compute the quantity rather than
read it*. **QA's addition is that computing the total is still not enough** — two compensating errors pass
a total check and leave both members misfiled. **The check has to be per-bucket against the enumeration.**
**This register's own open-set enumerations have been stated per-class with a summed line; from here the
sum is presentation and the per-class derivation is the check.**

| **BC-14** | `NOT EXECUTABLE` — subject test owed | **EXECUTABLE — constraint DISCHARGED** | Subject tested by QA: **human** (*the owner, in normal use*). Cell reconciled, 2 of 4 → 4 of 4 |

# Rev 32 — R31.4's bounded question is **WITHDRAWN, answered by derivation**. Nothing goes to the owner and nothing to QA. **And the third defect in this run's series, which is again this register's.**

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] pointed
at plan 013's **D41** rather than answering, on this register's own rule that *a question answerable by
derivation is not escalated.* **Derivation performed at the plan (D40, D41) and at this document's own
revs 19 and 20.** **No gate is opened here. No row moves.**

## R32.1 — The question is withdrawn. All three rows are answered on the record

R31.4 asked, of **BC-2, BC-3 and BC-19a**: *did the recorded observation cover every limb that is
invisible on screen, or was any limb taken from appearance?*

| Row | Answer, derived | Escalation |
|---|---|---|
| **BC-2** | **Directly attested, in his own words.** D41 records his per-row verbatim: ***"The notice showed. I've removed it."*** — an explicit observation of the notice, not an inference from an action. The ingress was separately established by a second prompt whose text D41 quotes verbatim (*"For the notice you saw — where did the message come from?"*). **Rev 19.4.1's revert trigger was checked at rev 20 and not met** | **None** |
| **BC-3** | **Already ruled by this register, at rev 20**: ***"BC-3 re-based on D40's attestation on the identical code path rather than on D41's inferred limb."*** The inference D41 explicitly disclaimed and routed here — *"attested for BC-2 and inferred for BC-3"* — **is not what BC-3 rests on, and has not been since rev 20** | **None** |
| **BC-19a** | **D41 states in terms that it does not reach BC-19a**, whose disposition is separately routed under **D40**. D40 carries his verbatim ***"BC-19a - Done."*** — a by-number attestation, which is exactly the residual class R31.4 named and R31.7 carries | **None.** The class is already recorded; there is no new question |

**So: nothing to the owner, and nothing to QA.** **Steering's judgement not to ask him was correct**, and its
reason was this register's own: a question about what an old observation covered is close enough to asking
him to produce something that it is worth one derivation first.

## R32.2 — **The defect: this register routed a question its own document had already answered**

**Rev 20 decided BC-3's basis. Rev 31 asked the question anyway, without checking rev 20.**

**That is the third defect in this run's series and all three share one shape:**

| Rev | Defect | What was not checked |
|---|---|---|
| **28** | Dropped rev 27's Status-Detail marker | The diffstat *looked* right; the count was not taken |
| **30** | Tested a superseded classification in one direction only | Whether the standard's change made the set too **small** |
| **31** | Routed to QA a question this document had already answered | **This document** |

**The shape is: acting on the artifact in hand without interrogating the artifact already held.** Each was
caught, and none by care — by a count, by a peer's re-derivation, and here by a pointer from steering.

**And the generalisable half is a gap in this register's own rule.** *A question answerable by derivation
is not escalated* — this stage applied that rigorously **upward**, declining twice to put the anchor-scope
question to the owner. **It did not apply it laterally.** R31.4 routed a sweep to QA without deriving
first.

> **The asymmetry has a cause worth naming: escalating upward feels expensive, so it gets a test.
> Routing sideways feels free, so it does not.** It is not free — it spends a peer's pass and can
> commission a sweep the record could have settled. **The rule is one rule: derive before routing,
> in any direction.**

**Recorded, and this register's share of it is not reduced by the fact that steering caught it kindly.**

## R32.3 — R31.3's attribution, refined by a dated note. **The superseded text stands.**

**R31.3 recorded that "the relay and the document said different things" and drew the lesson that this
stage verifies at the document rather than ruling from a relay. That finding stands and is unchanged.**
**Its attribution needs one correction, and steering disclosed it unprompted.**

**The phrase — *"the flag can be wrong with nothing to show it"* — is QA's own, verbatim, from QA's message
to steering.** Steering relayed it faithfully and without checking it against QA's document. **So the
overstatement originated with the author of the document it overstated.**

**That makes the lesson stronger than R31.3 stated it, not weaker:**

> **Verifying at the document protects against the author's own summary, not only against an
> intermediary's.** A stage's prose about its own artifact is not evidence of what the artifact says.
> **R31.3 read the failure as a relay-chain hazard; it is a prose-versus-artifact hazard, and the relay
> chain merely transmitted it.**

**This is plan 013's D42 instantiated, one day after it was written, by a second stage.** D42 ruled that
steering's *"disciplines hold rigorously where it writes register rows and fail where it writes prose"*,
and stated **explicitly that this generalises to every stage that writes prose alongside a structured
artifact** — carried to the retrospective as a finding in its own right rather than as an anecdote about
one coordinator. **QA's message overstating QA's document is a second member of that class, and this
register's own Status-Detail blocks are prose alongside a structured artifact and are not exempt.**

**Apportioned plainly, because a diffuse finding teaches nothing:** QA's document was **correct**; QA's
prose overstated it; steering passed the prose as a finding, which its own standing rule forbids, and has
said so and is correcting it with the owner. **This stage caught it at the last point at which it could
be caught.** **That the system worked at the last step is not evidence the earlier steps are sound — it is
the reason the last step exists.**

## R32.4 — Steering's observation on rev 31's rule, recorded because a second instance is what makes a rule

Steering reports that *"when a ruling changes the standard, every classification made under the old
standard is void — not narrowed"* reached **the rider's scope** on the same day: **MEDIUM-1 was accepted
under one cost, QA changed the standard by finding three sites, and steering re-put the whole scope rather
than asking whether the approved item had grown** — which it describes as having worked *"by luck of
framing, not by rule."*

**Recorded, and the recording is the point.** The rule was derived here from a defect in this register's
own classification. **A second, independent instance in a different stage on a different object within
hours is what distinguishes a rule from a post-hoc description of one mistake.** It goes to the
retrospective as a rule with two members, not as this stage's apology.

# Rev 33 — Predicted-value corrections **ACCEPTED**. BC-19a's disposition **ENDORSED**, and its residual is now specific and permanently unresolvable. **The stop condition is AGREED, with three conditions.**

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] relayed
QA's `85634d3`, **leading with the fact that this pass changes Predicted-value cells** — which QA asked be
surfaced rather than buried, and which is the right way to bring it. **Read at QA's document (L3608–3632,
L3480), not taken from the relay. No gate is opened here.**

## R33.1 — The Predicted-value corrections are ACCEPTED, and the third pair was the worst of the three

**QA's argument is right and this register accepts it in full:** the **Condition** cell is authoritative
and already required every limb added, so **nothing new is asked of the product**. Correcting a Predicted
value to match its Condition is not widening a row — **it is closing a gap between what the row requires
and what the row can record.**

**And this pair was more dangerous than the two before it, which is worth stating because the sequence
reads as diminishing returns and is not.**

| Pair | Gap between | Consequence of the gap |
|---|---|---|
| condition ↔ failure mode | what is required · **what the operator is told to watch** | the operator watches the wrong thing |
| **condition ↔ predicted value** | what is required · **what the observation is recorded beside** | **the row is recorded satisfied while a required limb was never observed** |

**The second is the worse failure and it is one step closer to the record.** A misdirected operator may
still notice the right thing; **a row whose prediction omits a limb produces a clean, well-formed PASS
that is silent about it.** **BC-7 named 2 of 3, BC-8 3 of 5, BC-9 2 of 4** — and every pass in this chain,
this register's included, read past them because **nobody asked that question of that pair.**

**The widening rule is satisfied.** QA applied it to itself unprompted: each addition is a limb the
condition already names, so no new observation is created, **and the BC-7 and BC-8 additions carry their
instrument with them** — which is the exclusion that stops *"the screen looked fine"* being filed against
a limb the screen cannot show.

**And the origin is worth recording plainly: this pair was found by applying rev 31's generalisation to
rev 30's ruling.** Rev 30 ruled the standard; rev 31 generalised *when a ruling changes the standard, every
classification made under it is void*. **Neither pass applied either to the pair that mattered most.** The
rule found what its authors did not.

## R33.2 — BC-19a: QA's disposition is ENDORSED on this register's own doctrine, **and the residual it leaves is now specific and permanently unresolvable**

**QA found BC-19a's Predicted value naming 1 limb of 2 (*appears*, not *clears*), recorded it
found-and-not-corrected with the reason, and did not edit it.** Its ground: *"editing a terminal row's
prediction after the fact would change what the record says was predicted at the time it was observed."*

**That is exactly this register's own rule and the endorsement is unqualified.** R25.3: *"the observed
result is recorded beside the prediction, not over it."* **A terminal row's prediction is evidence, not
documentation** — it is the thing the observation was recorded against, and editing it retroactively
changes what was predicted. **QA reached this independently and applied it against its own sweep's
momentum, which is the harder direction.** Recording it in the table rather than omitting it is what makes
the omission a decision.

**But the finding compounds with R32.1, and this register must say so rather than let two clearings sit
adjacent and look like one.**

R31.4 cleared BC-19a's **failure-mode** gap on rev 19.2's reasoning — *he attested against the row by
number, so no cell was in the path.* **That reasoning assumed the attestation bypassed cells. It is now
established that BOTH human-facing cells named one limb of two.** So if he formed *"BC-19a - Done"* by
reading the row — and there is nothing else he could have read — **what was available to read displayed
*appears* and was silent on *clears*, in both columns.**

**R32.1 did not positively clear BC-19a; it recorded that the by-number attestation was already carried as
a residual and that no new question arose. That remains true. What changes is the residual's content:**

> **BC-19a's *clears* limb has no positive evidence from any route.** The by-number attestation does not
> name it; both human-facing cells were silent on it; **and its subject was deleted by the owner at
> `2d90e8a`, so no observation can now be obtained.**

**BC-19a stays TERMINAL `EXECUTED`, and reverting it is refused on a specific ground rather than on
reluctance:** **its subject is gone.** Reopening it would convert a terminal row into an obligation that
is permanently unreachable — **manufacturing exactly the state this register spent rev 28 clearing as a
phantom.** BC-19b already stands `TERMINAL: SUBJECT WITHDRAWN` for the same subject's removal.

**Nothing goes to the owner.** Asking him to observe a deleted notice is impossible, and asking him to
re-characterise an old attestation is what this register's BC-21 doctrine forbids outright. **This is a
residual to name in the gate, not a question to ask.**

**Residual inventory, now four — three general and one specific, all silent:**

| # | Residual | Silent because |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **BC-5's subject** — in the product, never characterised; obligation ended by owner ruling | `WITHDRAWN` is a closed shape |
| 2 | **The plan's half-sentence** — describes half the shipped change by owner ruling | The sentence reads complete |
| 3 | **Attestation by reference rather than characterisation** — class; members **BC-21**, **BC-19a** | The row reads `EXECUTED`, which is what it is |
| 4 | **BC-19a's *clears* limb — no positive evidence from any route, and none now obtainable** | Terminal, and its subject no longer exists to re-observe |

**4 is a consequence of 3 and is listed separately because it is the only one that is closed to future
evidence.** Collapsing it into the class would lose the property that matters.

## R33.3 — The recording constraint is in the rows now, and QA swept the class rather than the instance

**Endorsed, and QA's reason is better than the constraint's original form:** *a constraint that lives only
in a message is not a constraint, because the operator reads the row.* **This register imposed the BC-7
constraint at R31.3 in its own document, where no operator will ever see it.** It is now in **both** the
Predicted-value and failure-mode cells of **BC-7 and BC-8**.

**And the sweep is the right shape** — rows naming the flag or bit: BC-7, BC-8, BC-15, BC-16; BC-16's
primary is a probe so the constraint is meaningless there, BC-15 is `WITHDRAWN`, **leaving BC-7 and BC-8.**
**BC-8's bit-*cleared* limb is the same defect as BC-7's bit-*set* limb, on 5712's own repair route** —
which this register would not have found, having been handed one instance and having reasoned about that
instance.

**This register's R31.3 constraint is therefore discharged into the rows and is no longer this stage's to
enforce by memory.** It is enforced where it is read.

## R33.4 — **The stop condition: AGREED**, with three conditions that are what make the agreement load-bearing

QA's declaration: *"the two cell pairs are now consistent with each other and with the conditions, so the
next defect found in this register will be a different kind, and if it is not, the rows are being edited
rather than improved. I am not looking for a third cell pair."*

**Agreed — and this register's reason is stronger than QA's own, so it is given rather than the courtesy
version.**

QA's ground is diminishing returns and edit risk, both real: **the rows have been rewritten three times in
a day, and every rewrite is a fresh opportunity to introduce the defect it is correcting.** But the
decisive ground is elsewhere:

> **Row quality is not what is blocking this chain. Eleven rows are executable and none has been
> observed.** Every defect found across five revisions has been in the **instructions for observing**, and
> not one in the observing, **because no observing has occurred.** Continuing to refine instructions while
> the observation count stands at zero is displacement, however good each individual finding is. **Stopping
> is not a concession on rigour; it is redirecting effort to the thing that is actually absent.**

**Three conditions, without which the agreement would be a pleasantry:**

1. **The gate records this sweep as *stopped by agreement at a defined point*, not as *completed*.** A
   bounded audit reported as exhaustive is a silent cap, and this register does not publish those. **The
   gate will state that a third cell pair was not searched for, by agreement between QA and UAT, and why.**
2. **The stop binds deliberate search, not incidental reporting.** **QA has stated a prediction — *the next
   defect will be of a different kind*. It is treated as one.** If a third-pair defect surfaces while a row
   is being executed, it is recorded, not suppressed by the stop; **a falsified prediction is data about
   whether the stop was premature, and is not a breach of it by whoever finds it.**
3. **The stop expires at the post-rider tip.** The rider changes production code at three sites. **When
   the surfaces beneath these rows move, the cell-consistency question is fresh because the artifact
   changed, not because anyone went looking** — and it is answered inside QA's normal re-verification, not
   as a new sweep. **This is the same principle as prediction staleness, applied to cells rather than to
   values.**

**Both stages hold it, and this register will not keep finding members.**

## R33.5 — QA's prose rule, adopted here without amendment

QA set its two texts side by side — the register cell's *"only the Message JSON shows it"* against its own
prose one turn later, *"with nothing to show it"* — and its verdict is the correct one: **"Different
claims, and the difference is the entire ruling."** One names an instrument and makes the limb observable;
the other says the limb has no witness.

**Its adopted rule is adopted here too:** **when prose summarises a row it has just written, the row is
quoted, not paraphrased. A paraphrase of one's own cell is a fresh claim and gets checked like one.**

**This register is squarely inside that rule and has said so** (R32.3): its Status-Detail blocks are prose
alongside a structured artifact and are not exempt. **Third member of plan D42's class in two days —
steering, QA, and this register — which is what D42 predicted when it refused to scope itself to one
coordinator.**
