# Rev 34 — **A verdict's identity must name the criteria it was measured against, not only the tree.** The rider has landed; three things this register recorded in advance have fired.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] relayed
code review's verdict at `e1d9b2b` **together with a dependency disclosed by the reviewer after it had
already published**, in the reviewer's own words rather than as a summary. **Tree, ancestry and plan rev 47
re-derived here. No gate is opened.**

## R34.1 — Re-derived at this rev's write time, not inherited

| Fact | Value | Instrument |
|---|---|---|
| Working tree | **`chain/013-rider-2` at `e1d9b2b7090b46709861a9c4b2a57f340617777b`**, tracked-clean | `rev-parse`, `status --porcelain` → 0 |
| `origin/master` | **`2d90e8a`** — **the branch is unpushed** | `ls-remote`, computed at the remote |
| Ancestry | **`2d90e8a` is an ancestor of `e1d9b2b`** | `merge-base --is-ancestor`, exit 0 |
| Code review | **`APPROVED`**, `Reviewed-At: e1d9b2b…`, doc `e648aa0`, range `2d90e8a..e1d9b2b`, 2 commits / 20 files / 10 non-test | at the document |

**Steering's account holds in every particular.**

## R34.2 — **Three things fire at once, and all three were recorded in advance. That is the only reason they are not surprises**

**The rider has landed. One event, three consequences this register wrote down before it happened:**

1. **R28.5's predicted expiry has occurred.** The code review verdict `APPROVED_WITH_COMMENTS` at
   `2d90e8a` **is now EXPIRED** — its tree moved, exactly as R28.5 said it would, and **nothing in that
   document announces it.** R28.5 exists so that this is a recorded fact rather than a discovery. **No
   stage may cite it.**
2. **R33.4's stop condition has expired**, by its own condition 3. The cell-consistency question reopens
   **because the artifact moved, not because anyone went looking**, and it is answered inside QA's
   re-verification rather than as a new sweep.
3. **The surfaces beneath the register rows have moved**, so **prediction freshness is owed at the new
   tip** — the same trigger as rev 18, whose marking QA Phase 3 later cleared **by execution at
   `2d90e8a`, a tree that is no longer the tip.**

**On (3), this register records the trigger and does not perform the marking.** Row definitions are QA's;
**QA is verifying at `e1d9b2b` now and that pass is where freshness lands.** **What this register does say
is that the eleven rows were made executable against `2d90e8a` and the tree has moved** — so
**executability is annotated as pending QA's freshness check at the new tip, not asserted across it.**
**Carrying an executability judgement across a tree change is the same error as carrying a verdict across
one**, and this register has spent six revisions saying so about other people's verdicts.

## R34.3 — **The finding: verdict identity has two axes and this chain has been watching one**

The reviewer disclosed, unprompted and after publishing:

> *"At plan rev 46 — the revision in force when I reviewed — **M9-d's acceptance property required `pnpm
> lint` to 'exit zero on the tip.'** … My own gate table records `lint` **exit 1**. So **an acceptance
> criterion of the milestone I was reviewing was unmet at the tree I approved, and my APPROVED did not
> address it.** … **My APPROVED now rests on D51 having restated that clause.** Had the planner gone the
> other way and kept it, my verdict would have needed revisiting."*

**And its own characterisation is the one to keep:** *"The record is not false; it is **incomplete in a way
that reads as complete**."*

**This is the answer to a gap this register raised at R24.2 and R26.7 and said it would derive, and the
answer is larger than the question was.** This register asked what **paths** an anchor should cover. **The
anchor needs a second axis entirely.**

> **A verdict rests on two things: the code, and the criteria it was measured against. It expires when
> EITHER moves.** R24.2's doctrine — *a verdict without a named identity cannot expire, which is what makes
> it dangerous rather than merely incomplete* — **has been applied to code only, by every stage in this
> chain including this one.**
>
> **`Reviewed-At: e1d9b2b` names the tree. Nothing names plan rev 46.** So a reader of that document cannot
> tell the verdict was measured against a criteria set that has since been superseded — **and had the
> planner gone the other way, the verdict would have become wrong silently, with its document still
> reading as complete.** That is precisely the property R24.2 identifies as dangerous, on an axis nobody
> was watching.

**Consequence for the gate, and it is this register's to enact:** **the gate names both — the commit and
the plan revision in force — and states that either moving expires it.** The path-set question R26.7 parked
is still owed and is now the smaller half of the anchor.

## R34.4 — The verdict stands, and this register re-derived rather than accepted that

**The verdict is not amended and nothing about the tree changed.** But **rev 31's rule does not exempt a
favourable change**: *when a ruling changes the standard, every classification made under the old standard
is void — not narrowed.* **The code review verdict was made under plan rev 46. Rev 47 superseded it.** So
the verdict required re-derivation against rev 47 **even though rev 47 helped it**, and **the fact that the
answer was going to be convenient is not a reason to skip the step.**

**Re-derivation performed at the plan, reading rev 47's whole write set rather than the one clause the
reviewer checked:**

| Rev 47 / D51 changed | Bears on the verdict? |
|---|---|
| **M9-d's *"exit zero on the tip"* clause restated** — both `tsc` projects 0, parsing errors 0, `lint` non-zero on the accepted residue | **Yes — the disclosed one.** Removes the objection |
| **Validation item 1 amended** so a non-zero `lint` exit on the accepted residue is not a failure of the item | **Yes, and the reviewer did not name this one.** Same subject, same direction |
| **R11's prediction record written** — *"the disposition rule fired as designed"* | No — a plan-internal record, not a criterion |
| **M9-b carries CR24-2** as a member of its own class on a dead path, fixed under D22 | No — dispositions a finding the reviewer raised; does not change a standard it approved against |
| **Round 24's four LOWs grow nothing** | No — disposition |

**Result: rev 47's criterion changes are confined to the lint-residue subject, and both move in the
direction that removes an objection. Nothing else in rev 47 changes a standard the verdict was measured
against.**

**So the verdict at `e1d9b2b` is sound under plan rev 47, and this is established by reading rev 47's write
set — not inferred from the reviewer's account of one clause.** **Nothing goes back to the reviewer**, and
**nothing goes to the owner**: derive before routing, in any direction (R32.2), which this register got
wrong two revisions ago and is applying here.

**On the dated line steering has put to the reviewer:** this register **supports it and will not press it**
— it is the reviewer's document. **If the reviewer declines, the gate carries the dependency regardless**,
because the gate is what a later reader opens first. **It is better in both places and it is sufficient in
one.**

## R34.5 — The reviewer's instrument note: a third variant, and it is the one that lies in the alarming direction

Checking steering's account of rev 47, the reviewer found **`grep -c "exit zero on the tip"` going 1 → 2**,
where the naive reading is *the clause survived and multiplied.* **Both hits are quotations of the
withdrawn clause** — inside the D51 row and the changelog — **the plan naming what it changed rather than
dropping it silently.** Its rule: **"A count over text is not a reading of it."**

**Third distinct variant of this class in two days, and the three are worth setting together because they
fail in three different directions:**

| Instrument | Direction | Naive reading |
|---|---|---|
| QA's row count off a printed listing | **undercounts** | fewer members than exist |
| This register's rev-28 diffstat (`267 insertions, 1 deletion`) | **looks correct** | a legitimate line-split |
| The reviewer's `grep -c`, 1 → 2 | **overcounts, alarmingly** | *the clause survived and multiplied* |

**The remedy is the same in all three and is now stated at full strength:** **a count is not a reading, a
sum is not a check, and a plausible diffstat is not a verification.** **What settles a claim about a
document is reading the passages the count points at** — which is what the reviewer did, and what caught
it.

**Note also that the plan behaved correctly here**: it **quoted the withdrawn clause rather than deleting
it**, which is this chain's superseded-text discipline — **and that correct behaviour is exactly what made
the count lie.** A record that preserves what it supersedes will always defeat a naive occurrence count.
**The discipline is right and the instrument must account for it.**

## R34.6 — Steering's second unprompted self-correction today, recorded

Steering had told this register and the owner that CR24-5 *"was right and produced a plan revision."* **The
reviewer corrected it and steering carried the correction here:** CR24-5 was narrower — **a registration
gap**, a ruling binding stages that was not readable from the register. **The unmet criterion is what was
found when going to check CR24-5, and it is a gap in the reviewer's own verdict rather than a credit to its
finding.** Steering had collapsed the two.

**Recorded because the pattern is now three-deep and consistent**: steering has corrected its own framing
to this register twice today, unprompted, and QA once. **The correction rate is not the finding — that all
three were caught by someone other than their author is.**

## R34.7 — Ledger delta at rev 34

# Rev 35 — Two corrections to rev 34, **and they are not the same kind.** One claim was superseded by action; **the other was false when written.**

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] reported
that a statement in rev 34 no longer holds. **Verified at the code-review document (`47e233b`) at this rev's
write time — which is the discipline that would have prevented the second correction below. No gate is
opened. No row moves.**

## R35.1 — Verified at the document

| Claim | Verified |
|---|---|
| Round 23 now opens **`EXPIRED — DO NOT CITE`** | **Confirmed, L6449.** Both anchors named as moved; superseded in full by round 24 |
| `Reviewed-At` carries both axes | **Confirmed.** Field reads **`TREE e1d9b2b on chain/013-rider-2, CRITERIA plan rev 46 (d588790), standing under rev 47 (06fad81)`**, with the rule stated in the field itself |
| Basis names **both** criteria changes | **Confirmed**, re-derived by the reviewer via `git diff d588790 06fad81` rather than from the relay |

## R35.2 — **Correction one: *"nothing in that document announces it"* — TRUE when written, superseded by action.** The instruction stands; its urgency is discharged

**Superseded text, preserved:** rev 34 stated at **R34.2** and again at **R34.7** that the expired
`2d90e8a` verdict was expired **"and nothing in that document announces it."**

**That was accurate at rev 34's write time. It is now false, because the reviewer acted.**

**And separating what survives from what does not is the whole of this note:**

- **The instruction — *no stage may cite the `2d90e8a` verdict* — STANDS, unchanged.** Its ground is the
  **expiry**: the tree moved. **That ground is untouched by anything the reviewer did.**
- **The urgency is DISCHARGED.** *"Nothing announces it"* was never the ground; it was the reason the
  expiry was dangerous. **Rev 34 stated ground and urgency in one breath and thereby made a durable
  instruction look contingent on another party's inaction.**

> **A rationale that depends on another party's inaction is not a durable rationale.** The reviewer was
> always free to act, and did. **Rev 34 should have written the instruction on the expiry and the urgency
> beside it, not through it.**

## R35.3 — **Correction two, and it is materially worse: *"Nothing names plan rev 46"* was FALSE WHEN WRITTEN**

**Superseded text, preserved:** rev 34 stated at **R34.3**: **"`Reviewed-At: e1d9b2b` names the tree.
Nothing names plan rev 46."**

**The document's Verdict Basis named rev 46 in prose.** The claim was **false at the moment it was
written**, and it is not rescued by the finding it supported being correct.

**What is true, and is what rev 34 should have said:** *the field a reader keys on named only the tree,
while the criteria revision lived in prose beside it.* **The reviewer's own formulation is better than
either of ours** — *"the constraint was in the document but not in the field that carries the identity"* —
and it establishes the hole was **larger** than this register framed it: **`Reviewed-At` contained no plan
revision at all.**

**The defect in how the false claim was produced, which is the part worth keeping:**

> **This register asserted a negative existential — *nothing names X* — about another stage's document,
> from a read six revisions old, without re-opening it.**

**That is this chain's purest instrument-lie shape, and this register has spent nine sightings naming it in
other people's work.** An existence or absence predicate is the hardest claim to make soundly; **rev 34's
was not made over a filtered stream but over *memory*, which is worse, because memory produces no
contradicting counter-instrument at all.**

**And the remedy was already this stage's own written rule, applied at half strength:** *a status restated
from a predecessor document names that document as the authority and is re-read at write time, not carried
from an earlier read in the same session.* **Rev 34 named the document as the authority and did not
re-read it.**

**The irony is exact and is recorded rather than smoothed: this happened inside the finding about anchors —
a prose claim overstating an artifact, in the revision arguing that a field must carry what prose cannot be
trusted to carry.** **Plan D42's class, third membership for this register**, and the first that is a
concrete instance rather than an acknowledgement (R32.3).

**The two corrections are deliberately not collapsed.** One is a true statement overtaken by events; the
other is an untrue statement. **Filing them together would lose exactly the distinction this register
insists on for terminal states** — and would let the worse one shelter under the better.

## R35.4 — What the reviewer did, and the two things it asked be recorded

**All three points acted on**, and on the dual anchor it **checked before agreeing** rather than adopting —
finding the hole larger than either framing. **The rule now stands in the field it governs, not only in
prose about the field.** On Validation item 1 it **re-derived by `git diff d588790 06fad81` rather than
taking the relay**, confirmed its basis line had been wrong by omission, and stated explicitly that
soundness under rev 47 was established **by this register reading rev 47's whole write set, not by its own
account of one clause** — *"that distinction is the whole point and I have not softened it."*

**The reviewer asked two things be recorded as this register's.** The first is a rule and is recorded as
one: **a classification made under a superseded standard is void, not narrowed — and the answer being
convenient is not a reason to skip the step.** It notes it would most easily have failed that one, the
conclusion being in its favour and already relayed to it.

**The second is an observation about this register's checking, and it is recorded with its counterweight
attached, because publishing it alone would be self-flattery at the exact moment it stops being true.**
Both times this register checked the reviewer's work today it found something larger than the thing being
checked. **And this revision is the third check — of this register's own rev 34 — and it found two defects,
both this register's, one of them false at the moment of writing.** **The practice is what has value, not
the practitioner, and it cuts the way it is pointed.**

## R35.5 — The instrument class: a fourth member, and the reviewer's generalisation completes it

**A fourth member, found in the `Reviewed-At` field while verifying the above** — the reviewer's own
round-23 defect, recorded against itself: *"my Class named **sites that split** but my predicate enumerated
**schemas that emit the delimiter** — the trigger set, not the defect set. True count at `2d90e8a` is
THREE; I reported one. QA caught it."* Its rule: **an enumeration must run the predicate its own Class
sentence names.**

**That is R22.1's lesson, reached independently from a different defect.** This register wrote, correcting
rev 21's D44 strike: *"an instrument that searches for the token searches for the token — a strike is
verified against the claim class it was ordered against."* **Two stages, two unrelated defects, the same
rule.** **A rule reached twice independently is a different object from a rule stated once**, and it goes
to the retrospective as such.

**And the reviewer's generalisation is better than this register's and is adopted without amendment:**

> **The common factor across all four is not that counts are unreliable. It is that each instrument was
> sound and each predicate was wrong for its question.**
>
> **A record that preserves what it supersedes will defeat any naive occurrence count — and the better the
> record's discipline, the more reliably it will.**

**The second sentence is the one this register would not have reached.** It says the hazard **scales with
the quality of the record**: a chain that quotes superseded text rather than deleting it — which this one
does, by rule, in this very revision — **guarantees that occurrence counts over its documents lie.** **The
discipline is right and must not be relaxed; the instruments are what must change.**

QA Complete at `33908af`. **This is a NEW decision by this stage at the tree and criteria named below. It
inherits no anchor from any predecessor document, from a docket, or from a relay.**

**Rev 35's Next Action 3 was honoured before this was written**: every document this gate asserts a fact
about was re-opened at this rev's write time — the plan, the code-review document, the QA document, and
the tree. **That action existed because rev 34 asserted a negative existential from a six-revision-old
read, and this gate is the document where that would have cost the most.**

>
> **THIS DECISION EXPIRES WHEN EITHER MOVES.** A commit to the tree expires it; a change to plan 013's
> milestones, acceptance properties or Validation items expires it. **Neither expiry announces itself,
> which is why both are named here** (R34.3).

→ **0 lines**; **`master` = `2d90e8a`**; **`origin/master` = `2d90e8a`, computed at the remote** — **the
branch is UNPUSHED and nothing has been released.**

**COVERED SET — the 20 paths in `2d90e8a..e1d9b2b`**, stated explicitly because *a verdict whose scope is a
hash alone is unfalsifiable in the way an unnamed one is; it merely looks rigorous* (R26.7):

= 10. No discrepancy.**

### R36.1.1 — The criteria moved while this gate was being written. **Derived, not taken — and it caught a defect in this gate's own draft**

**The plan advanced to rev 48 (`f5b7cac`) mid-draft.** QA's verdict names `Criteria-At: plan 013 rev 47`.
**This register's own rule — *a verdict expires when either the code or the criteria move* — fired on the
gate that established it**, which is the correct outcome and not an irony to be enjoyed.

**Rev 47 → 48 derived here by `git diff`, reading the whole write set** (8 insertions, 7 deletions, three
hunks), **not taken from the planner's report or from the relay:**

| Hunk | Change | Criterion? |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `Status-Detail` and `Rev:` metadata | **No** |
| 2 | changelog row 48 added | **No** — record only |
| 3a | **M9-a's `Instrument for the negative` clause restated** — from *"no site calls the joiner any more"* (`grep -c generateErrorMessage → 0`) to *"no site recovers line structure from joined library output"* (`git grep -c "split(' | ')" -- src` → 0), the rev-47 form **named as mis-specified in place** | **No — and this had to be checked rather than assumed.** See below |
| 3b | **M9-b's CR24-2 note corrected** — the reviewer called the branch unreachable; QA executed it and found it **dead for `incoming`, live for `current`**; the DRY concern stands, the reachability claim does not. The plan states in terms that this is *"not by a plan change"* | **No** — a factual note |

**Why 3a is not a criterion change, established on the plan's own structure rather than on anyone's
characterisation of it:** the plan organises each item as **Class / Property / Instrument**, and **the
changed text sits inside the `Instrument for the negative …` clause. The `Property` clause is
byte-identical across rev 47 and rev 48.** The property — *the number of visible lines equals the number
of issues, and no substring of any issue's message is split* — never moved. **A mis-specified check of an
unchanged property was corrected.**

**And the direction is worth stating plainly, because it inverts the usual caution:** **under rev 47's
literal instrument text, M9-a's negative was UNMET at `e1d9b2b`** — the joiner survives, called with a
one-element array. **Citing rev 47 would anchor this gate to a criteria set containing a check that fails
on the very tree it approves**, and QA is the stage that reported that check as wrong (INFO-1). **So rev 48
is not merely permissible here; it is the sounder anchor.**

**QA's `Criteria-At: rev 47` carries, because no property QA verified moved** — established by the
derivation above. **Whether QA's own field stands under rev 48 is QA's call and not this stage's**; this
gate does not depend on QA re-issuing, because the delta was verified here. **If QA rules otherwise, this
gate expires with its verdict and is re-issued.**

### R36.1.2 — **A defect in this gate's own draft, caught by steering rather than by diligence**

**The draft of R36.1 read: *"CRITERIA: plan 013 rev 47 (`06fad81`), read at rev 48 with no criterion
changed since."*** **That clause asserted a negative about the rev 47 → 48 delta without running the diff.**
This register had read the plan's header, seen `Rev: 48`, and asserted what changed between them from
nothing at all.

**It is rev 35's defect repeating inside the gate, one revision after this register recorded it as a Next
Action.** And it was caught **because steering sent an unsolicited warning before this document was
committed** — **luck in the timing, not diligence in the method.**

**The refinement, which is why rev 35's action was necessary and insufficient:**

> **Rev 35's action said *re-open every document whose contents the gate asserts a fact about* — and this
> register DID re-open the plan.** Opening a document tells you its current state. **It tells you nothing
> about a delta.** A claim about *what changed between two revisions* is settled by a diff and by nothing
> else.
>
> **Verify the claim in the form the claim is made.** A claim of *content* needs a read; a claim of *change*
> needs a diff; a claim of *quantity* needs a computation; a claim of *absence* needs an exhaustive
> enumeration. **Re-reading is not a universal solvent, and treating it as one is how this clause got
> written.**

**Recorded here, in the gate, rather than in a later revision** — a gate that concealed a near-miss in its
own drafting would be exactly the *"incomplete in a way that reads as complete"* artifact this chain has
spent two days learning to refuse.

## R36.3 — **VALUE DELIVERY ASSESSMENT.** The Product Owner's question, which is not the same question the other three stages answered

**Value Statement, copied from plan rev 48 §"Value Statement and Business Objective" (APPROVED AS WRITTEN, D4, not a word amended):**

> As a **guild owner editing a Components V2 message in hooks.gg**, I want **every editing surface around
> the composer — "Save As", the Message JSON view, copy and Load, import from a Discord message URL, a
> webhook URL or the bot's "Get Message" code, and cross-tab change detection — to carry my message's
> components and its V2 flag faithfully**, so that **no editing action silently discards what I built or
> misreports what kind of message it is, and I never land on an empty copy of a message that was whole a
> moment ago**.

**Assessment: PARTIAL — and the shortfall is not a defect anyone found. It is a step nobody has taken.**

**What is established.** Every automated instrument is green at `e1d9b2b`: suite **47 files / 605 / exit 0**,
both `tsc` projects clean, ESLint parsing errors 45 → 0, all six M9 properties met, TDD gate passed,
**F-QA31 verified fixed by an instrument written before the fix and unedited since**, CR24-6 discharged
with a captured oracle and two mutants. **Chain 013 carries no QA-raised blocking condition.**

**What is not established, and it is the value statement itself.** **This value statement's entire content
is that the user is not *silently* misled** — *silently discards*, *misreports*, *an empty copy of a message
that was whole a moment ago*. **Its failure mode is, by construction, one that leaves no trace in a test
run.** The register's eight `PRE` rows are that statement decomposed into observations — Load naming the
right mode, drop notices naming the right types, the flag bit matching the wire, the notice appearing and
clearing — **and not one of them has been observed by a human.**

**CR24-6's Assumption Not Checked is this stated at the code layer, and it is the sharpest thing in the
chain:** every instrument — QA's and the round's — **stops at the string the `message` field receives.
Nothing proves `ConfirmationProvider` renders it, renders it where the eye goes, or renders it at all.**
Its falsity signature is **the owner presses Load and the dialog is empty, truncated, or shows the previous
message's text — right in the string, wrong on the screen.** **That is precisely "misreports what kind of
message it is", and every green test in this chain is compatible with it.**

> **So: the value is demonstrated in the code and undemonstrated at the surface, and the gap falls exactly
> where this value statement lives.** A suite cannot close it, because **the failure mode is silence, and
> silence is what instruments are worst at and what a person at the surface is best at.** **QA's own line
> is the same conclusion from the other side and is adopted here: *"BC-1 is not a formality now that
> F-QA31 is fixed; it is the only thing that closes this."***

**No drift from the plan's objective is detected.** The implementation addresses the surfaces the value
statement names. **The gap is evidentiary, not directional.**

## R36.5 — **RELEASE DECISION**

> # **APPROVED FOR RELEASE, conditional on the human click-through of BC-1, BC-4, BC-6, BC-7, BC-8, BC-9, BC-10 and BC-18.**
> # **If any of these fail, return to the implementer before release.**

**This is NOT an unconditional approval, and `NOT EXECUTED` is not a terminal state. No release is recorded
complete while any of the eight stands at it.**

**The eight precede the merge, and the reason is mechanical rather than cautious:** **`master` serves
production, so pushing to `master` deploys.** **For a repo that deploys on push, "before merge" and "before
deploy" are the same instant.** These are `PRE` conditions; **a `PRE` row observed after the merge was not
observed before the deploy, and recording it as satisfied afterwards is the substitution this register has
refused throughout.**

**Every outstanding condition, with an owner and its permitted terminal states:**

| # | Row | Owner | Terminal states available | Gates |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **BC-1** · Load confirmation names the actual mode | **the owner**, at a browser | `EXECUTED` (result + date) · `WITHDRAWN` (owner only) · `SUPERSEDED` | **release** |
| 2 | **BC-4** · dialog stays · error shown · nothing created | the owner | same | **release** |
| 3 | **BC-6** · unsupported **type** removed, notice names that type | the owner | same | **release** |
| 4 | **BC-7** · V2 · **bit set** · warning | the owner | same | **release** |
| 5 | **BC-8** · Classic · content intact · **bit cleared** · warning · no V2 error | the owner | same | **release** |
| 6 | **BC-9** · two options · each names its drop · nothing created first · dismissal non-destructive | the owner | same | **release** |
| 7 | **BC-10** · spot-observation limb (suite limb **MET**) | the owner | same | **release** |
| 8 | **BC-18** · notice appears · persists · **clears** | the owner | same | **release** |
| 9–12 | **BC-12, BC-14, BC-17, BC-20** | the owner, **in normal use after deploy** | same | **chain closure only** |

**Rows 9–12 are approved conditionally and gate the chain's closure, not the release act.** **BC-20 has an
absence signature: no volume of quiet is evidence for it.** **BC-12 refutes by construction — one 4xx is a
hotfix trigger.**

**`RECOMMEND WITHDRAWAL` is not issued for any row**, and no row is recommended for withdrawal to make this
gate cheaper.

## R36.6 — **The walkthrough, written for the person doing it**

**Two recording constraints, which are the difference between an observation and an impression:**

1. **BC-7's bit-set and BC-8's bit-cleared limbs are settled by opening the Message JSON — never by the
   screen looking right.** *"Everything looked correct"* is **not** an observation of those limbs and must
   not be filed as one. **This is now in both rows' own cells** (R33.3), so it is enforced where it is read.
2. **BC-1 carries CR24-6's unchecked assumption and is the only instrument that can settle it.** **Look at
   the dialog, not at the code.** If it is **empty, truncated, or showing the previous message's text**,
   that is the failure — **and every automated test in this chain passes in that state.**

**Record the observed result beside each row's prediction, never over it.** A row that cannot be reached is
recorded as such rather than passed.

## R36.7 — **RESIDUAL RISKS.** Four, all silent, none of them a defect found

**None announces itself. None is findable by reading the register's terminal column. That is why this
section exists.**

1. **BC-5's subject** — a behaviour in the product **never characterised by anyone**; the obligation was
   ended by owner ruling and **nothing is claimed about the behaviour either way.** `WITHDRAWN` is a closed
   shape, so a reader scanning terminal rows sees a discharged register.
2. **The plan describes half of one shipped change, by owner ruling (U-C5).** Mode-follows-marking is stated
   for flagged-but-empty messages; **it is not stated that ordinary component-less messages changed too** —
   previously skipped, now actively set to classic. **No stage may "correct" it. The deployment record
   carries the full statement; the plan does not.**
3. **Attestation by reference rather than by characterisation** — class; members **BC-21** (adopted a
   described act) and **BC-19a** (attested by row number). Both rows are correctly terminal.
4. **BC-19a's *clears* limb has no positive evidence from any route, and none can now be obtained** — its
   subject was deleted at `2d90e8a`. **Listed apart from (3) because it is the only residual closed to
   future evidence.**

5. **M9-b's spec is blind to a live path.** Verified at the reviewer's document (`41ca30b`, L6884–6896):
   `settledMode` is applied to both `incoming` and `current`; **`current` is the stored record and is
   guarded by nothing.** Measured at `e1d9b2b`, a stored row-E record gives `fromMode="v2"`,
   `toMode="classic"` — **production's answer is correct** — while **M9-b's spec oracle returns `"classic"`
   for that same record.** **No user-visible defect today; production holds the correct value.** The
   residual is that **nothing catches it if that changes.**

**Residual 5 is a different kind from 1–4 and is not collapsed into them.** **Residuals 1–4 are about what
is not *known*; residual 5 is about what is not *guarded*.** Its signature is the absence of a protection
behind a behaviour that is currently right — which is why no terminal column and no green suite can
surface it.

**An absence that is the CORRECT state, recorded so a later reader does not read it as a missed fix:**
**`emptied-composite-drop-unnamed` (F-QA32) is absent from the fixed tree and that is expected.** This
**inverts** the chain's own silent-mode doctrine — a reader primed by *"quiet is no evidence"* finds nothing
where a defect was described and reads an **omission**. **Both readings of an absence are available and
neither is safe by default; only the record settles which was expected.**

**A bounded audit, reported as bounded** (R33.4 condition 1): the register's cell-consistency sweep covered
**(condition ↔ failure mode)** and **(condition ↔ predicted value)**. **A third cell pair was not searched
for. The sweep was STOPPED BY AGREEMENT between QA and this stage at a defined point — it did not complete** —
because eleven rows were executable with zero observed and further refinement was displacement. **The stop
expired when the tree moved; QA's freshness pass at `e1d9b2b` covers the artifact change.**

**Two INFO findings, neither blocking, both recorded because a wrong instrument produces a false result in
whichever direction it points:** **INFO-1** — M9-a's **plan-stated** instrument says a grep *"returns 0"*
and **it returns 1**; the code is right and the instrument is wrong, and anyone running it literally gets a
**false failure**. Routed to the planner. **INFO-2** — **CR24-2's "dead path" claim is wrong**: dead for
`incoming`, **live for `current`**, and the live path executes correctly. **The DRY concern stands; the
reachability claim under it does not.**

> **A HAZARD ATTACHED TO CR24-2, WHICH IS NOT RESIDUAL-SHAPED AND MUST NOT BE FILED AS ONE.** Steering's
> account had this creating *"no task and no owner"*. **At the document it does: CR24-2 stands as an open
> `LOW` assigned to the implementer**, and the reviewer's correction says in terms — ***"DO NOT fix this by
> deleting the branch as dead: that premise is false and the branch is correct. Collapse the two encodings
> to one, keeping production's."*** **The finding is STRENGTHENED, not withdrawn.** **An implementer acting
> on the superseded premise would delete a correct, live branch.** It does not block this release — it is a
> `LOW`, the code review approved with it open, and no user-visible defect exists — **but it must not be
> lost and it must not be acted on as written in round 24.**

### R36.7.1 — Two notes on method, recorded because they are the positive half of this chain's instrument findings

**A third independent member of the predicate-mismatch class.** The reviewer's own framing: *"CR24-2's
subject is a predicate that does not match the claim it supports. I committed that defect inside the
finding that raises it"* — it had checked one of `computeLoadDiff`'s two arguments' guards and generalised
to both. **Three members now, from three stages and three unrelated defects**: this register's rev-21 D44
strike (verified against the token, not the claim class), the reviewer's round-23 enumeration (the trigger
set, not the defect set), and this. **And in all three nothing looked wrong** — here, *the call site it
cited was the right call site and the guard it cited was really there.* **A rule with three independent
members from three authors is not a lesson about any of them.**

**And the method that caught it, which is the counterpart worth keeping.** On CR24-6 the reviewer concedes
it was wrong about **the consequence and not merely the scope**, and names what saved it: it built the
oracle **and then tried to make the seam transmit**, rather than accepting the form of its own argument.
**Every instrument finding in this chain was caught by someone attempting to falsify a claim rather than
to confirm it** — a mutant, a counter-count, a diff, an execution. **That is the transferable part; the
individual defects are not.**

## R36.8 — What this decision does NOT do

- **It does not authorise the merge to `master`.** **The merge is a separate act with a separate owner
  (devops), and because `master` serves production it is the deploy.** An approval at a chain-branch commit
  is not an approval of a merge that has not happened.
- **It does not close chain 013.** Four `POST` rows stand non-terminal.
- **It does not revive the `4a7126b` decision**, which remains `EXPIRED IN FULL`. **Expiry is not
  suspension; this is a new decision, not a restoration.**
- **It does not rest on `UAT Complete`**, which is a status reached at `4a7126b`.
- **It grants nothing to any tree but `e1d9b2b` or any criteria but plan rev 47.**

## R36.9 — Version and changelog

**Recommended: MINOR.** New and corrected behaviour across the editing surfaces, no breaking API change.
**Mode-follows-marking changes observable behaviour for ordinary component-less messages** — previously
skipped, now actively set to classic — which is user-visible and argues against `patch`, and is a
correctness fix rather than an incompatibility, which argues against `major`.

**Key changes** — *and per residual 2, the changelog is written from the **deployment record**, not from the
plan, which by owner ruling states half of the mode-follows-marking change:*

- Editing surfaces carry components and the V2 flag faithfully across Save As, Message JSON, copy/Load,
  URL/webhook/bot-code import, and cross-tab change detection
- Unsupported component types are named in the drop notice rather than silently discarded
- Load confirmation reports the actual resulting message mode (F-QA31)
- Mode follows marking: flagged-but-empty messages **and** ordinary component-less messages
- Error formatting no longer defeats readable multi-issue errors (MEDIUM-1, eliminated structurally)

## R36.10 — State

**Release decision: `APPROVED FOR RELEASE, CONDITIONAL` (R36.5), at TREE `e1d9b2b` / CRITERIA plan rev 48 (`f5b7cac`).**
**Either moving expires it.** **The `4a7126b` decision remains `EXPIRED IN FULL`.**

**UAT Status: UAT Complete** — for this gate, at this anchor. **Open: 12 rows, all observations, all fresh,
none observed.**

**Next Actions** — *point-in-time; later stage documents are authoritative on these items' current state.*

1. **The owner** — the walkthrough: **BC-1, BC-4, BC-6, BC-7, BC-8, BC-9, BC-10, BC-18, before the merge.**
   **BC-1 first**; it carries CR24-6.
2. **This stage** — record each result beside its prediction and terminalise; **an unconditional approval
   follows only when all eight are terminal.**
3. **Devops** — the merge to `master`, **after** step 1 and **only** on step 2. **It is the deploy.**
4. **Implementer** — **CR24-2: collapse the two encodings, keeping production's. DO NOT delete the branch
   as dead — that premise is withdrawn and the branch is correct and live** (R36.7). **Planner** — **INFO-1 is discharged at rev 48** (R36.1.1) and needs nothing further. **Code review / implementer** — INFO-2, whose plan-side note is corrected at rev 48 while the code fix stands owed under D22. **Steering** — the U-C5 Decisions Register row, **still unwritten**.
5. **Nobody** — merges before the eight are terminal, cites the `2d90e8a` code-review verdict, or reads

# Rev 37 — **The gate is already published.** QA's re-anchor verified; the hold arrived after the commit; and QA's oracle finding strengthens a hazard rather than moving a verdict.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] answered
the rev-48 question and **asked this stage to hold before publishing.** **Verified at QA's document and at
the tree. No verdict moves. No row moves.**

## R37.1 — **The hold arrived after the gate was committed, and that is a process fact, not a fault**

**Rev 36 was committed at `68e6159` before this message arrived.** Steering sent *"THE WORD… write the
gate"*, this stage wrote and committed it, and the hold followed. **Recorded plainly because a gate that
quietly absorbed a hold it had already overtaken would be a false record of its own timing.**

**And the gate does not need to be unpublished, for the reason it was anchored the way it was.**

> **R36.1 states: *THIS DECISION EXPIRES WHEN EITHER MOVES.*** If the owner orders a fix round and the tree
> moves, **the gate expires by its own terms** — cleanly, visibly, and without anyone needing to notice.
> **A published decision carrying a named anchor that expires is strictly better than an unpublished one**,
> because the unpublished one leaves the chain with no recorded position at all while the tree is in doubt.

**Steering's concern is real and its bound is what the anchor buys.** The cost of the tree moving is one
re-issue at a new anchor — **not a wrong decision left standing, which is the cost the anchor exists to
prevent.** **This is the dual anchor doing the job it was written for, one revision after it was written.**

## R37.2 — QA re-anchored to rev 48, derived independently. **Two stages reached the same anchor from the same evidence without coordinating**

**Verified at QA's document at this rev's write time:** `Criteria-At: "plan 013 rev 48, f5b7cac —
RE-ANCHORED 2026-08-25 from rev 47 (06fad81); the tree is unmoved at e1d9b2b and no acceptance property
changed. This verdict expires when EITHER the tree or the criteria move"`. **The captured artifact exists:
`verification/013/probe-qa-verify-rider2/plan-rev47-to-rev48.diff`, 45 lines** — the same figure this
register derived at R36.1.1.

**Rev 36 stated that whether QA's field stands under rev 48 was QA's call and that this gate did not depend
on the answer. QA ruled the same way, and derived rather than accepted** — three hunks, no unnamed fourth,
**and it additionally confirmed that no hunk touches Validation and Release Conditions, Testing Strategy or
Objective**, which this register did not check by name. **That is a real addition to R36.1.1's derivation
and is adopted.**

**QA's own framing is the part to keep**, and it refused the same convenience this register refused:
*"I am not arguing my way out of my own field… That the reading is convenient — text-only, my own finding,
moves toward what I verified — is exactly the reading the rule exists to refuse."* And its caveat:
**"zero movement licenses a scoped pass; it never licenses carrying a verdict forward silently"** — which
is why it recorded a **RE-ANCHOR section** rather than editing a field quietly.

### R37.2.1 — The cost of the dual-anchor rule, recorded now rather than discovered later

**QA carried the structural point further than this register did: the dual anchor makes a stage expire its
own verdict by raising a good finding.** QA's INFO-1 corrected M9-a's instrument; the correction moved the
plan; the moved plan expired QA's own `Criteria-At`. **A rule that penalises a stage for finding something
is a rule that will quietly stop being applied.**

**What keeps it payable is that the re-anchor is cheap — and it is cheap only because the tree did not
move.** **Recorded as a known cost with a named precondition, because an unrecorded cost is how a good rule
is abandoned without anyone deciding to abandon it.** **If a finding ever moves the criteria while the tree
is also moving, this becomes expensive and someone will be tempted to skip it. That is the moment to
re-read this paragraph, not to re-derive the rule.**

## R37.3 — QA's oracle defect: **not a release condition, and this register agrees.** It strengthens a hazard already recorded

**QA disclosed a defect in its own pre-registered oracle**: `modeAfterLoad` returns `'classic'` for a stored
row-E record where production correctly returns `'v2'`. **No assertion is wrong today — no fixture is a
row-E draft.** **QA is explicit it is a fix-round condition and not a release condition, and this register
concurs: nothing is wrong in production at `e1d9b2b`, the gate's decision does not move, and no row is
added.**

**What it changes is the strength of two things rev 36 already carries, and the change is material.**

**Measured by QA under the wrong collapse — a CR24-2 fix keeping the spec's encoding instead of
production's:** QA's extended oracle **4 failed**; **M9-b's own spec 8 passed**; full suite **635 tests /
50 files → 4 failed, all four QA's. 49 of 50 files green.**

> **So the CR24-2 hazard has a SECOND wrong fix, not one.** Rev 36 recorded that deleting the branch as
> dead would remove a correct, live branch. **QA has now measured that keeping the spec's encoding is
> equally wrong and passes 49 of 50 files.** **Without QA's extended oracle in the tree, the whole suite is
> blind to it.**

**And the sharpest consequence lands on rev 36 itself.** R36.7's CR24-2 note is **prose in this document** —
*"DO NOT fix this by deleting the branch as dead."* **QA's word for that is the right one: a banner is not
an instrument.**

**This is R33.3's rule, arriving at this register's own door on a different surface.** That rule read: *a
constraint that lives only in a message is not a constraint, because the operator reads the row.* **Its
counterpart here: a hazard that lives only in prose is not a guard, because the implementer runs the
suite.** **Rev 36's warning is a banner. The guard is a test, and the only candidate test currently carries
a wrong constant.**

**Residual 5 is therefore amended by dated addition — the rev-36 text stands as written:**

> **AMENDED 2026-08-25 (rev 37).** Residual 5 recorded that M9-b's spec is blind to the live `current`
> path. **Add: the only instrument that is *not* blind is QA's extended oracle, and that oracle carries a
> wrong `modeAfterLoad` constant in the same area** — harmless against today's fixtures, and **the reason
> the suite's protection against a wrong CR24-2 fix rests on one instrument with a known defect in it.**

**Nothing in this amendment gates the release act**, and residual 5 remains what it was: **about what is not
*guarded*, not about what is not known.** **It is now better measured, which is the whole difference between
a residual named and a residual understood.**

## R37.4 — Standing position

**The gate stands, unchanged, at TREE `e1d9b2b` / CRITERIA plan rev 48 (`f5b7cac`).** Re-derived at this
rev's write time: `e1d9b2b`, tracked-clean, **`origin/master` still `2d90e8a`, branch unpushed — nothing
released.**

**If the owner orders a fix round and the tree moves, this gate EXPIRES BY ITS OWN TERMS and is re-issued
at the new anchor.** **No verdict is carried across, and no part of rev 36 is inherited** — the re-issue
re-derives, as every anchor in this chain now must. **QA's verdict expires with it, on the same clause, and
neither expiry needs anyone to remember it.**

**Either way, the eight `PRE` rows remain unobserved.** **A fix round changes what must be re-verified; it
does not change what must be looked at.**

| **CRITERIA** | **plan 013 rev 48 (`f5b7cac`)** | **plan 013 rev 49 (`9637735`)** — `Rev: 49`, D52 / M10 registered | **YES** |

> watching stood still.** **A tree-only anchor would read as LIVE right now** — `e1d9b2b` is byte-identical

## R38.2 — **The dual anchor's first live test, and it is worth more than the argument that produced it**

R34.3 argued from a hypothetical: *had the planner gone the other way, the verdict would have become wrong
silently.* **This is no longer hypothetical.** **Two stages and a coordinator all expected the tree axis.
The criteria axis moved first, and it moved within the hour.**

axis:**

> anyone needing to notice"*. That was wrong** — somebody has to look, and the anchor's whole contribution

that read, the sentence would have been false at write time** — **the identical defect rev 35 recorded and

after** — *verify the claim in the form the claim is made* (R36.1.2), on its third consecutive outing.
**Three revisions, three catches, and not one of them by memory or care.**

**Rev 37's *"without anyone needing to notice"* is superseded by this note; the text stands where it was
written.**

R37.2.1 recorded the dual anchor's cost and named its precondition: *it is cheap only because the tree did

# Rev 39 — **CR25-3 DISCHARGED: the paraphrase is faithful and the round is not invalidated.** Three provenance findings, none holding the tree — and the third is CR25-2's own defect recurring inside its fix.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `code-reviewer-013-4`'s **CR25-3**,
routed here by name via `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] on the ground that **QA authored the transcription
and an author checking its own paraphrase against the source is the weaker arrangement.** **Correct
routing, and this stage accepts it: the plan's Objective is this register's own source of truth.**
**No gate is issued. No row moves. The gate remains EXPIRED (rev 38).**

## R39.1 — Source of truth established before it was used

**The plan's Objective is only a source of truth if it is the current one.** Verified before relying on
it: **plan rev 48 → 49 (`f5b7cac` → `9637735`) is five hunks, and none of them contains `## Objective` or
any of D17's row text** — measured, not assumed. **The Objective this check reads is unmoved by the round
that expired the gate.**

**And the oracle file changed between this stage's first and second read of it** — the shared tree working
as it does. **Everything below is derived against the file as it stands now, not against the earlier read.**
**Reporting a finding against a stale read of a file that had already been fixed would have been this
chain's own defect committed in the act of checking for it.**

## R39.2 — **The check: all eight cells against the plan's Objective as written**

**Plan, `## Objective`, D17's five rows verbatim:** *"no flag and no V2 components → Classic, nothing
shown; V2 components without the flag and without content → V2 with the flag set and a warning; the flag
over components within the schema and no content → V2, nothing shown; the flag over content or embeds
without V2 components → a warning and **Classic** (the bit cleared, the body kept); V2 components together
with content or embeds, whatever the flag → the user **chooses** Classic or V2 and is told what the other
drops."*

| # | Cell | Draft | Governing row **in the plan** | Oracle expects | Agrees? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `bit=0 v2=0 classic=0` | `{}` | A — no flag, no V2 components | `classic` | **✓** |
| 2 | `bit=0 v2=0 classic=1` | `{content}` | A | `classic` | **✓** |
| 3 | `bit=0 v2=1 classic=0` | `{components}` | B — V2 components, no flag, no content | `v2` | **✓** |
| 4 | `bit=0 v2=1 classic=1` | `{content, components}` | E — V2 components with content | `choice` | **✓** |
| 5 | `bit=1 v2=0 classic=0` | `{flags}` | **D13 → row C** (see R39.3) | `v2` | **✓** |
| 6 | `bit=1 v2=0 classic=1` | `{flags, content}` | D — flag over content without V2 components | `classic` | **✓** |
| 7 | `bit=1 v2=1 classic=0` | `{flags, components}` | C — flag over components in schema, no content | `v2` | **✓** |
| 8 | `bit=1 v2=1 classic=1` | `{flags, content, components}` | E | `choice` | **✓** |

> ## **VERDICT: the paraphrase is faithful. No cell's expected mode disagrees with the plan's Objective as written. CR25-3's falsity signature is not met and the round is NOT invalidated.**

**Cells 4 and 8 are filtered out of `COMMITTING` by the oracle itself** (`expected !== 'choice'`), so the
literals actually asserted are six. **All six agree.**

## R39.3 — Cell 5: this stage derived the defect independently from the plan side, and found it already fixed

**Reached before reading the correction**: cell 5's draft is `{ flags: V2_FLAG }` — **the flag with no
components at all.** The Objective's row C reads *"the flag over **components within the schema** and no
content"*, which describes a draft that **has** components. **On the plan's text, row C does not govern
cell 5** except on a vacuous reading of *"components within the schema"* over the empty set — **and a cell
whose coverage depends on a vacuous reading is not a literal transcription, it is an inference.**

**The file already carries the fix.** At **CR25-2** the citation was corrected to **D13**, quoted verbatim,
with a comment stating the same reasoning this stage derived: *"This cell has NO components, so the
Objective's 'the flag over components within the schema' clause does not govern it; D13 does."*

**Two independent routes reached the same cell** — the reviewer from the header side, this register from
the plan side. **That is corroboration rather than agreement**, and it is worth more than either finding
alone, because **the cell was reachable from both directions and neither checker could see the other's
work.** **The reviewer's line is confirmed from the plan side: the cell doing the most discriminating work
had the weakest stated provenance.**

**D13's quotation verified against the plan at line 110: verbatim, word for word.** ✓

## R39.4 — **Three provenance findings. None holds the tree; none is a value error; all three are QA's file**

**1 — Row E is truncated in the header quotation, without an ellipsis.** The plan reads *"the user chooses
Classic or V2 **and is told what the other drops**."* The oracle's quotation stops at *"the user chooses
Classic or V2."* **Immaterial to every expected mode** — row-E cells are filtered out of `COMMITTING` and
the dropped clause governs what is *displayed*, not the mode. **But the header presents the text as a
quotation of the plan, and a quotation that silently drops a clause is not one.**

**2 — D13 is quoted correctly and is `SUPERSEDED IN PART by D17`, which the oracle does not say.** The
quoted clause **survives** — the plan's own superseding header states *"the 'not a conflict' clauses
survive as row C"* — so **the citation is sound and this is not a falsity.** But **an oracle whose entire
authority is literal transcription should say when it is quoting a partly-superseded row**, or the next
reader who opens D13, sees `SUPERSEDED IN PART`, and has to work out for themselves which half survives.

**3 — and this is the one worth the routing: the label doing the work is supported by a sentence the
oracle does not quote.**

> **D13's quoted clause says the bit over no components is *"NOT a conflict and is NOT refused."* It does
> not state a mode.** Nothing in the quoted text yields `v2`.
>
> **What yields `v2` is D13's superseding header — *"the 'not a conflict' clauses survive as row C"* —
> plus row C's outcome.** **That sentence is the load-bearing one and the oracle does not quote it.** The
> cell's stated provenance is *"D13: [clause] — row C, so V2"*, in which **"row C" is an unsupported
> assertion within the citation**, true and checkable but not shown.

**This is CR25-2's own defect recurring one level inside its fix**: the value is right, the citation is now
*correct* rather than wrong — **and the stated provenance is still thinner than the actual derivation.**
**Recorded because it is the third member of the class in this file's short history, and because an oracle
that is corrected once tends to be treated as corrected.**

**Remedy is one clause, and it is QA's to write, not this stage's** — this register does not author
instrument text (R29.4's rule, applied to itself): **quote D13's *"survive as row C"* sentence beside the
clause, and mark D13 as superseded in part.**

# Rev 40 — **CORRECTION: rev 39 was performed against the probe copy, not the shipped file.** The discharge's conclusion survives; one of its findings is retracted, two are re-scoped, and CR25-2 is **LIVE** against the tree.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `code-reviewer-013-4` (`288c0b4`) found
two copies of the oracle; `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] relayed it **and asked this stage to place the
error itself rather than take its account.** **Done, and the account is right in substance and wrong in
one detail (R40.3).** **No gate is issued in this revision.**

## R40.1 — Measured here, on both files, with instruments that compute before any filter

| Instrument | **shipped** — `website/src/utils/__tests__/013-qa-cr24-6-…test.ts` | probe — `agent-output/verification/013/probe-qa-verify-rider2/…` |
|---|---|---|
| `wc -l < file` | **186** | 215 |
| `grep -c "D13"` | **0** | **5** |
| `grep -c "CITATION CORRECTED"` | **0** | 1 |
| `grep -c "flag over components within the schema"` | **2** | 3 |

**Read directly, not inferred from counts** — the shipped `CELLS` block and header quotation in full.

> **CONFIRMED: CR25-2 is NOT fixed in the shipped file.** Its cell 5 — draft `{ flags: V2_FLAG }`, **no
> components at all** — still cites *"the flag over components within the schema and no content."*
> **The defect this register independently derived at R39.3 is live in the artifact that runs.**

**And confirmed independently: all eight `expected` values are identical across both copies** — `classic,
classic, v2, choice, v2, classic, v2, choice`. **No oracle verdict changes, no Round-25 measurement is
disturbed, and code review's `APPROVED` stands.** **This is a provenance and record divergence, not a
behavioural one.**

## R40.2 — **The error is this register's, and rev 39's own words convict it**

**Rev 39 read `agent-output/verification/013/probe-qa-verify-rider2/…` — it names that path in its own
text.** **That file is evidence about the oracle. It is not the oracle.** **The artifact under review is
the one the tree executes**, and **M10-b names it**: *"adopted into `src/utils/__tests__/`."* The pointer
was in the criteria this register had already read.

**The part that stings is rev 39's own defence.** It wrote: *"the oracle file changed between this stage's
first and second read… everything below is derived against the file as it stands now… reporting a finding
against a stale read of a file that had already been fixed would have been this chain's own defect
committed in the act of checking for it."*

> **That freshness discipline was applied, correctly, to the wrong file.** **Being current about the wrong
> artifact is not a partial defence — it manufactures confidence.** A stale read of the right file
> announces itself as stale; **a fresh read of the wrong file reads exactly like a fresh read of the right
> one.** **Rev 39 congratulated itself on the discipline that made the error harder to see.**

**And the symptom was noticed and mis-resolved.** Rev 39 saw the file change between reads and concluded
*"the fix landed."* **The correct question was not *did it change* but *which file am I holding*, and
nothing in rev 39 asks it.**

## R40.3 — **Placing the error, as asked — and contradicting steering on one point, as it asked**

**Steering's substance is right and this register confirms it independently: rev 39 checked the probe copy;
CR25-1 and CR25-2 are open against the shipped file.**

**Its decisive-evidence claim is not right, and it is better contradicted than inherited.** Steering
reasoned that *"the four words your row-E check turned on — 'and is told what the other drops' — are in the
probe copy and NOT in the shipped file… That is decisive about which copy you read."*

**Measured here: `grep -c "is told what the other drops"` gives shipped 0, probe 1 — but the probe's single
occurrence is NOT in its D17 quotation.** **Both files' quotations end identically at *"the user chooses
Classic or V2."*** **So that phrase's distribution establishes nothing about which copy was read, and
rev 39's row-E finding holds against the shipped file exactly as written.**

**What actually establishes it is rev 39's own citation of the probe path, plus `D13`: shipped 0.** **The
error is real; that particular proof of it is not.** **Recorded because a correct correction resting on a
wrong instrument is how this chain gets bitten twice** — and because steering said it would rather be
contradicted than have this inherited.

**Where the error entered is not this register's to allocate away.** Steering states it relayed QA's
characterisation — *"the tree's copy keeps a citation that is correct, merely thinner"* — without opening
the shipped file, and names that as its own BC-7 defect repeated. **That is steering's to record and it has
recorded it.** **This register's share is not reduced by it: rev 39 chose the file it read, and no relay
told it to read the probe path.**

## R40.4 — **CR25-3 RE-DERIVED against the shipped file. The conclusion stands; the findings do not all survive**

**Re-checked cell by cell against the plan's Objective (rev 49, verified unmoved by rev 48→49 at R39.1):**

| Cell | Draft | Shipped citation | Expected | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1, 2 | `{}`, `{content}` | row A | `classic` | **✓** |
| 3 | `{components}` | row B | `v2` | **✓** |
| 4, 8 | with content **and** components | row E | `choice` | **✓** (filtered out of `COMMITTING`) |
| **5** | **`{flags}` — no components** | **row C — WRONG** | `v2` | **value ✓, citation ✗** |
| 6 | `{flags, content}` | row D | `classic` | **✓** |
| 7 | `{flags, components}` | row C | `v2` | **✓** |

> ## **CR25-3's verdict is UNCHANGED and now rests on the right artifact: the paraphrase is faithful as to every expected mode, and the round is NOT invalidated.**

**Disposition of rev 39's three findings, against the shipped file:**

| # | Rev 39 finding | Against shipped |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | Row E truncated without ellipsis | **HOLDS, unchanged** — both quotations end at *"chooses Classic or V2"* |
| **2** | D13 quoted but `SUPERSEDED IN PART`, unmarked | **DOES NOT APPLY — the shipped file does not cite D13 at all** |
| **3** | *"row C"* is an unsupported assertion inside the D13 citation | **DOES NOT APPLY, same reason** |

**Findings 2 and 3 were findings about a correction that has not shipped.** They are **re-scoped to the
probe copy** and are **not retracted** — they remain valid criticism of the text QA wrote, and they become
live against the tree **if and when that text is adopted.** **Rev 39's claim *"found already fixed at
CR25-2"* is RETRACTED as to the shipped file; the rev-39 text stands where it was written.**

**CR25-2 is therefore LIVE against the tree, and this register's R39.3 derivation of it — reached from the
plan side, independently — is the standing description of the defect.**

## R40.5 — The class, adopted from the reviewer, because it names the direction that hid it

> **This is `probe-cr1`/`probe-cr10`'s fossil defect running in reverse.** There, an embedded copy fell
> **behind** the tree and silently stopped measuring it. **Here a copy ran *ahead* of the tree and caused a
> gate stage to report a fix that has not shipped.** **Same defect — a second copy of the code under test
> treated as the code under test — and the direction of drift is what disguised it.**
>
> **The artifact under review is the one the tree executes; any other copy is evidence about it, never it.**

**Adopted, and this register adds the operational half it just paid for:** **a second copy is most
dangerous when it is *better* than the shipped one.** A copy that lags looks wrong on inspection. **A copy
that leads looks like progress**, and every reader who opens it comes away more confident than the tree
warrants.

## R40.6 — Why the gate is not issued in this revision, and it is not a withholding

**This register's own position stands: a published decision with a named anchor that expires beats an
unpublished one.** **That argument is about uncertainty, and this is not uncertainty.**

**R36.5 requires that a conditional approval name, for every outstanding condition, an owner and one of
three terminal states.** **The shipped-file CR25 status cannot be named yet**: whether it is a **residual**
(the record diverges, nothing behavioural, no fix) or a **fix-round item** (the shipped file is corrected)
**is a tree move that steering is putting to the owner now.** **Those two answers produce different
condition lists, and this register does not write a condition list it knows is about to be replaced by a
different one rather than by a re-anchored one.**

**Everything else is derived and ready** (R40.7). **The gate issues on his answer, either way, without
further derivation of the axes.**

# Rev 41 — **WITHDRAWAL of rev 40's contradiction, and THE RE-ISSUED RELEASE GATE.** `APPROVED FOR RELEASE, conditional on the human click-through of eight `PRE` rows`, at TREE `b56570d` / CRITERIA plan rev 49.

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: the owner ruled *"Record it, leave it"* —
**no tree move** — which makes the shipped-file CR25 status a **residual** and satisfies R36.5's
requirement that every outstanding condition carry an owner and a terminal state. **Both anchors re-derived
immediately before this commit. This is a NEW decision inheriting nothing from rev 36.**

## R41.1 — **WITHDRAWAL: rev 40's contradiction of steering's evidence was WRONG. The error was this register's**

**Rev 40 stated:** *"the probe's single occurrence is NOT in its D17 quotation"* and *"both files'
quotations end identically at 'the user chooses Classic or V2.'"*

**Measured here, at this rev's write time, with `grep -n` on both files:**

```
shipped  23: *    ... whatever the flag -> the user chooses Classic or V2"
probe    23: *    ... whatever the flag -> the user chooses Classic or V2 and is told what the other drops"
```

**The probe's single occurrence IS at line 23, inside the D17 quotation. The quotations do NOT end
identically. Steering's instrument was sound and its inference was available.**

> **The contradiction is WITHDRAWN in full. The error was this register's.** Rev 40 recorded steering's
> proof as unsound *"because a correct correction resting on a wrong instrument is how this chain gets
> bitten twice."* **The instrument was not wrong. That sentence was, and it is retracted** — a
> withdrawn-but-correct proof is the same defect wearing the other coat, and steering is right to say so.

**How the false claim was produced, because it is the third consecutive instance of one class:**
**rev 40 inferred a location from a stale read instead of measuring it.** At rev 39 the probe's quotation
*was* truncated — this register saw that with its own `sed` — and rev 40 reasoned from that memory that the
count-of-1 must sit elsewhere. **It never ran `grep -n`.**

| Rev | Defect | Committed |
|---|---|---|
| 39 | Checked the probe copy, not the shipped file | in the check |
| 40 | Asserted a location from a stale read rather than measuring it | **inside rev 39's correction** |
| 41 | — | **this withdrawal** |

**One factual note, stated because the record should be accurate about sequence and NOT offered as a
defence: the probe's quotation was truncated when rev 39 read it, and has since been corrected** — QA
acting on rev 39's finding 1, published *"in the two artifacts it owns."* **So at the moment of the
finding, both copies read identically on that line.** **That does not rescue rev 40's claim**: this
register did not know it, did not measure it, and the reason it gave was false. **The gap between "my
claim was wrong" and "my claim was wrong for a reason I could not have known" is exactly where a
withdrawal goes soft, and this one does not.**

**What is unchanged: the ownership of the original error.** Rev 39 chose the file it read. **No relay told
it to read the probe path**, and steering's own share — relaying QA's *"correct, merely thinner"* without
opening the file — reduces nothing here.

## R41.2 — The owner's ruling, and the residual it creates

**Ruling: *"Record it, leave it."*** **No tree move; the shipped file is not corrected.** Not taken:
*"Fix it now"* (an implementer round, costing another full re-pass cycle before the walkthrough, for text
that changes no behaviour) and *"Ride the next round"* — **put to him with the honest caveat that the only
likely trigger for such a round is the open finding he had already declined to fix, so it may never come.**
**Agent-authored options, steering's wording, no verbatim sentence.**

**He was told what it costs, in the option's own text:** *"The guard still works. But the file in the tree
cannot justify itself to a future reader — it cites the wrong rule for the one case doing the most work —
and the correct version lives only in a document nobody running the test will open."*

**So: CR25-1 and CR25-2 are OPEN against the shipped tree, by ruling, and their terminal state is
`RESIDUAL — owner ruled, recorded not fixed`.** **Owner: the record.**

## R41.3 — **ANCHOR.** Both axes, derived immediately before commit

> ### **CRITERIA: plan 013 rev 49 (`9637735`)**
>
> **THIS DECISION EXPIRES WHEN EITHER MOVES.** **Rev 36 expired on the criteria axis while the tree stood
> still (R38.1) — a tree-only anchor would have read as live. Neither expiry announces itself; both are
> checkable, and that is the whole of what the anchor buys** (R38.2).

### R41.5.1 — **The bad count was this register's, and the way it survived is this register's own doctrine unapplied**

**QA's disclosure:** *"My 'all eleven rows fresh' took its count from steering's phrasing rather than from
my own enumeration. I restated a count from a relay, which is the single practice this document has spent a
chain warning against."* **Steering's:** the figure originated at **rev 31 of this document** — *"eleven of
twelve executable"* — **and it kept relaying it after the event that falsified it.**

**So the count is traceable to this register, and the mechanism that kept it alive is the one R35.2 named:**

> **Rev 31's "eleven of twelve executable" was TRUE when written and became FALSE at rev 36, when BC-1's
> blocking condition was discharged by work.** **This register updated its own count and never marked the
> old one superseded — it simply stopped repeating it.** **R35.2's rule was written about a claim and
> applies identically to a number: *a correction that only quietly stops repeating the claim leaves the
> claim standing.*** **A superseded figure with nothing marking it superseded has nothing to contradict it,
> so it kept travelling — through steering, into QA, and back to this register as a measurement.**

**A count is a claim. Retiring one requires the same dated supersession as retiring a sentence, and this
register gave it none.** **Recorded as this register's, not as QA's error and not as steering's.**

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] asked
for the eight `PRE` rows in a form actionable without opening another document, **and declined to assemble
them itself on the ground that paraphrasing verdict-bearing text is where this fleet has failed all day.**
**That reasoning is correct and is why this is written here rather than there.** **No verdict moves. The
gate at rev 41 is unchanged.**

**Appendix B is `(rev 11)` and is STALE.** It predates the failure-mode reconciliation of six rows
(revs 29–31), the predicted-value corrections of three (rev 33), and the D31 exclusion clauses. **It is not
repaired and it is not lifted. §R42.1 below is the current operator text and supersedes it for this
walkthrough.** *(Appendix B is not deleted; superseded text stands where it was written.)*

**Sources, read at their own documents at this rev's write time:** every condition, predicted value and
exclusion below is taken from **QA's register** (row definitions are QA's); the paste-ready payloads are
from **Appendix C**, authored under the owner's ruling that *"if it cannot be generated for me to input
into the editor, it will not be done."* **Nothing here is paraphrased from this document's own
restatements.**

## R42.1 — **FOR `OWNER-QUEUE.md` — lift verbatim from here down to the end of this section**

---

### Chain 013 — eight things to check before the merge

**Why before the merge:** pushing to `master` deploys. There is no window between merging and shipping, so
these happen first. **If any of them fails, it goes back to the implementer rather than out.**

**How to report each one:** *pass*, or *what you actually saw*. **If you cannot reach a step, say so —
that is a result, not a gap.** Nothing here needs you to fetch anything from Discord.

---

**1. The Load confirmation tells the truth. (BC-1) — do this one first.**

*Why first: every automated test in this chain stops at the text the dialog is handed. **Nothing proves the
dialog puts it on screen.** You are the only instrument that reaches this.*

- Paste this into **New Message** and save it — that is your V2 test message, no hunting required:
  `{"components":[{"type":17,"components":[{"type":10,"content":"hi"}]}]}`
- Open it, change the JSON so the message would come out **Classic** instead, and press **Load**.
- **Read the confirmation dialog before you confirm anything.**

**Passes if the dialog names *both*:** which top-level components are being added or removed, **and**
whether the message type changes (V2 ↔ Classic). **Both must be right — either one wrong is a fail.**

**Fails if** it says the type changes when it does not, **or** says nothing about the type when it does
change — and also if it is **empty, truncated, or showing text from a previous message.** That last one
matters most: **the text can be correct and the screen still wrong**, and no test in this chain can see it.

---

**2. Pasting a V2 container gives you V2, with the flag actually set. (BC-7)**

- Paste into **New Message**: `{"components":[{"type":17,"components":[{"type":10,"content":"hi"}]}]}`

**Passes if all three:** the editor is in **V2** · the container is there · **a warning is shown**
· **and the V2 flag bit is set.**

> **The flag bit is checked by opening the Message JSON. Not by the screen looking right.** Everything can
> look correct on screen while the message is wrong on the wire — the JSON is the only place it shows.
> **"It looked fine" is not an answer for this one.**

---

**3. Pasting a flag over plain content gives you Classic, and keeps your text. (BC-8)**

- Paste into **New Message**: `{"content":"hi","flags":32768}`

**Passes if all five:** the editor is **Classic** · the text `hi` is still there · **a warning is shown**
· **no V2 error appears** · **and the V2 flag bit is cleared.**

> **Same rule as above: the bit is checked in the Message JSON, not on screen.**

---

**4. Pasting both content and components asks you which to keep. (BC-9)**

- Paste into **New Message**: `{"content":"hi","components":[{"type":17,"components":[{"type":10,"content":"hi"}]}]}`

**Passes if all of:** you are offered **two options** · **each one says what it will drop** · **nothing is
created before you choose** · **backing out leaves your message and the pasted text exactly as they were**
· **and both options work** — take one, then repeat and take the other.

> **NOT a failure:** the two options **do not appear on their own**. A notice appears first with a
> **Resolve** link, and the options are behind it. **That is the intended behaviour.**

---

**5. An unsupported component is named, not silently dropped. (BC-6)**

- Paste into **New Message**:
  `{"components":[{"type":17,"components":[{"type":10,"content":"hi"}]},{"type":11,"media":{"url":"https://picsum.photos/200"}}]}`

**Passes if:** the container survives **and the notice names the dropped type** — it must say what was
removed, not just that something was.

> **NOT a failure:** a *container that ends up empty* being removed without being named. That is by design
> and is a different case from this one.

---

**6. The paste warning stays put, and goes away when you fix the JSON. (BC-18)**

- Paste either item 3's or item 4's payload into **New Message** and leave the modal open.

**Passes if all three:** a warning **appears** · it **stays** — it does not vanish on the next keystroke ·
**and it clears once you correct the JSON.**

> **NOT a failure:** if you correct it *through* a state that has both V2 components and ordinary content,
> the warning is **replaced** by a different notice with a **Resolve** link rather than disappearing.
> **A notice that changes is not a notice that failed to clear.**

---

**7. The same paste behaves the same way in both places. (BC-10)**

- Take **any one** of the payloads above. Paste it into **New Message**. Then paste **the same one** at the
  **Load** surface on an existing message.

**Passes if:** both surfaces do the same thing — same outcome, same thing dropped, same kind of notice.

*(The automated half of this is already done. This is one spot-check by eye that the two surfaces agree.)*

---

**8. A bad import keeps the dialog open instead of navigating away. (BC-4) — BLOCKED, NOT ON YOU**

**Do not wait on this one.** It needs a fixture that fails the content floor, **and that fixture is owed by
steering, not by you.** When it arrives the check is: attempt the import, and it **passes if** the dialog
**stays open** · **the error is shown** · **and no new message is created.**

---

*End of the section for `OWNER-QUEUE.md`.*
