# Rev 43 — **The owner's report against the walkthrough. FIVE rows → `EXECUTED`. Two do not close, and the reason is the constraint this register wrote. One consolidated ask.**

**Date**: 2026-08-25. **Session**: `uat-hooks-013-5`. **Trigger**: `steering-hooks-013-3` [b04529] relayed
the owner's report verbatim and complete — **seven lines, `-as expected-` against items 1 through 7, and
nothing else in the message.** **Steering set nothing and did not pre-empt the shape of this.**
**Rev 23's ruling re-read at this document at write time, not cited from memory.**

## R43.1 — What is different from rev 23, and it is the thing six revisions bought

**Rev 23 ruled that *"-as expected-"* is compatible with two acts — a deliberate observation, which closes
a row by construction, and a general impression, which is *"worth nothing"* against a row whose failure
signature is **absence**.** **That ruling stands and is applied, not softened.**

**Two facts change what the phrase can reach here, and both are recorded before the ruling that uses
them:**

1. **He answered items 1–7 and did not answer item 8.** Item 8 is the only one marked **BLOCKED, NOT ON
   YOU** in the queue. **The set he answered is exactly the runnable set.** A general impression does not
   precisely omit the one item marked as not his. **This is evidence of item-by-item work against R42.1**,
   and it is the strongest thing in the report.
2. **R42.1 enumerated every limb of every pass condition before he looked.** At rev 23 the phrase was
   ambiguous partly because **the expectation itself was under-specified** — the row's cell named one limb
   of several. **That is no longer true of any of these seven.** *"As expected"* now has a determinate
   referent: **the enumerated pass condition he was reading.** **This is precisely what the failure-mode
   and predicted-value reconciliations of revs 29–33 were for, and it is the first moment they pay.**

**So the phrase reaches further than it did at rev 23. It does not reach everywhere.**

## R43.2 — **The line, and it is this register's own silent-limb test rather than an ad hoc split**

**The report is uniform: the same seven words, seven times.** **Uniformity is compatible with careful
per-item execution *and* with a single global impression** — and nothing in the text discriminates.

> **For an item whose every limb is visible on screen, BOTH readings close it.** If he worked item by item,
> he saw the enumerated thing. If he formed a general impression of a screen, the enumerated thing was on
> that screen. **The readings converge and the row closes.**
>
> **For an item carrying a limb that is SILENT on screen, the readings diverge** — a correct screen and a
> wrong wire are indistinguishable to any amount of looking. **That divergence is exactly what the
> recording constraint was written to catch**, and a uniform phrase cannot carry the item-specific
> assertion that the item's own text flagged as its trap.

**The two items carrying a silent limb are the two carrying the recording constraint. That is not a
coincidence and it is not a line drawn to be convenient: the constraint is the marker of the silent limb.**

## R43.3 — Ruling, item by item. **The observed result is recorded BESIDE each prediction, never over it**

| Item | Row | Prediction, as R42.1 stated it | Observed 2026-08-25 | Ruling |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **BC-1** | dialog names **both** — types added/removed **and** whether the message type changes; not empty, truncated, or showing prior text | *"-as expected-"* | **`EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25** |
| **2** | **BC-7** | V2 · container · warning · **bit set, checked in the Message JSON** | *"-as expected-"* | **`NOT EXECUTED` — OWNER REPORT ON RECORD, NOT YET DECIDABLE** |
| **3** | **BC-8** | Classic · `hi` present · warning · no V2 error · **bit cleared, checked in the Message JSON** | *"-as expected-"* | **`NOT EXECUTED` — OWNER REPORT ON RECORD, NOT YET DECIDABLE** |
| **4** | **BC-9** | two options · each names its drop · nothing created first · dismissal non-destructive · **both outcomes taken** | *"-as expected-"* | **`EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25** |
| **5** | **BC-6** | container survives · **the notice names the dropped type** | *"-as expected-"* | **`EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25** |
| **6** | **BC-18** | warning appears · persists · **clears** when the JSON is corrected | *"-as expected-"* | **`EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25** |
| **7** | **BC-10** | both surfaces give the same outcome, drop set and notice class | *"-as expected-"* | **`EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25** |
| **8** | **BC-4** | dialog stays · error shown · nothing created | **not reported — correctly** | **`NOT EXECUTED` — blocked on steering's fixture, not on him** |

**Why 4 closes where 2 and 3 do not, since its last limb also requires a second act:** **BC-9's *both
outcomes* limb is not silent.** If he took only one option he knows he took only one; nothing hides that
from him, and the pass condition he was reading says *"take one, then repeat and take the other."*
**Adopting a described act is asserting it** (R25.1). **BC-7's and BC-8's bit limbs are different in kind:
the screen looks identical whether the bit is right or wrong**, so no care on his part and no re-reading of
his phrase can settle them.

**BC-1 carries the chain's sharpest open assumption and closes it.** QA's Assumption Not Checked was that
**every instrument stops at the string the `message` field receives and nothing proves the dialog renders
it, or renders it where the eye goes.** **A human read the dialog and it was as stated. That assumption is
discharged by the only instrument that could reach it** — which is what this register has said for
nineteen revisions and what the walkthrough existed to obtain.

## R43.4 — **The one consolidated ask. One question, one word, closes both rows**

## R44.1 — The answer, weighed rather than adopted

**He chose *"Yes, opened the JSON."*** **Provenance, disclosed as always: agent-authored options, steering's
wording; the question itself is this register's verbatim; no verbatim sentence from him beyond the
selection.**

|---|---|
| **"Yes, opened the JSON"** — taken | both close, nothing further owed, no capture, no re-run |
| *"No, screen only"* — declined | both stay open; **one more look at the JSON, not a capture** |
| *"Not from memory"* — declined | **costless, and requires no claim about memory** — recorded as **unstated**, not as a negative observation |

**Two ways of leaving the rows open stood in front of him, one of them entirely costless and demanding no
assertion at all, and he took neither.** **None of the three options carried a penalty**, which makes this
framing **more neutral than BC-5's**, where no alternative was costless (R26.3). **The selection is
weighable and it closes.**

**BC-7 → `EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25. BC-8 → `EXECUTED` — 2026-08-25.** **The observed result is recorded
beside each prediction, not over it.** **The recording constraint is satisfied on its own terms: the limb
was settled by opening the Message JSON, which is what the constraint asked and what he states he did.**

**Steering states it is writing the fixture and will say so if it cannot author one honestly rather than

**What is NOT available: a quiet drop, a "satisfied incidentally", or a decision that the row no longer
matters because it is the only one left.** **A row that is the last obstacle is the row under the most
pressure to be waved through, and this paragraph exists to be quoted at whoever is tempted.**

# Rev 45 — **THE GATE EXPIRED ON THE CRITERIA AXIS AND IS RE-ISSUED AT REV 50. BC-4's FIXTURE EXISTS: THE ROW LEAVES STEERING AND BECOMES THE OWNER'S LAST CHECK.**

**Provenance of this revision, stated first because this session did not write revs 1–44.** This is
`uat-hooks-013-6`, launched `claude --agent uat -n uat-hooks-013-6` with **no brief and no prompt file** —
its entire input was `steering-hooks-013-3`'s cross-session message. It is **not** a resumption of
`uat-hooks-013-5` and inherits **no context** from it: everything below is re-derived from the documents
and the tree in this session. Per the document-lifecycle skill's "Interrupted and Resumed Runs", this row
exists so the resume-integrity question has an answer: **nothing written in revs 1–44 is carried on
memory; every claim this revision restates was re-read at this document at write time.**

## R45.1 — **THE CRITERIA RULING: the gate EXPIRED at 19:31, and this revision RE-ISSUES it. It did not "fail to bite".**

Steering disclosed that its D53 registration pass (`eb0e291`, 19:31) took plan 013 to **rev 50**, one minute
after rev 44's gate committed (`ff0c82c`, 19:30) anchored at **CRITERIA plan rev 49**. It asked which of two
things governs: this register's hard rule at rev 38, or its own carry-forward precedent at R36.1.1.

**Re-derived from the file, not from the message** — because R36.1.2 is the rev in which this register
asserted a negative about a plan delta *without running the diff*, and that defect does not get to recur
inside the ruling about it:

| Instrument | Result |
|---|---|
| `grep -n '^Rev:' planning/013-…md` | `15:Rev: 50` — **one** `Rev:` line; the plan is at rev 50 |
| `git log --oneline eb0e291..HEAD -- planning/013-…md` | **empty** — `eb0e291` is the last commit touching the plan |
| `git diff --numstat 9637735 eb0e291 -- planning/013-…md` | `3	1` — counted by an instrument that computes **before** the output filter, per RTK.md |
| `git show eb0e291` (hunk read, not summarised) | header `Rev: 49`→`50`; **one** changelog row; **one** Decisions Register row, **D53** |

**The delta is purely additive registration.** No milestone, no acceptance criterion, no Validation item,
no release condition. D53's own text says the act it authorises is already complete and that it "changes no
criterion, milestone or release condition", and the diff bears that out independently.

**The ruling — and it is not the convenient one.** Rev 41's anchor reads *"CRITERIA plan rev 49 (`9637735`)
… either moving expires this decision."* **I ran the check. It reads FALSE. The gate expired at `eb0e291`.**

Three reasons the tempting alternative is refused:

1. **Rev 38's refinement forbids reading the anchor as self-announcing.** *"A named anchor makes expiry
   CHECKABLE, not SELF-ANNOUNCING… somebody has to look."* Looking and then declining to act on what I saw
   is worse than not looking, because it converts a checkable clause into a discretionary one.
2. **R37.2.1 predicted this exact cost and rev 38 already paid it once**, recording that *"the rule stands
   unamended, and the cost is acknowledged rather than renegotiated."* **This is the second arrival of the
   same cost, and the first one where the expiring change is trivial** — which is precisely when a
   carve-out ("registration-only deltas don't count") is most attractive and most corrosive. **The rule is
   not amended.** A rule renegotiated the second time it is inconvenient was never a rule.
3. **R36.1.1 is not a competing rule; it is the RE-ISSUE PROCEDURE, and this revision has been reading it
   as one.** R36.1.1 did not hold that the anchor had not moved — it verified the delta and then carried
   the field forward *with the derivation stated*. Expiry → verify the delta → re-issue is one operation,
   and naming it correctly is the whole difference between a gate and a habit.

**So: EXPIRED, then RE-ISSUED.** Both axes re-derived at this revision's write time, from the tree:

> ### **ANCHOR — TREE `b56570dfa916368e9942e671d594112750567923` on `chain/013-rider-2`; CRITERIA plan 013 rev 50 (`eb0e291`).**
> `git status --porcelain src/ bot/` returns **empty** — no production source uncommitted, so the SHA
> stands alone and no content-identity manifest fallback is owed. `origin/master` = `2d90e8a`,
> **unmoved**; `git rev-list --count origin/master..HEAD` = **3**, branch **unpushed, nothing released.**
> **Either axis moving expires this decision, and any change to production code expires it and every
> predecessor verdict with it.**

**The interval is recorded rather than smoothed.** Between `eb0e291` (19:31) and this revision, the gate
was **expired** and `b56570d` was **ungated**. Nothing was released in that window — verified, not assumed:
`origin/master` is still `2d90e8a`. **An expiry is not a refusal**, and this one cost the chain nothing
except the requirement to say so out loud.

**Steering's disclosure is credited and its handling was right.** It brought the fact to the gate rather
than letting the gate find it, and it declined to rule on its own act. **The registration pass itself is
not a defect** — a ruling the owner gave has to land somewhere, and D53's row registers its own provenance
defect rather than smoothing it. **What this revision declines is the inference that a harmless delta is a
non-delta.**

### R45.1.1 — QA's `Criteria-At: rev 49` expires by its own clause, and that is QA's call, not mine

QA's field reads *"this verdict expires when EITHER the tree or the criteria move."* The criteria moved.
**By the same reading I have just applied to myself, QA's verdict is expired and QA re-issues it.** Routed,
not decided here.

**This gate does not depend on that re-issue**, on R36.1.1's stated basis: **no property QA verified
moved** — established above by reading the hunk, not by asserting a negative about it. The rev 49→50 delta
contains no criterion at all. **If QA rules otherwise, this gate expires with its verdict and is re-issued
again.**

Authored here because the walkthrough is this stage's and QA declined to write it (§V.31). It carries
**all five** facts QA states the text must carry, **plus** the V.32 rider (R45.5), and **breaches no
standing ruling — D36 included**: nothing in it touches whether Discord accepts an empty component, in any

> component, in any form."*** **It was not defensible when written and no defence is offered: it is a
> word-substitution slip that made the sentence assert the exact opposite of the clause following its own
> dash, and of this register's ruling three lines below (R45.4) and in its changelog row and
> `Status-Detail`, both of which read *"no breach of D36"* and are unaffected.**
>
> **What the correction does NOT change**: the ruling, or the text it certifies. **Re-verified at the file
> rather than asserted** — the liftable block between the rules contains **no mention of D36 and no mention
> of Discord accepting an empty component**, and `grep -c D36 OWNER-QUEUE.md` returns **0**, so the defect
> never reached the delivery copy. **The operator text was clean and remains byte-unchanged; the defect was

>
> **Found by `steering-hooks-013-3`, reading this document at the file, not by this register.** Recorded
> with the finder named because it is the second time today that steering's read caught a defect in this
> gate's own text before it hardened (R36.1.2 was the first), and **twice is a pattern about where the
> checking is actually happening, not a run of luck.**
>
> **Why a slip of one word is recorded at this weight.** A certification sentence that asserts its own
> falsification is not a typo in the ordinary sense: **a retrospective or `pi` auditing D36 compliance would
> find a bolded admission of breach inside the section written to demonstrate compliance**, three lines
> above the clean text — and D36 is the ruling steering breached twice today, so a false positive in the
> record costs more than an ordinary one. **A wrong entry in a compliance record is worse than a missing
> one, because it is believed.**
>
> **The method failure, stated so it is checkable rather than resolved to care:** this register read the
> **block** it was certifying and did not read the **sentence** doing the certifying. **Certification prose
> is not covered by verifying the thing certified**, and the two were three lines apart. The cheap check is
> to read a compliance clause against the ruling it names — the same operation R45.1 ran against the plan
> delta — rather than against the artifact beneath it.

**Replaces item 8 of `OWNER-QUEUE.md` in its entirety. Lift between the rules, unchanged.**

**8. A bad import keeps the dialog open instead of navigating away. (BC-4) — THIS ONE IS NOW YOURS. The fixture exists.**

*This replaces the "BLOCKED, NOT ON YOU" version. Nothing was owed by you before and nothing was missed. It is now the last of the eight.*

- Open **New Message** and click the **JSON** tab.
- Paste exactly this, replacing whatever is in the box:

  `{"content": ""}`

- **Stop there. Read the area directly below the JSON box before you press anything.**

**It passes if all three hold:**

1. **The dialog stays open.**
2. **The message below the box reads exactly:** `Message must have content, embeds, or components.`
3. **Then** press **Continue**. **Save is greyed out and does nothing**, and **you are not taken anywhere** — no new message is created.

**Three ways to get a wrong answer. If you hit one, write what is in bold instead of *pass* or *fail*:**

- **You pressed `Continue` before reading below the box.** Page 2 does not show that error. You get a greyed-out **Save** with nothing on that page saying why. **This is not a failure — go back to the first page and read there.** (The missing explanation on page 2 is a real defect; it is already written down, and it is not what this check is about.)
- **You used `{}` instead.** Don't — it looks like an equally good test and isn't. It breaks the same rule, but the check that produces the error never runs on it, so you get **no error and a greyed-out `Continue`**. That is neither pass nor fail. **If that is what you saw, write "not executed".**
- **You pasted `{"content": ""}`, the text is visibly sitting in the box, and still no error appears and `Continue` stays greyed out.** **That one is a finding, not a mistake on your part — write down exactly that.** It would mean the editor never handed your text to the app, which nothing in this chain has ever tested.

**One thing that is not the answer:** the JSON tab shows its own complaints while you are still typing — malformed JSON and schema errors, inside the accordion. Those don't settle anything. **Only the exact sentence in point 2 does.**

**Note for whoever lifts it**: the three bullets are not padding and are not a general caution. Each one
names a **specific, executed** way this row returns a wrong answer — QA §V.31.3, §V.29 and §V.32
respectively. **A trimmed version of this text is a different check.**

## R45.4 — **F-QA33: QA's recommendation ACCEPTED. Not a 013 rider; not absorbed either.**

QA raised it non-blocking with its own position — *a new plan, not a 013 rider* — and routed the judgement
here. **Accepted, as Product Owner, for four reasons, the first of which is the only one about value:**

1. **It is not what 013 promised.** The Value Statement is about the editing surfaces round-tripping a
   Components V2 message without silent loss. F-QA33 is a *page-2 error-display asymmetry* on a refusal
   path. Real, and outside the thing the owner was promised.
2. **QA's own dating settles authorship**: `git log -S` puts the un-gated `Continue` at `4fe53b6`,
   **2023-08-22** — three years before this chain. **013 created the page-1 error, not the un-gated
   control.** The asymmetry is 013's delta; the control is not.
3. **The repair QA recommends against is the one that looks cheapest**, and this register agrees with QA:
   gating `Continue` on the floor changes a three-year-old control on **every** load path to fix an
   asymmetry created on one. That is a scope change, not a fix.
4. **The cost of doing it here is not the diff.** Touching production code at this gate expires the tree
   axis, expires QA's verdict, expires this decision, and re-runs code review and QA — for a low-severity
   display defect, with one release condition outstanding. **That is a bad trade and it would be a bad
   trade even if the fix were one line.**

**Disposition: `DECLINED as a 013 rider — routed as a new-plan candidate.` Non-blocking. It does not move
the release decision.**

**And it is not free, which is why it appears twice in this revision.** QA's sharpest point is that F-QA33
**changes how BC-4 is executed** — an operator who advances to page 2 first sees a greyed `Save` with no
explanation and reports BC-4 FAILED. **That is carried into the operator text as the first hazard bullet
(R45.3), which is where declining to fix it has to be paid for.** F-QA33 is **not** discharged by being
declined; it is **open, owned by the roadmap/planner as a new plan, and named in Next Actions** so it
cannot evaporate on the strength of a decline.

**Not carried, and named so the omission is visible rather than silent: whether Discord accepts an empty
component. D36 is absolute.** The question presents itself directly adjacent to this floor — as steering
warned it would, from its own two breaches today — and it is **dropped: not tracked, not routed, not
re-raised, and not handed to 012.** This paragraph records that it arose and was dropped; it does not
carry the question.

## R45.5 — **QA's routed "Assumption Not Checked" (§V.32): OWNERSHIP ACCEPTED, and bound to BC-4's own execution**

**The assumption**: CodeMirror delivers the pasted document to `setJsonMessageString` unchanged. QA cannot
test it — `useCodeMirror` is stubbed via `next/dynamic`, so the editor's `onChange`, *the single link
between the operator's keystrokes and every behaviour QA established*, is never executed.

**Accepted, with the falsity signature carried into the operator text verbatim in substance**: pasted text
visibly in the box, **no error**, `Continue` **greyed out** — screen-identical to the `{}` trap of §V.29 and
distinguishable from it only by the fact that text *was* pasted. **This is why the operator text names the
fixture and the trap separately and tells him which screen means which**; a walkthrough that said only
"paste JSON and look for an error" could not tell these two apart, and the register would have taken a
falsified assumption as a failed row.

**Terminal states, bound to BC-4 and named in advance rather than improvised:**

| BC-4 outcome | §V.32's terminal state |
|---|---|
| **`EXECUTED`** — the exact sentence appears | **`EXECUTED — assumption HELD`.** The editor demonstrably delivered the text; the observation that closes BC-4 closes this **at no extra cost**, exactly as QA proposed |

## R45.9 — **A defect in this document, which this revision made worse rather than found**

`Status-Detail` is **69,578 characters**, measured by parsing the frontmatter, and **this revision added
~3,500 of them.** The document-lifecycle skill is explicit: *"A field long enough that it has to be read in
full has stopped answering that question."* A stage or the owner reads `Status-Detail` to decide whether to
act; **this one cannot be read to decide anything.** It has become a rev stack — a **record** kept on a
**declarative** surface, which the skill names as exactly the mixture that does not work.

**Not repaired here, and the reason is stated rather than assumed.** Collapsing it is a rule-driven trim,
and the skill binds every such trim to a per-piece accounting line naming what the removed text carried and
where that obligation now lives — **in the same commit as the deletion.** For forty-five revisions of
prose that is a substantial pass, and **the moment to run it is not while the last release condition is
open and a gate is live.** Deferring it is a choice with a cost, and the cost is that the next reader of
this field gets no help from it either.

**Routed: the retrospective**, as a process finding with a concrete remedy — a rev stack belongs in the
changelog and in the numbered rev sections, both of which already carry it here, so the collapse is
genuinely lossless and the accounting lines are cheap to write. **The observation generalises past this
document**: any stage that appends its rev summary to `Status-Detail` reaches the same state, and nothing
in the current definitions stops it at rev 3 rather than rev 45.

# Rev 46 — **THE OWNER REPORTED `BC-4 pass`. THE ROW DOES NOT CLOSE ON IT YET, AND ONE QUESTION CLOSES IT.**

## R46.1 — His report, verbatim and complete, recorded before it is weighed

> **BC-4 pass**

**That is the entirety of what he wrote about the row**, relayed by `steering-hooks-013-3` unpadded and
explicitly *not* expanded into three limbs — **steering declining to author an observation he did not make
is the correct handling and is credited as such.** His full message was **`BC-4 pass. devops now`**; the
second sentence is recorded at R46.6 and **is not evidence about the row.**

## R46.2 — The weighing, and the one thing that decides it

**What is genuinely strong here, stated first, because the case for closing is real and this register is not
going to caricature it:**

1. **`pass` is not a freehand verdict — R45.3 defines it three lines above the word**: *"It passes if all
   three hold."* He used the vocabulary this register handed him.
2. **The text was built so that every hazard has its own non-`pass` output**: `{}` → *"write 'not
   executed'"*; pasted-but-inert → *"write down exactly that"*; advanced-first → *"go back to the first page
   and read there."* **Three specific alternatives stood in front of him and he used none of them.** That is
   the R44.1 property — a report is worth more when the ways of not-passing were enumerated *before* he
   looked.
3. **BC-4 carries NO SILENT LIMB, and this register's own rev-43 test therefore points at closing it.** All
   three limbs are visible on screen, and V.32's falsity signature is **the absence of limb 2**, not a
   fourth hidden thing. **This is not BC-7/BC-8**, where the screen looked identical whether the bit was
   right or wrong.

**And what nevertheless holds it open — one thing, not a pile:**

**`pass` is a CONCLUSION against a definition, not an account of a screen; and there is exactly one screen
that produces a wrong `pass`.** It is **hazard A**: he advances to page 2 first, sees a greyed-out `Save`
with no explanation, and never reads limb 2's sentence at all. **Note what that screen contains — a greyed
`Save` and no navigation, which is R45.3's own limb 3.** A reader who skipped point 2 would be looking at
something that resembles a pass.

**The argument that decides it is not about him at all — it is about R45.4.** **This register declined
F-QA33 four hours ago on the explicit ground that its cost would be paid in the operator text's first
hazard bullet.** That decline is defensible **only if the register then checks that the bullet worked.**
**Closing BC-4 without confirming the one limb F-QA33 endangers would retroactively empty the reasoning
that declined F-QA33** — the hazard would have been absorbed in prose and never tested. **A stage does not
get to cite a mitigation as its reason for declining a fix and then decline to verify the mitigation.**

**QA pre-committed this register to the standard, in its own words at §V.29**: recording a row passed on a
screen that did not execute the check is *"a green row over a check that never executed, which is the
precise failure shape this pass was asked to avoid."*

**The asymmetry, stated plainly because it is the whole calculation:** asking costs **one exchange**, which
steering has offered and whose delay it has already taken as its own to explain. Being wrong the other way
ships M3-c's user-facing behaviour unverified and records QA §V.32 as `HELD` on an entailment nobody
observed. **Those are not comparable, and this register does not need a close reading of two words to see
which side to err on.**

### R46.2.1 — **The test that removes the pressure from the ruling, applied out loud**

**`devops now` is queued behind this row, and R44.3 predicted exactly this**: *"a row that is the last
obstacle is the row under the most pressure to be waved through, and this paragraph exists to be quoted at
whoever is tempted."* **This register is the party tempted, so it applies the test rather than asserting it
is immune:**

**Would it ask if nothing were queued behind it?** **Yes** — and the reason is checkable rather than a
feeling: **it can name the screen the answer rules out.** Hazard A. A question that cannot name what it
would exclude is rigour-theatre and should not be asked; this one can, so it is not.

**And the opposite vice is named too, because it is the one this register is more likely to commit:**
**manufacturing a second ask to look thorough burns the owner's time on a row he already answered in the
vocabulary he was given.** That is why this is **ONE question about ONE limb**, not a re-execution, not a
per-limb interrogation, and **not seven times** — the rev-43 → rev-44 move exactly.

## R46.3 — The question, and its framing tested against R25.1 before it is sent

**Asked once. It settles BC-4 and QA §V.32 together, at no extra cost, exactly as R45.5 provided.**

> **Did the sentence `Message must have content, embeds, or components.` appear below the JSON box?**
>
> - **Yes — I read that line**
> - **No — I never saw that line** *(a finding about the app, not a mistake by him, and not a failure of
>   the row)*
> - **Not from memory** *(costless; recorded as unstated, no claim about memory required)*
> - **"My `pass` stands — stop asking"** *(his ruling, and it is available; recorded with its cost)*

**Framing tested against R25.1, whose residual is that a structured prompt produces a selection and a
selection is an adoption.** **The residual is NOT avoided here and is not pretended away** — but it is at
its minimum this chain has managed, for a reason that is structural rather than rhetorical: **the question
asks for the presence of ONE EXACT STRING on a screen, which is as close to *what did you see* as a closed
question can get.** It asks him to report a **sighting**, not to ratify a verdict. **Two of the four
options require no claim at all** — *"Not from memory"* is costless and demands nothing about his memory,
and the fourth ends the matter on his authority. **`No` is explicitly de-penalised in the option text
itself**, because the answer this register most needs to be able to hear is the one that reopens work.

**Why the presence of that one string settles both rows.** Established by QA's execution at §V.29, not
inferred here: the error can only render if `loadedMessage` exists, which requires `setMessage`, which
requires the parse to have run **on text the editor actually delivered**. **So limb 2 observed ⟹ CodeMirror
delivered ⟹ QA §V.32's assumption `HELD` by construction.** One string, two rows.

independently characterised the error string: he did not state it, and under R45.3's definition his word
ENTAILS it.** **An entailment from an adopted definition is not an observation**, and the distinction is

His message ended **`devops now`**. **Steering has not invoked devops and disclosed the pressure to this

# Rev 47 — **BC-4 `EXECUTED`. QA §V.32 `EXECUTED — ASSUMPTION HELD`. ALL EIGHT `PRE` ROWS ARE TERMINAL AND THE APPROVAL IS NOW UNCONDITIONAL.**

## R47.1 — His answer, verbatim and complete

> **Yes — I read that line**

**The entirety of his response.** Selected from R46.3's four options, put to him as a blocking selection
prompt with **nothing set and nothing added** — steering confirms it carried all four unaltered, including
the costless *"Not from memory"* and the row-ending *"My `pass` stands — stop asking"*, with the
de-penalising parenthetical travelling on `No`. **He took neither exit.**

**What the answer does that his first report could not.** Rev 46 held the row because *"pass"* **entailed**
limb 2 under an adopted definition without **stating** it. This answer states it. **The register now has a
sighting of the exact string rather than a conclusion about a row** — which is the distinction R46.4 was
written to preserve, and it is now discharged on the right side.

**And it kills hazard A independently, not by inference.** Hazard A *is* the screen on which that line was
never read — QA's **D1b** renders page 2 and finds **no floor message there**. **A man who read that line
was on page 1, below the JSON box, which is exactly where R45.3 sent him.**

## R47.2 — **The §V.29 chain, RE-DERIVED at QA's captured instrument rather than asserted**

Steering offered this as a legitimate extra step and said it would carry the delay. **Taken** — and it cost
one read, because the artifact that settles it was already captured. **This is document review of an
instrument's recorded output, not code inspection; this stage read no source.**

R46.3 claimed *limb 2 observed ⟹ CodeMirror delivered ⟹ §V.32 `HELD`*, sourced to QA §V.29. **§V.29 alone
establishes only the forward direction** — no parse ⟹ no `setMessage` ⟹ `loadedMessage` undefined ⟹ the
floor line short-circuits on its `loadedMessage ?` ternary ⟹ `contentFloorError` undefined ⟹ no error.
**The chain needs the CONTRAPOSITIVE, and one link in it was cited rather than shown: that the exact string
is produced BY the floor computation and not by something else on that screen.**

**The `floor-off` mutant supplies exactly that link, and it was already in the capture.**

| Established | By |
|---|---|
| Disabling the floor (`validateContentRequirements` always `undefined`) **removes the error** | `floor-off` **KILLED — 7 failures, including D1 (error shown)**. *"The render harness is therefore reading the floor's real output, not a constant"* |
| Nothing intercepts the paste before the modal | `choice-on` **KILLED — 14 failures**: were a choice dialog to intercept, *"nothing would reach the modal and no floor error would appear"* |
| The `loadedMessage ?` ternary short-circuits when the parse has not run | §V.29 / **C2**, by executing react-use's shipped `useUpdateEffect.js` |

**So, contrapositively and by execution rather than by this register's reasoning: the string on screen ⟹
the floor computation ran and returned a message ⟹ the ternary did not short-circuit ⟹ `loadedMessage` was
defined ⟹ `setMessage` ran ⟹ the parse ran ON TEXT THE EDITOR DELIVERED.** **QA §V.32's assumption is
`HELD`, and it is held by the mutant, not by a syllogism this stage wrote.**

**The one residual in the chain, named rather than buried.** The step *"the exact string is the floor's"*
rests on **QA's finding**, not on this stage's own check: §V.31.5 records that the JSON tab renders its own
errors — malformed JSON, Zod issues — **inside the accordion**, a different string in a different place,
which is precisely why R45.3 demanded the exact sentence rather than *"an error is shown."* **That is
cited, it is QA's to own, and this register does not claim to have closed it.** **It is why the question
quoted the string in full instead of asking whether he saw an error.**

## R47.3 — **BC-4 and QA §V.32: TERMINAL**

| Item | Terminal state | Date | Result and basis |
|---|---|---|---|

**RESIDUAL 3 GROWS TO ELEVEN, and the entry states which kind this member is.** He selected an option label
rather than writing his own sentence, so it is an adoption and the class takes it. **But it is the NARROWEST
member the class has**: what was adopted is *the presence of one exact string, quoted in full in the
question itself* — a perceptual fact, not a characterisation of behaviour and not a verdict against a row.
**The register must not read as though he described the screen; he confirmed a line he was shown.** **That
is the strongest form this chain's method can produce, and naming its limit is not a complaint about it.**

## R47.5 — **THE APPROVAL IS UNCONDITIONAL. And what that does NOT mean.**

> # **APPROVED FOR RELEASE — UNCONDITIONAL.**
>
> **All eight `PRE` release conditions are terminal, every one at `EXECUTED`.** The conditional clause
> carried since rev 36 is **discharged**, not waived.
>
> **ANCHOR: TREE `b56570dfa916368e9942e671d594112750567923` on `chain/013-rider-2` · CRITERIA plan 013
> rev 50 (`eb0e291`)** — **both re-derived from the tree at this revision's write time**, neither
> inherited: `git status --porcelain src/ bot/` → **0 lines**, so the SHA stands alone and no manifest
> fallback is owed; plan header `Rev: 50`; last plan-touching commit `eb0e291`. **Either axis moving
> expires this decision, and any change to production code expires it and every predecessor verdict —
> code review's and QA's — with it.**
>
> **Predecessors, re-read at this write time, each its own authority, both now anchored to the SAME pair
> as this gate**: code review `Code Review Approved`; **QA `QA Complete`, `Verified-At: b56570d`,
> `Criteria-At: plan 013 rev 50 (§V.34)`.** Suite **48 files / 627 tests / 627 passed** at `b56570d`.
>
> **Recommended version: MINOR** — new user-facing capability across the editing surfaces, no breaking
> change to an existing surface. **Recommendation only.**
>
> **THIS IS NOT A RELEASE APPROVAL FROM THE OWNER AND DOES NOT SUBSTITUTE FOR ONE.** `devops now` is a
> direction to the pipeline. **Pushing requires his explicit approval, and nothing in this register
> supplies it.** `origin/master` `2d90e8a`, branch unpushed, **3 ahead, nothing released.**

**What unconditional does NOT mean, stated here because this is the sentence devops acts on and it is the
one most likely to be over-read:**

1. **The chain is NOT closed.** **BC-12, BC-14, BC-17 and BC-20 stand `NOT EXECUTED`.** They are
   **post-deploy-observable: they gate the chain's CLOSURE, never the release act.** **The release is
   unblocked; the ledger is not discharged.**
2. **The gate is not a standing permission.** It is anchored to two axes and **expires on either**. Devops
   re-derives both **itself**, from the tree, at its own start.
3. **F-QA33 remains OPEN** — declined as a 013 rider, routed as a new-plan candidate. **Releasing does not
   close it**, and its decline is now paid for: the hazard bullet it justified **worked**, which R47.1
   records as the check that F-QA33's decline was owed.

## R47.7 — **A conflict in this stage's own definition, surfaced rather than quietly resolved either way**

This stage's definition says: *"When UAT passes, update the plan's Status field to 'UAT Approved' and add
changelog entry."* The plan currently reads `Status: Active` at rev 50.

**Enacted literally, that instruction expires this gate at the moment it issues it.** The plan file **is**
this gate's criteria axis; writing to it changes the file the anchor names, and a gate that edits its own
criteria source in the act of issuing is unsound in a way no amount of care fixes. **This is a real
bootstrap conflict in the definition, not a pretext for skipping the step.**

**Neither evasion is taken.** **Not skipped** — the Status update is owed and is routed below. **Not
self-expired** — this register does not write to the document its own decision is anchored to.

**Mechanism, so it is a routing and not a shrug**: **the planner enacts it as a rev 51 registration pass**,
and this gate re-anchors to rev 51 by the expire-and-re-issue procedure R45.1 established — **which is now
routine and cheap: this register and QA have each run it once today, and QA's run had controls.** **If the
owner's release lands first, devops closes these documents on commit and the point is moot; the routing
stands either way so it cannot evaporate on timing.**

**Routed to `pi`/the retrospective as a process finding**, stated so it is true whether or not today's
instance exists: **a stage instruction that writes to the document a stage's own verdict is anchored to
will expire that verdict every time it is obeyed.** Today's instance is the evidence beneath it.
